# vigia — Specification

Status: **v0, 2026-07-30.** Source of truth. Code is written against this document; see `CLAUDE.md` § Method for the drift rule.

---

## 1. Problem

Running a full git TUI in a pane beside an AI coding agent, to watch changes as they land, is the wrong tool. A multi-panel git client spends the pane on branches, commits, stashes and status. In a pane already halved against the agent, every panel is a few lines tall and names truncate.

What is wanted: **the live diff, fullscreen, auto-updating, scrollable, mouse-driven, and beautiful.** Nothing else.

## 2. Product class — the load-bearing decision

`vigia` is a **monitor**, not a reviewer. That single distinction generates every budget below, and the budgets are the product.

| | Reviewer | **Monitor (`vigia`)** |
|---|---|---|
| How it starts | Launched per review | Already open |
| Interaction | The point | Rare or never |
| Correct when untouched | No | **Required** |
| Runtime | Minutes | **Days** |
| Latency budget | Seconds | **A frame** |

`btop` is the reference for what monitor-class feels like: you read state from shape and colour, glance away, glance back, and never configure anything.

Design rationale beyond this section is recorded outside the codebase; call `recall` when a decision needs it.

## 3. Invariants

Numbered because tests reference them. Each must have a test that fails on violation. **An invariant without a failing test is a wish.**

Budgets are **absolute** and chosen to be defensible on their own terms, not relative to any other tool.

| # | Invariant | Budget | How it is proven |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I1** | Redraw is **event-driven**, never a timer that runs unbidden. No filesystem event and no git index change means no work. **A clock is permitted only while the thing that armed it is still on screen and still changing**, and the three conditions are the whole of the licence: it may not start on its own, it may not outlive that thing, and the idle path must be untimed *by construction* rather than by care. Hold-to-repeat on the scrollbar's step buttons (§11.1) is the first clock to meet them and is an **instance of this rule rather than an exception to it**: anything else bounded the same way is already licensed and needs no further ruling. **A sample inside the history window is such a thing**, ruled 2026-08-22 by [#243](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/243): the window ages, it empties `HISTORY_WINDOW` after the last write, and the clock stops with it. | **0 wakeups** with an empty window, which is the state a monitor left open overnight is in. While the window holds a sample: **at most one wake per `HISTORY_SAMPLE`**, and at most `HISTORY_SAMPLES` of them after any burst, because the window drains | CPU sampled over a 60s idle window **entered with the window already empty**; assert no render calls. Two structural halves rather than one: `nothing_held_means_no_timer_at_all` asserts the loop's wait is untimed with nothing held, and `an_empty_window_and_nothing_held_means_no_timer_at_all` asserts the same of the ageing clock, so both budgets are structural rather than merely observed |
| **I2a** | **Re-diffing is incremental** — the frame path never re-diffs a file that did not change. | re-diff cost ∝ what changed, **not** worktree size | Assert the re-diff count and byte count for a single-line edit, across **two** fixtures differing only in changed-file count. One fixture cannot prove it: see §7 |
| **I2b** | **Re-highlighting is incremental** — only changed hunks are re-parsed. | re-parse ∝ edit size, **not** file size | Assert the re-parse count and byte count for a single-line edit, across **two** fixtures differing only in file size. One fixture cannot prove it, for the reason I2a's row gives: see §7 |
| **I3** | **Flat resources over days.** No unbounded growth in RSS, file handles, or temp files. | **RSS drift < 5%** over 24h; **zero** temp files retained | Soak: synthetic edits driven through the whole pipeline, RSS sampled at a fixed count across the window. Scheduled rather than per-commit, and the scheduled window is shorter than 24h for a reason that is not ours: see [`RULINGS.md`](RULINGS.md) and §7 |
| **I4** | **Streams, never buffers.** A frame **builds** only what it draws: diffs, highlighting and reads of file *content* follow the window, never the worktree. The one exception is the diff's **height**, which is counted for every changed file once per tick, without materialising any of it. | **first paint < 100ms** on a 100k-line diff; **counting the height < 16ms** on the same fixture | `criterion`, gated in CI; the counting bound in `crates/vigia/tests/reads.rs`, and `what_a_row_exact_scrollbar_would_cost` records what it was admitted on |
| **I5** | **Correct with zero interaction.** Auto-follows the newest change and scrolls to it, untouched. | — | Scripted edit sequence, snapshot the frame, no input given |
| **I6** | **Legible at 40 columns.** No horizontal overflow, no truncated-to-useless labels. | — | Snapshots at 40 / 80 / 120 columns, plus structural gates in `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs` sweeping every width from 1 to 120: no row over-occupies, no hint is cut in half, and every label that lost characters says so |
| **I7** | Startup to first paint is imperceptible. **The first frame draws the diff without colour**, because a grammar's patterns compile on first use at 74-362ms per grammar, which is **124 to 695ms for a cold screenful** at the scale a frame pays it (Windows figures taken under `codegen-units = 1`, which §6 now records as overstating what ships by roughly 6x on that target and by nothing at all on Linux), and the frame after it is where that belongs; the reader gets content rather than a blank alternate screen. **And no frame parses under a grammar nothing has compiled**, whenever it arrives: a file whose language this process has not met draws plain, the warmer compiles it off the frame, and the wake that ends the warm is the redraw that colours it. Without that second half the same cost lands mid-session on a frame nobody asked for ([#129](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/129)), which is where §7's cold-path exclusion was doing the work an invariant should. | **< 50ms**, and **< 16ms** for the frame that meets a grammar | `crates/vigia/tests/first_paint.rs`, over the **shell's** first paint rather than the core's, asserting the body is full and that nothing was parsed before it asserts the clock. `criterion` does **not** track this shape: `benches/engine.rs`'s `open_and_first_paint` builds no `Highlighter`, which is the core-only measurement §10 corrects. The second half is `crates/vigia-core/tests/warm.rs`, in two tiers: a wall clock against 16ms, watched failing at 93.73ms, and the structural pair that says *why* — a cold grammar parses **zero** lines and draws plain, and the same hunk colours once a warm has landed |
| **I8** | Terminal restored on **every exit the process can observe**: the quit key (Ctrl-C included), an error return, a panic under `panic = "abort"`, and an externally delivered termination — `SIGINT`, `SIGTERM` or `SIGHUP` on Unix, a `CTRL_C`, `CTRL_BREAK` or `CTRL_CLOSE` console event on Windows. **`SIGKILL` and `TerminateProcess` (`taskkill /F`) are outside it on both**, because neither runs any code the process owns, and they are the whole of what *no design* can reach. Four further cases are excluded by **choice** rather than by the platform, and the difference matters to anyone reading this row as a promise: `SIGQUIT`, whose default dump is the thing whoever sent it asked for; `CTRL_LOGOFF_EVENT` and `CTRL_SHUTDOWN_EVENT`, which reach a service rather than a console application; and **the second ask**, which takes the default disposition and restores nothing, because a process that has been told once and is still here has to remain killable. The **first** ask leaves the way the quit key does, exit **0** and no message. §11.1 carries both halves. | — | Takeover order and its exact inverse; the partial-failure unwinding; a panic-hook test; escape sequences against DEC's own numbers; and a two-process gate that signals a child and reads back what its guard wrote |
| **I9** | Steady-state frame time holds 60fps under continuous edits. | **< 16ms** p99 | Gated over the **frame path**, not the primitives: a settled frame, one line rewritten before each frame, every file materialised. The caller-side gate **paints as well as collects**, for the reason §7 gives. `criterion` tracks the same shape |
| **I10** | **Glanceability history is bounded.** Churn history is bounded by a fixed window and a fixed cap on tracked paths, independent of how many files the session changed. A path that ages out of the window is dropped entirely. | **≤ 256 paths**, **≤ 120s**, whatever the session changed | Structural, in `crates/vigia-core/tests/history.rs`: a fixture recording **10,000** distinct paths asserts the store sits *at* the cap and that eviction actually ran, and a window of silence empties it. The soak asserts the same over the real process, and **refuses to assert** in a window that reached neither rule. Not in `tests/budgets.rs`: every gate there is a ratio or a duration, and this is a count |

A regression past any budget **fails the build.**

> [!NOTE]
> **The measurement histories behind these rows live in [`RULINGS.md`](RULINGS.md)**
>
> Six rulings carry archaeology a reader applying the table does not need, and
> each keeps its active constraint above and its evidence trail there:
>
> - **I1** pays a cost its own row does not mention and its own measure cannot
>   see, found 2026-08-15 while ruling
>   [#123](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/123). `crossterm`'s mouse
>   bundle sets `?1003h`, any-event tracking, so pointer motion over the pane
>   wakes the loop — and `run` draws once per drained batch whether or not any
>   wake in it acted, so the cost is a whole frame rather than a wake. *"No
>   filesystem event and no git index change means no work"* is the row's letter
>   and this is work, so **it is a gap rather than a mis-measurement**; the
>   measure is a 60s *idle* window and a moving pointer is not idle, so nothing
>   has ever looked. Unmeasured, unreclaimable portably, and tracked as
>   [#154](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/154).
> - **I4** was **narrowed 2026-08-01**: a height is hunk boundaries and line
>   counts, not content — totalling through full diffs was 442.71ms where
>   counting is 8.76ms, so the height exception in the row exists and #49's
>   refusal of a repository-wide total stands for sums over content. And the
>   height walk became **incremental 2026-08-04** (#101): a stat replaces a
>   read, 16.98ms p50 → 9.40ms over a hundred undrawn files, incremental
>   outside the settle margin and deliberately not inside it. Three rejected
>   alternatives (parallelise, stream, approximate) are recorded there with the
>   numbers they were written against, and what deliberately remains unfixed is
>   [#84](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/84).
> - **I2** is two numbers because re-diffing (`gix`, Phase 1) and
>   re-highlighting (`syntect`, Phase 2) have different dependencies and phases;
>   the 18.58ms-vs-3.27ms measurement that forced the split is there.
> - **I8** stopped saying `SIGINT` because raw mode makes Ctrl-C a key event,
>   never a signal, and the externally delivered signal it was narrowed to
>   exclude is **covered since 2026-08-08**
>   ([#24](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/24)). The ruling worth
>   keeping is the one about *shape*: it was taken symmetrically, on both tier-1
>   platforms in one issue, because the single-task version was Unix-only and
>   [#16](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/16) had already rejected a
>   guarantee that means different things on different targets. What made that
>   affordable was measurement rather than principle — neither half adds a crate
>   to any graph — and the row's exclusions are now the same sentence on both
>   platforms rather than two different promises.
> - **I3**'s scheduled window is shorter than 24h because a GitHub-hosted job is
>   terminated at six hours; the full window runs by `workflow_dispatch` or
>   locally, the sample count is fixed so the statistic is computed identically,
>   and a window short enough to be all warmup measures only warmup — §7
>   carries that as a rule.

## 4. Scope

**In:** working-tree diff (unstaged by default), event-driven refresh, follow mode, scroll (keyboard + mouse wheel), syntax highlighting, per-file churn visualisation, a pinned file list above the diff (§11.1), responsive layout, theming.

**Out of v1, deliberately:** staging, committing, rebasing, branch or commit browsing, annotations, comment threading, AI features, remote operations. Each is reviewer-class and each would cost an invariant.

**Deferred, not rejected:** multi-worktree view (several agent sessions at once — the strongest differentiator after glanceability, and the most btop-shaped); Jujutsu and Sapling support.

## 5. The differentiator: glanceability

`btop`'s real achievement is that state is readable from **shape and colour** without reading text. `vigia`'s translation:

- per-file **churn sparkline** — change density over time
- a **heat strip** locating change within the file
- live **+/− counters**
- a **visual pulse** on what just changed

This is what makes it a monitor rather than a narrow diff view, and it is where design effort goes once the invariants hold.

### 5.1 What the README mockup commits to

`assets/preview.svg` is the most detailed design artifact in the project, it is **public**, and until now it was specified by a three-sentence caption. A picture in a README is a promise; these are the promises in it, and what each one costs.

Elements the four bullets above do **not** cover are marked **(unspecified)** — they are in the picture, so they are committed, and they had nowhere to be described.

| Element in the mockup | What it needs |
|---|---|
| Header: `vigia · 3 changed` … `watching` | A **mode word**, so there is a set of modes. The changed-**line** total is the §10 header question, and the file count drawn here is not it: a per-file count is free where a repository-wide one is not. **Ruled 2026-07-31: there is no line total** ([#49](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/49)), so this row is now fully specified rather than partly and the header's facts are exactly three. A repository-wide `+`/`-` is a reviewer's summary of a changeset rather than a monitor's account of what is happening, it puts first paint back in proportion to the size of the diff against I4, and the only way round that is the wake I1 forbids. §10 carries the argument. **Ruled 2026-07-31 and implemented: the set is two**, `watching` and `not watching`. This cell used to say that `watching` "implies at least a settling state and an idle one", and it implies neither: both are durations, and a duration cannot be drawn honestly by a shell that only wakes when a file changes. That is the same wall the pulse hit one row below, and the two words left are the only two states the shell can actually tell apart. The header also draws the **worktree** name on the left where the picture draws `vigia`, which is the first of two deliberate departures from the mockup, the second being §5.1's element split. See §11.1 for both. **Reopened 2026-08-01 on the phrasing rather than the facts** ([#67](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/67)): the three facts are right and `watching · 3 files` reads as *"watching 3 files"*, a verb with an object, naming a curated set that does not exist and that B6's no-flags ruling puts out of scope. The ladder is the proof they were meant to be independent — the count drops first and the word survives alone — and at the full rung they fuse anyway. The count is also not about the watched thing: this watches the whole worktree, and `3` is what changed inside it. **Ruled 2026-08-02 and implemented: the count moves to the left, with the worktree, and reads `3 changed`; the mode word takes the right alone.** The fix is *adjacency* rather than vocabulary, which is what makes it a layout ruling and not a rewording: §11.1 already lays the footer out by subject, the header has the same three subjects available, and it was seating a tree-fact next to a self-fact. Separated, the count has nothing beside it that a participle could govern. **`changed` rather than `files` is the load-bearing half**, because `vigia · 3 files` on the left would be a *worse* claim than the one being replaced: this repository has more than three files in it, so the count has to name the change rather than the things. Two cheaper alternatives were rejected and are recorded so the ruling is a choice: `watching · 3 files changed` stops parsing as a sentence, since a clause cannot be a participle's object, and costs eight columns on a row this section forbids from taking a second line; `live · 3 files` stops parsing for the same reason and spends the `watching` / `not watching` negation pair ruled one sentence above, which exists so that a reader who has learned one word has learned both. See §11.1 for the ladder, which keeps its order and changes only which side each rung is dropped from |
| Per-file **sparkline** | A **retained time series per file** — samples of churn over a window, bucketed. This is the only unbounded state in the design and it is the one I3 forbids growing: the **window** and the **path cap** are part of the invariant, not a rendering detail. The sample *rate* is not, and the paragraph below is where that was settled rather than assumed. **Ruled 2026-07-31 and implemented: a 120-second window in 8 buckets of 15 seconds, capped at 256 paths, evicted by window and by least-recently-changed.** That is I10, which now has a row above rather than a warning below. **The store stopped sampling at that resolution on 2026-08-16** ([#198](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/198)): it keeps 120 one-second samples per path and sums fifteen of them into each drawn bucket, so a drawn column covered the same fifteen seconds it always did and the strip was byte for byte what it was. **A drawn bucket is ten seconds since 2026-08-18** ([#161](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/161)), because the window divides into **twelve** rather than eight: fifteen seconds was coarse enough that a steady worktree drew only five and a half of the ramp's nine rungs, which is a block rather than a shape. Measured over forty seeded series, twelve takes a steady worktree from 8% of buckets empty to 17% and from 5.5 distinct heights to 6.5, which is more texture and not more noise. **Still not an I10 change**, for the reason this cell already gives: that row bounds paths and window, and how finely the window is divided sits underneath both. The store's sample rate is untouched at 120, and twelve divides it exactly, which the same `const` block asserts for both drawn counts. The eight was a **sampling rate chosen from one element's column count**, which was fine while that element was the only reader and stopped being fine when a worktree-wide graph wanted the same store across a whole pane. **It is not an I10 change**: that row bounds paths and window, and how finely the window is divided sits underneath both. It makes eviction *tighter* rather than looser, which is worth stating because the opposite would matter: on the fifteen-second grid a write was dropped somewhere between 105 and 120 seconds of age, and on the one-second grid between 119 and 120, so the 120-second bound still holds and holds more nearly. **The sentence introducing this cell needed amending rather than reinterpreting**, which is `CLAUDE.md` rule 1 taken rather than dodged: it said the window *and the sample rate* are part of the invariant, hanging off the clause naming **I3**, and that is now false of the sample rate. I3's budget is *RSS drift < 5% over 24h*; 4KiB to 60KiB at the cap is a one-time fixed allocation of about 0.2% of the tens of megabytes the soak measures, and drift is what that invariant bounds. Measured rather than assumed: `History::record` at the 256-path cap goes from 4.2µs to 17.5µs p50 and 7.5µs to 47.4µs p99, which is 0.3% of I9's 16ms. One sample per path per coalesced tick, and heights are scaled against the **one figure across every bucket on screen** rather than per row, because the question a reader asks down a file list is which file is busiest. **Reopened 2026-08-03 on what an *empty* bucket draws** ([#78](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/78)): the store is fed from the watch, so a file already modified when `vigia` started has no tick behind it, and a reader who opens the pane beside an agent that has written twenty files saw twenty rows and no sparklines. That is the *ordinary* first frame rather than an edge, because the pane is opened precisely when work is in progress, and it left the element §5 names as one of four differentiators absent exactly where it is worth most. **Ruled 2026-08-03 and implemented: a bucket with no writes draws a track, so a file with no history is the all-empty case and draws a full one.** One rule rather than a special case for the cold row, and it is the rule the heat strip's cell below in this table and `Theme::bar_track` already state: this table read the picture that way for the heat strip and never for the sparkline, and `Theme::bar_track` reached the same rule a third time for the scrollbar, which is not a glance element and so is not in this table at all. **It costs nothing, and that is what changed rather than the argument**: [#77](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/77) reserves the slot at every width whether or not a row can fill it, so the column is spent either way and the only question left is whether it says anything. Nothing is invented by saying it: the track's claim is "no churn in the window", which is true, where backfilling from git would put a fabricated number in the one element whose job is to be trusted at a glance. **The track is `_`, deliberately not the ramp's `▁`.** The heat strip may draw a live slice and its track from the same glyph because colour is its only channel; a sparkline's channel is **height**, so spending the ramp's floor on "no data" would make one write and no writes the same shape and leave colour alone carrying the distinction the lowest block exists to protect. `·` was rejected for sitting mid-cell, which would draw an empty bucket *above* a bucket holding one write. **And the picture was corrected on one row**, which the issue asked for conditionally and which turned out to be unconditional: the dimmed `Cargo.toml` drew eight buckets, seven of them at the ramp's floor height in the ramp's own floor colour `#1f6f3f` and one taller, and dim is the recency ladder's `cold` rung, whose meaning is *nothing is tracked for this path* rather than *this path is old*. A cold row carrying a bucket is therefore a state the tool cannot produce **at all**, not merely at launch, so that row's eight bars are now uniform track. **The picture had no sparkline track in it to read**, which is why `Theme::dark`'s `spark_track` is the one value in that palette chosen rather than read off the file: before this correction every quiet bucket in the picture was drawn in a *bucket* colour. The correction then put one there, and it is drawn in the chosen `#30363d` rather than in the `#21262d` the heat strip on that same row uses, so the artifact and the shell agree. **The two tracks differ on purpose and that is the ruling this cell adds to the one above**: a sparkline track is one stroke where a heat slice is a solid block, and the same value behind a twelfth of the ink reads as absence, which is the state this element exists to stop looking like. Rows 1 and 2 are left alone deliberately and every bucket in them is a written one, so nothing there is the shape this rules against. **And the last thing the picture asked for here is drawn, 2026-08-16** ([#196](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/196)): the mockup ramps its sparkline across five greens, tallest brightest, and the shell drew one flat colour. `Theme::spark`'s docblock recorded that gap for a phase and stopped there, on the ground that the picture *"does not answer this"* because its hue is wrong. It is right about the hue and was never the question: green already means addition two rows down, so the ramp is cyan. Three stops rather than five, through the same `Band` and `Theme::band` the heat strip below uses, so one row does not carry two gradients that mean the same thing in two vocabularies. **The 256-colour cube chose the values rather than taste**: its axes are `0, 95, 135, 175, 215, 255`, so a first attempt put two `dark` stops in one cell, and on `light` the same sparseness below 95 means the flat value it had becomes the *middle* stop and the quiet end lightens. That asymmetry between the two palettes is forced and is written into both. `ansi` spends two stops for the reason the heat strip does. See §11.1 **A sample weighs the bytes a write moved and a height is measured against `scale_of` rather than against the busiest bucket, both since 2026-08-18** ([#232](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/232)): a sample counted files written before that, so a worktree where one file is saved repeatedly put a one in every sample, made itself the peak, and drew every active column at full height. §11.1 carries the rule and why the two glance elements share it while dividing by different quantities. **And the bucket count is a width rung since 2026-08-20** ([#234](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/234)), which is what closed the ceiling that reopening left. The window divides into **twenty-four** source buckets of five seconds, and the shell sums adjacent ones onto the rung a pane affords: twenty-four at the widest, twelve from fifty-seven columns up, six from forty-five. So a drawn bucket is still ten seconds on every pane the picture is measured at, and the count of them is what a wider pane buys. **What that measurement found is that a finer *period* buys nothing and a finer *column count* buys the shape**: twelve buckets of five seconds draws fewer distinct heights than twelve of ten (5.25 against 5.75 on a steady worktree, 6.20 against 6.35 on a bursty one), while twenty-four of five draws 6.72 and 7.40. [#223](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/223)'s *"distinct heights peak near four to five seconds"* was taken with the column count moving with the period, so it was a claim about columns wearing a period's clothes. **Twenty-four rather than any other divisor** because the rungs have to halve, or a strip shedding one bucket at a time leaves widths where the picture is neither the window nor an obvious fraction of it, and twelve has to stay on the ladder because twelve is what the panes below the new rung draw. **The dense glyph rungs gain almost nothing from it**, 2.83 distinct heights against 2.80, which is [#244](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/244)'s finding rather than this row's and is stated rather than left to be discovered. **Still not an I10 change**, for the reason this cell already gives twice. |
| Per-file **heat strip** | Hunk line-ranges projected onto a fixed number of buckets across the file's length, so it needs the file's **total line count**, not only its diff. Colour rule when one bucket holds both additions and deletions. **Ruled 2026-07-31 and implemented.** Bucket count is **12**, from the picture, which draws exactly twelve; the picture also draws an empty bucket as a **dark track** rather than as a gap, so a drawn strip is always its full width and a reader can see how much of the file is untouched. The one exception is a file with no line diff *at all*, which draws no strip rather than a strip of pure track: §11.1 carries that ruling and why it is the opposite of the sparkline's. A mixed bucket is **yellow**: every alternative paints it as pure, and separating addition from removal by position is the strip's whole job. **Intensity is three steps, matching the picture** ([#11](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/11)). This cell said two for a phase, because sixteen foreground-only colours hold a normal and a bright of each hue and no third stop, so the ramp was as wide as the palette could draw rather than as wide as the picture asked for. A wider palette closed it. At sixteen colours it is still two, and `ansi` spells that out in its own fields rather than leaving the depth ladder to collapse them by accident. The line count it needs is **free**: see §5.2. **The twelve are drawn as twelve, 2026-08-16** ([#196](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/196)). The picture draws them as separate rects, width `11` on a pitch of `14` with `rx="2"`, and the shell drew `█` twelve times, which on a cell grid is one continuous bar. The projection was computing twelve slices and a reader could not count them: only a change of band marked a boundary, so two adjacent slices in one band read as a single wide one, and *where in this file the work is* is exactly the question that needs counting. A small square puts the gap inside the cell, so the strip keeps its twelve columns and no rung of the width ladder moves. It also ends a collision the suite had been working around: `tests/legibility.rs` matches a sparkline bucket by colour **and** glyph because the strip drew the same block a full bucket does, and the two elements now share no glyph at all **Twelve is a rung rather than the count, since 2026-08-18** ([#161](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/161)): the source resolution is twenty-four and twelve is what a pane at the picture's own width draws. §11.1 carries the ladder and the share that decides which rung a pane affords. |
| Per-file `+42 −7` | Covered. Per-file counters are free — a file must be diffed to be drawn (§10). |
| A **dimmed row** (`Cargo.toml` in the mockup renders fainter than the rows above it) | A **recency gradient**: rows fade as their last change ages. **Ruled 2026-07-31 and implemented as three rungs of one ladder** — pulse, live, cold — read from I10's store; see §11.1. Sixteen foreground-only colours have three intensities to spend, so the gradient is a ramp of three rather than a fade. **Corrected 2026-07-31: the fourth rung is not [#11](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/11)'s to bring**, and this cell used to say it was. The rung count belongs to `Recency` in `vigia-core`, which has exactly three variants and whose `cold` means *untracked* rather than *old*, so a wider palette draws the same three rungs in better colours and cannot draw a fourth. A real fade needs the store to emit an age fraction, which is a core change with an I10 budget on it. See §11.1 |
| `●` on the diff header | The pulse, but drawn as a **persisting mark**, not a flash. So it has a **decay**. **Ruled 2026-07-31 and implemented: it persists for exactly one tick and cuts rather than fades.** It is the top rung of the recency ladder above, not a second mechanism, and the rung is deliberately *not* a duration: see §11.1 for why a wall-clock decay cannot be drawn without the timer I1 forbids. It marks **every** path in the newest tick, which is what §11.2 B2 said the pulse was for. **Ruled 2026-08-03 and implemented: the dot carries it alone and the `just changed` label is gone**, from the picture as well as the shell, which is the first change made *to* `assets/preview.svg` rather than to the code beneath it. The label was the row's second statement of one fact. The dot is already unique on the screen, it is already the pulse colour, and the row it sits on is already the brightest rung of the gradient one row above, so a reader who has seen the mark once has nothing left to learn from the words beside it: the label read as a caption on a signal that had already been read. **A label is not free on a monitor**, which is what makes this a ruling rather than taste. Text has to be *read*, and §5 puts the whole differentiator in what can be taken from shape and colour without reading; fourteen columns of it sat in the widest rung of the row ladder, so at every width below sixty-five the picture and the shell disagreed about what the pulse even was. The one thing the words did carry that the dot does not is the *name* of the state for a first-time reader, and that is what the README is for. See §11.1 for the rung the ladder loses with it |
| Diff body: **syntax highlighted content**, five classes deep (`kw`, `fnn`, `typ`, `var`, `con`) over a default foreground | I2b, and a **class set** rather than a palette: what the picture commits to is which distinctions are worth a colour, not which colour each one gets. Ruled 2026-07-30 and implemented; the engine emits meanings and the shell colours them, which is what leaves the palette to #11. See §11.1 |
| A **tinted row** with a coloured left bar on every added and removed line | A per-row **background**. It is what separates a changed line from a context line in the picture, because the text itself is highlighted identically on both, and it is doing the same work the dimmed row above does: it is how the eye finds what moved without reading. Not drawable at sixteen foreground-only colours, where an ANSI background is a solid block rather than a tint. **Ruled 2026-07-31 and implemented** ([#11](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/11)): the wash is painted across the whole row, gutter and trailing blanks included, so it reads as a band rather than as a highlight behind some text. **The left bar is the pane's leading cell, background only, and it costs no column.** Ruled 2026-08-17 ([#218](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/218)) after the element had shipped as the tint alone for three phases: `added_bar` and `removed_bar` existed as keys and `Painter::line_row` consumed them, and every built-in left them empty. It is one cell of the blank margin [#119](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/119)'s ladder keeps and the wash already bleeds under, so it is a **width rung**: drawn from forty-three columns up, absent below, where the wash and the sigil carry the signal alone. Where the margin is two columns the bar takes the leading one and the rest stays margin, which is the picture's own proportion. **And it reaches `ansi` and every depth, where the wash reaches neither**, because what rules the wash out below 24-bit is stated about *text* (*a quantised background is a solid block, and a block behind highlighted code destroys the colours on it*) and this cell has no code on it: a wash must be a **tint**, which cannot be chosen without knowing the background, where a bar is a **saturated block on a blank column**, which needs no such knowledge. **It was refused before, and the refusal is kept rather than deleted**: it read *the bar has no terminal equivalent that does not spend a whole column, and I6 forbids one on decoration*, and the sigil cell carried it by inverting. True while the pane drew from column zero, false once #119 gave it a margin, and nobody re-read it. The sigil keeps its own colour now, which is what every diff tool does and what the mockup draws: its bar is a sliver *beside* a green `+`, not a recolouring of it. The picture draws that bar three pixels wide in a nine-pixel cell, so it is sub-cell and has no terminal equivalent that does not spend a whole column, and I6 forbids spending one on decoration; the sigil is the one cell on the row that already means *this line changed*, so it carries the bar rather than a column being found for it. Both are absent on a palette that declines them and at a depth that cannot express them, which is where §11.1's recorded loss still stands |
| Status bar `0.8ms frame` | Instrumenting the render path and drawing the result. Self-referential: measuring and painting the number costs frame time that I9 gates. **Ruled 2026-07-31 and implemented: the p99 of the last 128 completed frames, nearest-rank, sampled every frame** ([#41](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/41)). The **statistic is p99 because I9 is**, and a readout reporting a median would be silent about exactly the frames the budget exists to bound. **128 because §7 already says why not 30**: at 30 samples a nearest-rank p99 is just the maximum, and at 128 it is rank 127, so one cold outlier is excluded and two are not. That is the behaviour a monitor wants, since the 60.97ms first-touch parse two rows down is a real frame that should not sit on the readout for two minutes. **A frame is the whole turn of the loop** — the wake, the drain, every `Frame::advance` in the batch, `View::collect`, and the paint — which is §7's rule that a gate is written against the caller's whole frame, applied to a readout instead. The number drawn is necessarily the **previous** frames', because a frame cannot include its own paint in what that paint draws; that is honest rather than stale, since it describes completed frames and stays true while nothing happens |
| Status bar `11MB` | A live RSS readout. I3 samples RSS in a **soak test**, never on screen; reading it per frame is a syscall on some platforms. **Ruled 2026-07-31 and implemented: it is read once per painted frame, on all three tier-1 targets, and drawn in `MiB`** ([#41](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/41)). Three things had to be settled and only one of them was cost. **The read is a syscall everywhere it ships**, which is not what the soak's own helper suggested: `soak.rs` spawns `ps` on macOS and `tasklist` on Windows, and the latter is **42.8ms median** on the reference machine against the 16ms I9 budget, 2.7x it for the read alone. What makes it affordable on screen is that both platforms have an in-process answer through a crate `gix` **already** puts in the graph, so neither costs a new crate: `libc::proc_pidinfo` on macOS and `windows-sys`' `GetProcessMemoryInfo` on Windows, against `/proc/self/status` on Linux, which needs nothing. The soak keeps its subprocess readers where the shipped one has none and is sampled 288 times across a window rather than per frame, which is what that 42.8ms is the reason for. **`MiB` rather than the mockup's `MB`**, because I3's soak is the only other place this quantity is quoted and it is in MiB throughout; two units for one number is two dialects, and a reader comparing the screen against a soak report would read the 4.9% difference as drift. `assets/preview.svg` was corrected to match. **And the number is "as of the last change", not "now"** — the trade that had to be accepted rather than solved: this shell wakes only on a filesystem event, so a pane left open on an idle tree shows the RSS from whenever the last write landed. Refreshing it needs a wake I1 forbids inventing, and the pulse's escape from the same wall does not transfer, because an event names paths and never bytes. It is consistent rather than broken: the diff on screen is *also* as of the last change, and this readout now carries the same contract as everything beside it |
| Status bar `follow ▶` | A follow-state indicator, which presumes the mode exists. **Landed with I5**, on the footer rather than a third chrome line: see §11.1. I6 later gives it a line of its own, above the hints, at the widths where one line cannot hold both. |
| Key hints `q quit · f follow · ↑↓ scroll` | A hint bar, and it **constrains I6**: roughly thirty columns of it must degrade legibly at forty. **Ruled 2026-07-30: it does not degrade by shortening.** The footer takes a second line instead, and only below the width where a whole line holds the bar does it drop hints, `JK files` first and then `jk scroll`. See §11.1 for why the first of those is a bonus rung that never decides the footer's height. See §11.1. |

Three of these are corrections rather than gaps:

1. **`f` toggles follow.** The mockup shows a dedicated key and a state indicator. That is the answer to §11.2 B1, and it was published before the question was asked. Ruled and implemented 2026-07-30; `f` is in `input.rs` and the rule is §11.1.
2. **The dimmed row and the pulse are one mechanism**, not two: both are recency rendered as intensity. Specifying them separately would produce two decay clocks that disagree on screen. This said "the `just changed` label" until 2026-08-03, and the label going does not weaken it: the correction was always about the two being one ladder rather than about which glyphs the top rung spends.
3. **The table above is a list of elements and was silent about the container**, which is what let B4 sit `(proposed)` for two phases while the picture and the code drew different screens. Ruled 2026-08-01 ([#66](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/66)): the file list is a **region**, pinned above the diff, and §11.1 carries it. Every row in this table is unchanged by that; what changed is where three of them are drawn and for how long they stay there.

**The left rail is not a departure at all, and since §11.2 B14 it is not even a width away from the picture.** [#252](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/252) put the pinned list beside the diff from 134 columns, and `assets/preview.svg` is a **109-column** render: it was measured below the arrival width, so what it draws is the stacked layout and the stacked layout is what still shipped there. [#295](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/295) made the rail a gesture, so the stacked layout is now what ships at **every** width until a reader asks otherwise, and the picture and the default describe the same pane everywhere rather than only below 134. The picture is not behind the code and the code has not left the picture; the two describe the same pane. What the rail does owe §5.1 is the glance complement, and it draws exactly the pictured one at every width a reader runs, which §11.1 records and `tests/rail.rs::the_rail_grows_with_the_pane_and_keeps_the_pictured_complement` asserts against a rung read off that 109-column render rather than restated.

**A fifth departure, and it was in the diff body where the first four were in the chrome and the glance row: the mockup stands its sigil off the line and the shell drew it flush.** Found 2026-08-15 by a reader watching the pane, and settled by the picture's own coordinates rather than by argument: the sigil sits at `x=72`, one cell of `~8.1px` ends it at `80.1`, and every content origin is `x=88`. Ruled towards the picture and implemented the same day ([#164](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/164)); §11.1 carries the rule, why it does not ladder, and what the column costs at forty.

**The picture's split of elements across the two regions was the second departure, and it is closed 2026-08-17 by correcting the picture rather than the code.** It gave the diff's heading no kind letter, no counters, no heat strip and no sparkline, so each region drew a different subset. That is coherent only while the pinned region shows every changed file, and an automatic unbounded changed set means it cannot: a file scrolled out of a capped region would take its counters with it and leave nowhere to read them. Both regions draw the same row through the same `Painter::file_row`, which also leaves one degradation ladder to gate rather than two. The shell was right, the reason is still true when checked rather than inherited, and the mockup had simply never been brought forward, so the mockup moved: its heading now carries the same elements in the same slots as the summary row for the same file. §5.3's law is what licenses that direction, since the picture answers *open* questions and does not outrank a decision made against an invariant.

**One sentence of that departure was also wrong about the picture, which is worth keeping rather than absorbing.** It read *"it gives the summary rows no kind letter"*, and the mockup has drawn `M` at `x=32` on every summary row since it was first committed. Nothing downstream could tell, because a claim about a picture is checked by opening the picture and the sentence read like a description of one. This is the second time §5.1 has carried a citation of the wrong region ([#149](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/149)'s is the first), and both were found by auditing the artifact rather than by any gate, which is what [#156](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/156) is open about.

**Two further corrections to the mockup in the same pass, and neither was a departure: the picture was simply behind.** Its status bar drew `q quit · f follow · jk scroll · JK files`, which is the hint bar [#80](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/80) replaced with `q quit · f follow · ? keys` when the gestures sheet landed, so the picture was advertising two keys the shell had stopped naming and omitting the one that reaches all of them. And its state cell drew the follow marker without the `N/M` position beside it, which `state_rungs` has drawn since the pinned region existed. Recorded because the two look identical from inside the file and are not: a departure is a decision somebody made, and this was a picture nobody had opened since the ruling that changed what it should say. §5.3's *"corrected in the same pass as any ruling that changes what it draws"* is the rule that was missed, and missing it is silent, which is why the audit that found these was of the artifact rather than of any gate.

**The third departure was the mockup's right-hand order, and it closed 2026-08-17 by redrawing the picture.** It placed sparkline, then counters, then heat strip, left to right, with the pulse away at the right-hand edge. `Painter::file_row` allocates right to left by priority (counters first because they are the row's content, then the pulse, then the heat strip, then the sparkline), so the drawn order is **pulse, heat strip, sparkline, counters**. The renderer's order is the one that survives a narrow pane, because allocating by priority is what lets the ladder drop the least valuable element first; the picture's was the one that read well at a width nothing degrades at, and it was left standing for three phases on the argument that a mockup's job is to promise a layout rather than to place it. **Reported from the picture and ruled the other way**: a reader comparing the two could not line up a single element, which is what a specification is for. The mockup now carries the renderer's order at the renderer's columns, measured off a 109-column render, which is that canvas exactly at one cell of 8.1px. **The colour half had already closed on 2026-08-15** ([#157](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/157)), which is why only the order was left; **and the counters became two right-anchored columns on 2026-08-02 without the order moving** ([#77](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/77)), that issue being about *alignment* where this was about *order*, which is the distinction that kept the two from being confused. So §5.1's departures are down to one: the header's worktree name. **What this cost is worth stating, because it is the argument that lost**: a picture that places every element where the binary places it is harder to redraw when the layout moves, and §5.3's rule that the mockup is corrected in the same pass as any ruling that changes what it draws is now doing more work. That is the trade, and it was taken on the ground that a specification nobody can check against the thing it specifies is not one.

**And the picture said something about those colours nobody had read off it**, which is the half of #157 that was not compliance. The mockup draws `+42` in `.grn` and `-7` in `.red` on its first two rows and `-0` in **`.faint`** on its third, so the rule it states is *value-dependent*: a half takes its diff colour where it has something to say, and the row's dim grey where it does not. §5.3 derives the same answer without the picture — green and red are lent "only where they restate the same fact", and a `-0` restates none — and the reason it matters is the reading the element exists for. On a worktree an agent is only adding to, an unconditional red would put red on every row, and *is anything being removed* would have no answer left on screen. What is deliberately **not** settled is which grey: the picture's `.faint` is `#6e7681` where `Theme::chrome_dim` is `#8b949e` on `dark`, and that is a question inside one role rather than about it. It is left open here beside the same question for the gutter, which draws `.dim` where the picture draws `.faint`. The two halves are still drawn or dropped **together**, because `+42` with no `-7` beside it reads as a total rather than as half a pair; it is only their ink that is per half.

**And the picture promised one more thing than this table listed: the row is columnar.** Every file row in `assets/preview.svg` puts the same element at the same `x`, so three sparklines read as one small-multiples chart and three heat strips as a comparison, which is the whole of what a file *list* is for. `Painter::file_row` right-packed instead, taking each element out of a budget that had already lost the elements outside it, so every element's position was a function of the widths of the ones to its right and no two rows agreed. Ruled 2026-08-02 towards the picture; §11.1 carries the rule and what it costs.

**A picture in a public README is a specification whether or not it is written down.** This one implied a retained time series, a recency gradient, two status readouts and a keybinding, none of which appeared in the spec, the roadmap, or any issue — while [#10](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/10) carried four of them in a single line. That is the same failure as §11: behaviour that exists somewhere real, with no line claiming it.

### 5.2 Where the mockup pulls against the invariants

§5 says design effort goes here "once the invariants hold", and [#10](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/10) repeats it: *"depends on the invariants holding first."* **That framing is wrong, and correcting it is the most consequential thing in this section.** At least two elements need retained state the frame path does not produce, in `vigia-core` rather than in the shell. They do not sit on top of the invariants. They **move** them.

**The sparkline needs precisely what eviction throws away.** `FrameStats.evicted` exists so the cached-diff map stays "bounded by the current diff rather than by everything ever edited" — that is how I3 is argued today. A churn sparkline is change density *over time*, so it has to survive a file settling: one that empties the moment a file stops changing shows nothing worth glancing at, and *"what was hot thirty seconds ago"* is the entire question it answers. Glanceability history therefore cannot live in the evicting map — and must not be unbounded either.

> [!NOTE]
> **I10 earned its row on 2026-07-31**
>
> It was written here as a **proposal**, deliberately kept out of the §3 table,
> because that table is for invariants with a failing test and this had none. It
> now has a budget (256 paths, 120 seconds), a fixture that drives ten thousand
> paths through it, and a soak assertion over the real process, so it has moved
> up. [#38](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/38).
>
> Two things it cost that this section did not predict. The store had to be fed
> from the **watch** rather than from the frame path, so `Tick` now carries every
> file a burst wrote instead of only the last, capped at the same 256 so a bulk
> operation cannot make a tick expensive before the store gets a chance to bound
> it. And the decay the mockup asks for turned out to be an I1 question rather
> than a rendering one: see §11.1.
>
> What it got right is the sentence everything else followed from. **The data
> structure was the decision and the drawing was the easy part.**

**The heat strip needs a whole-file property.** Locating change within a file requires that file's **total line count**, and the frame path is built to avoid exactly that: pure revalidation reads **0 bytes** (§10), the number I2a is written against. Measured naively — every changed file, every frame — it reintroduces the read I2a removed. It is cacheable per `(path, blob id)`, since a file's length cannot change without its content changing, so it is payable once per version rather than once per frame. **That caching is not an optimisation; without it the heat strip breaks I2a.**

> [!NOTE]
> **The cache above was never needed, and the reason is worth more than the prediction**
>
> **Corrected 2026-07-31 by building it.** [#39](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/39).
> The paragraph above is right that a whole-file read would break I2a and wrong
> that a new cache is what avoids it. `hunk::compute` interns **both sides** to
> diff them at all, so the working-tree side's line count is already computed on
> every diff and was being thrown away. It is now a field of `FileDiff`, which
> means it is cached and invalidated with the diff itself.
>
> That is **stricter** than the `(path, blob id)` key proposed here, not merely
> cheaper: a blob id names the index side, and a working-tree edit does not touch
> it, so a cache keyed that way would have served a stale length for exactly the
> file a reader is watching being written. `tests/frame.rs` gates the difference
> with an edit that keeps a file's byte length and changes its line count.
>
> The general shape, and it is the third time this project has hit it: **the
> expensive-looking property was a by-product of work already being done.** I5
> found the follow target already resolved by the gitignore filter; I10 found the
> burst's paths already resolved by the same filter; this found the line count
> already interned by the differ. Look for the by-product before designing the
> cache.

**Two status readouts measure the thing they run inside.** `0.8ms frame` means instrumenting the render path and drawing the result — a readout whose own cost falls inside the budget it reports, gated by I9 at 16ms p99. `11MB` is a live RSS number, and I3 samples RSS in a **soak test** precisely because reading it is a syscall on some platforms rather than free per frame. Both are honest to show; neither is free to show; the spec said nothing about either.

**Consequence for sequencing:** Phase 3 is not a rendering phase. Its two headline elements each need a core-side change with an invariant attached, so they belong in the same conversation as I2a and I3 rather than strictly after them.

**Confirmed by building the first one.** [#38](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/38) landed the sparkline, the recency gradient and the pulse, and the diff was mostly `vigia-core`: a new bounded store, a change to what a `Tick` carries, a new invariant with its own budget, and a soak assertion. The drawing was a bucket ladder and a three-step ramp. This section predicted that split and it was right, which is worth recording because the alternative reading — that Phase 3 is where the shell gets prettier — was the one both §5 and [#10](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/10) originally invited.

### 5.3 The design language

The principles the individual rulings keep deriving, stated once so a new question starts here instead of re-arguing an old answer. Each names where it already bit; the laws themselves live where they always did (§5.1 for the picture, §11.1 for behaviour) and are not restated.

**The picture binds the elements; the snapshots are what ships; the distance between them is the work.** One canonical mockup, corrected in the same pass as any ruling that changes what it draws — never redrawn ahead of a ruling, because a picture answering an open question *is* the answer and must not arrive by ambush (B4), and never left behind one, because that is drift (§5.1's departure table is the complete list of licensed disagreements). But the mockup is rendered in pixels, and a cell grid can only approximate it: what it specifies is the **element set and each element's promises** (§5.1's table), never glyph-for-glyph fidelity. The first live wide-pane run made the difference unmistakable — every promised element present, and the pane still reading far from the picture. When that happens, the gap is not a defect in either artifact; it is the design backlog, itemised: spacing, colour weight, composition, density. The snapshot suite carries the rendered truth at every width and state, which one picture cannot.

**Furniture runs full-bleed; text is inset.** The mockup draws washes and rules to the pane edge and glyphs one to three cells in, and the two roles must not swap: a wash that stops short reads as a misaligned highlight, text at column 0 reads as squeezed. **Ruled and implemented 2026-08-10** ([#119](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/119)); §11.1 carries the ladder, the floor that keeps it clear of I6's forty columns, and the reason a region pays it on one side. What the build added to the principle is that a margin has to be laddered as a **total** rather than per side, because stepping both sides on one column hands a widening pane a narrower row, which is the layout table's own rule reaching a place nobody had looked for it. Chrome is the third role and never grows for a transient thing (§11.1's notice rule).

**Furniture spans the region that drew it, not the screen.** The law above says a wash runs full-bleed; this says *full-bleed to what*. A row's wash, a rule and a scrollbar's track belong to the region they are drawn in and stop at its edge, and until 2026-08-23 the two readings were the same sentence because every region spanned the pane. [#252](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/252) put the list beside the diff, and the reading that had never had to be chosen became the only one that draws: a wash reaching the pane's edge from a region two thirds of the way across it would paint over the rail. `render` kept the seam open for three phases on purpose rather than collapsing two numbers that happened to be equal, and this is where the ruling now lives rather than in a comment beside the call site.

**Snap, never ease.** A monitor's premium feel is the state change that lands whole in one frame. Animation needs a clock, a clock needs the timer I1 forbids, and the refusal is not a sacrifice: easing tells a reader the tool is doing something, and this tool's whole claim is that it already did it. The same reasoning retired the pulse decay (§11.1) and the frozen-clock header words (§5.1) — anything drawn as *elapsing* lies the moment nothing wakes.

**Acknowledgment before action, priced honestly.** Every act the shell accepts answers on the same frame — a click lands, a drag tracks, a digit teleports. Extending that to *intent* (hover) was ruled on rather than accreted ([#123](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/123), §11.2 B10), and **this paragraph has now been wrong about it twice, which is kept rather than quietly edited out because the two errors are the same error.** It first said hover *"costs a wake class I1 currently never pays"*. I1 pays it: `crossterm`'s `EnableMouseCapture` sets `?1003h`, any-event tracking, so pointer motion already arrives and `run` draws once per drained batch whether or not any wake in it produced an action ([#154](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/154), `RULINGS.md`'s I1 section), and none of it can be given back portably. It then said hover was declined because **nothing would ever tell it to turn off**, naming as the mechanism that *"the takeover does not enable focus reporting"*. That was a line this repository had not written, not a fact about the world: `?1004h` is one step `TAKEOVER` can take, and inside the pane `?1003h` already reports leaving a row as arriving at the next. **B10 was reversed 2026-08-16 and hover is adopted**; §11.1 carries the behaviour, the clearing ladder, and what the takeover asks for.

**The tense of that sentence was wrong twice in two days, which is the maintenance cost of writing a claim that expires.** It first read *"`TAKEOVER` does not request focus reporting today"*, true when written and false by the time [#186](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/186) merged; the correction then said *"requests it today"*, which is the same shape pointed the other way and was caught by review rather than by anyone re-reading the paragraph that exists to record this failure. **The lesson is not "check the tense" but "prefer the claim that has none":** *"`?1004h` is one step `TAKEOVER` can take"* was true before the build and is true after it. A sentence about what the code does **now** belongs in §11.1, which is the section named for it and which a build already has to update, so this paragraph now states the price and points there for the state.

**The principle the reversal produced, stated here because this is where a new question starts**: a mark about an **input device** is licensed inside a body region on a surface the reader's pointer can act on, and it must be the quietest thing still visible in that region. The quietness is not decoration, it is what pays for a mark that can go stale: a glance has to reach the worktree first and the pointer never. §11.2 B10 carries the derivation and the confinement rule it amends.

**The pattern still generalises, and it is what survives both corrections**: a feel improvement that costs an invariant a sentence gets a decision issue; one that costs nothing (inset, half-page keys, file-granular jumps) gets a row. What B10 adds is larger than *the sentence has to be true when it is written*, which was the lesson drawn the first time and was not enough on its own, because the replacement sentence was written a paragraph later and checked no harder. **A conclusion that outlives the reason it was given is the thing to be suspicious of.** When a stated basis collapses, the question reopens; it does not acquire a new basis for the same answer. Two rows of Phase 8 reached that shape on one day, and this is the one where it happened twice inside a single ruling.

**Colour is spent by role, and the diff's is inviolable.** Green and red mean added and removed, may never collapse at any rung that has colour (the depth ladder's one absolute), and are loaned out only where they restate the same fact (the footer's follow marker, the counters). **A loan is per statement rather than per element**, which is what "the same fact" means when read strictly and which nothing had had to apply until [#157](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/157): a `-0` on a file row is the counters' element and is not the fact, so it keeps the dim grey while the `+2` beside it takes green. Cyan marks structure and instrument. Intensity is the recency ladder and nothing else — dim is a *meaning*, not a style. **Amended 2026-08-16 for the one case that is not durable** ([#189](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/189)): a mark that follows the **pointer** may take a weight of its own, because it is not a claim about the worktree and it is gone the moment the pointer moves. The ladder keeps its three weights and the exception is bounded by two things rather than by taste. **The first half of that boundary was stated wrongly and is corrected 2026-08-16** ([#193](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/193)): it said the mark sits **above** all three, and the paragraph two above it said in the same breath that a pointer mark *"must be the quietest thing still visible in that region"*. Both were written the same day and they cannot both hold. Quietness wins, because it is the half with a reason behind it: a hover can go stale and a recency cannot, so a mark that outranks every claim about the worktree is read first and is the one most likely to be wrong. **A pointer mark takes the pointer's own colour**, which is `Theme::bar_hover`'s, so one reading means *the pointer is here* on a step button, a thumb and a listed path alike. What keeps it off the ladder is the surviving half: it **underlines**, and nothing else in these palettes underlines anything. That was always the load-bearing channel and the brightness was never doing the work: on `ansi`, the default palette, a hovered path's foreground now equals `Theme::path_cold`'s outright, because sixteen names hold nothing between colour 8 and `Gray`, and the modifier is the whole separation there by design. `crates/vigia/tests/render.rs::a_hovered_row_reads_as_the_pointer_and_never_as_recency` and `::the_pointer_reads_the_same_colour_wherever_it_rests` are what fail if either stops being true. **The rule this replaced would have refused the feature**, and that is the second time in two days a rule here has been read as a wall when it was a description: the reader asked for hovered rows to be legible, and *there is no rung* was a reason to build one rather than to decline. A new element gets a colour only by taking a role, never by being distinct.

**And a second bounded exception, which is the durable one**: the row the caret marks draws its **path bold**, on top of whatever recency gave it (#193). This is not a third meaning for intensity, it is the caret's second channel: `▸` and the weight are one statement said twice, resolved from one predicate in `Painter::list`, so they name the same row or neither is drawn. The tie is `affords_caret`, which is what makes it safe: below seventeen columns the caret is dropped and the weight goes with it, and a bold row with nothing pointing at it would be a weight a reader cannot attribute. It shares `BOLD` with the pulse rung and that is not a collision, because both readings carry a glyph of their own — a pulsing row draws `●` and this one draws `▸` — and glyphs are also what survives `NO_COLOR`, where §11.1 rules modifiers matter most. **Why the region needed it at all**: every other durable distinction there is carried by weight, and the one row a reader most needs to find carried a glyph alone. The two exceptions are orthogonal rather than ranked, so a hovered current row reads as both: colour and underline are the pointer, weight is the file the diff is inside, and ranking them would take one of those facts off the screen.

**Shape carries signal; words are read.** The four glance elements are shape-and-colour precisely so the eye takes them without reading (§5), which is why the pulse lost its label and why a hint is dropped whole rather than abbreviated — half a word is a *reading* task on a surface built to avoid one. Text on screen is either content, a name, or advice, and each has its own degradation law (§11.1).

**Richness is the reward of space.** The invariants price wakes, writes and clocks — they price **ink at zero**: borders, dense graphs and colour ramps are all drawing on frames that already happen, so a monitor can be the best-looking thing in the terminal while staying the cheapest. The ladder therefore runs in both directions. Below I6's floor the pane is spartan because every cell is contested; upward, each rung *earns* more of the cockpit — the inset, then the colour the mockup already promises, then borders that speak ([#124](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/124)), higher-resolution detail, the worktree churn graph, a list deeper than six on a pane tall enough to hold it ([#125](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/125)). What stays refused is only what the invariants actually name: easing (a clock), decoration that must be configured (B6), and any element whose information is not already retained. An earlier draft of this paragraph refused borders and meters on taste; that was the author's dial, not the spec's, and the correction is recorded so the next reader knows which kind of rule this is.

## 6. Architecture

Cargo workspace, two crates:

- **`vigia-core`** — library. Git (`gix`), diff modelling, incremental highlighting (`syntect`), filesystem events (`notify`), the watch and coalesce engine, the frame path. **No terminal I/O, no ratatui.** Every invariant except I6 and I8 is testable here, headlessly.
- **`vigia`** — the `ratatui` + `crossterm` shell: input, layout, theming. Thin by design, so the TUI stays swappable and the engine stays provable. A library with a five-line binary on top rather than a binary alone, because §7 makes the snapshot suite the proof for I5 and I6 and a test cannot import a `main.rs`.

The split is a dependency decision, not a hedge: the TUI renders whatever the core produces, so the core has to work first or the TUI is being built on sand.

`notify` is named here because I1 requires filesystem events rather than a timer, and each platform delivers them differently: `inotify` on Linux, `FSEvents` on macOS, `ReadDirectoryChangesW` on Windows. `notify` is the standard Rust abstraction over those three, and it keeps the binary pure Rust. Verified 2026-07-30 against `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`, `x86_64-unknown-linux-musl` and `aarch64-apple-darwin`: no `cc`, `cmake` or `bindgen` in any of the three graphs. The `-sys` crates it pulls (`inotify-sys`, `fsevent-sys`, `windows-sys`) are FFI declarations against facilities the OS already ships, so they compile no C.

**`libc` on macOS and `windows-sys` on Windows** are named here for §5.1's memory readout, and they are named rather than merely used because CLAUDE.md's rule is that a dependency reaches the spec before it reaches a manifest. Both are **target-conditional dependencies of `vigia` alone**, both are `unsafe` FFI declarations against facilities the OS already ships, and **neither adds a crate to any graph**: `gix` already puts `libc` into the Apple graph through `gix-hash` to `sha1-checked` to `cpufeatures`, and `windows-sys` 0.61 into the Windows graph through `gix-sec`. What each one buys is one syscall in place of a **subprocess**. `soak.rs` reached the same numbers by spawning `ps` and `tasklist`, which is affordable 288 times across an hour and not sixty times a second: `tasklist` alone is 42.8ms median on the reference machine against a 16ms frame. Linux needs neither, since `/proc/self/status` is a file read.

**And both are `dev-dependencies` of both crates as well, for the budget gates' CPU clock** ([#212](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/212)). §7's absolute tier attributes a wall-clock overshoot to the host or to us by reading thread CPU time beside it, `std` exposes no CPU clock on any platform, and this time Linux needs one too: `clock_gettime(CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID)` on every unix, `GetThreadTimes` on Windows. Named here because the rule is that a dependency reaches the spec before it reaches a manifest, and **the distinction that keeps it cheap is `dev`**: a dev-dependency is absent from the published crate's graph and from the shipped binary, and the purity gate walks `-e normal,build`, so §6's no-C-toolchain rule is not even in play. It would hold anyway, since both crates are pure Rust bindings.

> [!WARNING]
> **Check the lock file, do not recall what a dependency costs**
>
> The paragraph above was nearly two platforms instead of three. `libc` was
> confirmed already-present with `cargo tree` and `windows-sys` was assessed from
> memory as *"a new crate in the graph"*, in the same hour, by reasoning that
> should have applied to both — and the Windows readout was filed as deferred work
> ([#56](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/56)) on the strength of it. The
> sentence directly above this one had said otherwise since Phase 1. **A
> dependency's cost is a fact about the lock file and is one command away, so an
> assessment of it that was not run is a guess** wearing a measurement's clothes.

**`signal-hook` on Unix and `windows-sys`'s `Win32_System_Console` on Windows** are named here for I8's externally delivered signal, and they are one decision rather than two: `std` has no signal API, the mechanism differs by platform, and the alternative to naming both is the platform-asymmetric guarantee [#16](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/16) already rejected as worse than one stated uniformly. Both are **target-conditional dependencies of `vigia` alone**, and — the paragraph above being what this one was checked against rather than recalled from — **neither adds a crate to any graph**: `cargo tree -i signal-hook` reports it already present at 0.3.18 on Linux and on Apple, pulled by `crossterm` through `ratatui`, and `Win32_System_Console` is a feature of a crate `vigia` already declares. Measured 2026-08-08, unique crates before and after: 183 Linux, 181 Apple, 185 Windows, unchanged on all three.

**It stays pure Rust, and `signal-hook` is the one dependency here where that had to be read rather than assumed.** It ships a `build.rs`, which is the shape §6 exists to catch: the file is `fn main() {}` for every feature but `extended-siginfo-raw`, `cc` is an *optional* build-dependency behind that one feature, and nothing enables it. The `no C toolchain` CI job is what keeps that true rather than this sentence.

**`regex-fancy` is what keeps `syntect` pure Rust, and its price is a compile deferred to first use.** `syntect`'s default engine is `regex-onig`, which is oniguruma, which is C: taking the defaults would put `cc` in the graph on its own and cost musl-static and Windows tier-1. That choice stands. What was never priced is *when* the pure-Rust engine does its work. `syntect` holds each pattern in a `once_cell::sync::OnceCell` and hands it to `fancy_regex` on first use, so the **first parse under a grammar** costs **74-362ms** where loading all seventy-five costs 318µs: Rust 93.11ms, Python 89.05ms, JavaScript 103.27ms, Go 74.14ms, Markdown 361.74ms, measured in release on the reference machine.

**And it is per pattern rather than per grammar**, which is the part that decides what can be done about it. Warming on a small sample leaves a *different* file of the same language still paying: measured over a 2.5KB synthetic sibling, `.js` is 80.49ms above floor, `.html` 40.10ms and `.cpp` 37.55ms. So **"this grammar is warm" is not a property that exists** and anything built on one would be acting on a false claim. That much has held since [#51](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/51) and is unchanged.

**What #51 also concluded, and what 2026-08-21 corrects, is the frame cost.** Its residual table was measured over **whole-file** parses, which carry a large parse beside the compile they were meant to isolate, and the frame path parses one *screenful*. Measured again at that scale, twenty-four lines, a fresh `SyntaxSet` per case:

| | cold | after the warmer read one real 64KB sibling | floor |
|---|---|---|---|
| `.rs` | 123.98ms | **2.40ms** | 2.40ms |
| `.md` | 694.75ms | 89.77ms | 90.65ms |
| `.toml` | 15.26ms | 0.43ms | 0.40ms |

The cliff is **flat in content size**, which is what says it is a compile rather than a parse: a 594-byte Markdown screenful costs **631.46ms cold and 0.97ms warm**, a 650x penalty on half a kilobyte, while a 16.8KB one costs 439.26ms and 117.00ms.

> **Every number in this section was measured on Windows under `codegen-units = 1`, and both halves of that sentence now matter.** The warm figures moved too, and by more than the cold ones: the 117.00ms warm screenful above is the defect [#261](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/261) closed, and the same worst screenful now parses in **8.81ms on Linux**. [#261](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/261) found that at `codegen-units = 1` the MSVC target compiles `fancy-regex` roughly 6x slower than at any other setting, so the compile costs above are inflated by a factor this repository chose and did not know it was paying: the fixture behind I7's second frame, a cold parse under one grammar in `crates/vigia-core/tests/warm.rs`, costs 107.88ms at 1 and **17.59ms at 2** (a different measurement from the screenful figures above, and quoted because it is the one taken at both settings). The profile now sets 2 (see `Cargo.toml`, which carries the measurement), so **these figures overstate what ships**. They are left rather than rewritten because re-measuring them honestly needs the platform they were taken on, and a number retyped across platforms is worse than a number with its provenance attached.
>
> That penalty is also **Windows-specific**, which nothing here previously said. Re-measured on Linux (rustc 1.98.0), `codegen-units` 1, 2 and 16 sit within 3% of each other on every fixture, so the cliff is not a property of the engine or of this profile but of one target's codegen. macOS has never been measured. A reader taking a number from this section should ask which of the four targets in §9 it came from before acting on it.

**So the exact claim is the negative one, and that is what the frame path acts on ([#129](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/129)).** A grammar nothing has ever built a `ParseState` for has every pattern uncompiled, which is knowable rather than estimated: `syntect` holds each pattern in its own `OnceCell` and exposes no way to fill one but a parse, so the two places in `vigia-core` that parse are the whole population. **A frame therefore never parses under a grammar the warmer has not run over. It draws that file plain, says which grammar it wanted, and the warm that follows wakes the loop to colour it.** That is I7's rule one grammar over rather than a new one, and it is why the cost §7 rules onto the cold path no longer lands on a frame at all.

Reaching every pattern of a grammar directly was checked and is **not available**: `SyntaxReference::contexts` is private, `ContextId`'s fields are private, and `Regex::try_compile` compiles a throwaway rather than filling the set's own cell. Parsing a real file is the only way in, which is why the warmer reads `WARM_BYTES` of one rather than a fixture.

**Warming past what the reader is about to meet is refused on memory.** Compiling ten grammars moves RSS from **6.73 MiB to 64.73 MiB** on the reference machine, Rust alone +12.43 MiB and Markdown +19 to +35 MiB. I3's budget is drift rather than a plateau, so that is a bad trade rather than a breach: a monitor left open for days would be carrying the compiled form of languages the session never opens. The exception is the **three** grammars the repository itself leads with, counted from the index, because the agent in the other pane is near-certain to write the language the repository is mostly made of and those megabytes are spent within seconds either way. **Counted per grammar rather than per extension**, and the difference is not cosmetic: the index can only be tallied by extension, since §6 puts `syntect` on the other side of `worktree.rs`, and a language spelled two ways (`.yml` and `.yaml`, `.h` and `.hpp`, `.md` and `.markdown`) is then counted twice at exactly the point where the counts decide who wins. So the tally comes back whole and the merge happens where the grammar is knowable.

**Which languages the dump covers is a decision, and until 2026-08-19 it was an inheritance.** `Highlighter::new` loaded `syntect`'s bundled dump: 75 syntaxes, a Sublime Text 3 era snapshot chosen by nobody. Probed against what a 2026 reader opens, 36 formats drew plain — TypeScript first among them, the most-used language on GitHub and the language this tool's own stated workload has converged on, and `Cargo.toml` drew plain in a tool written in Rust. **Ruled 2026-08-18 ([#235](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/235)): `vigia` covers every modern language, with no tiering.** The bar for a format is that a grammar exists that this stack can carry, and the recorded gaps below are the only exceptions.
