  **That thirty seconds is a property of the fixture and not of the code, which the day-long window is what proved.** Over 100 x 500 the climb takes about **eight hours**, so the 10% prefix sized against the paragraph above discarded 2.4 hours of it and the gate fired at **13.26%** on a process flat to 0.02 MiB/h past its plateau. [#126](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/126) ruled that this is an instrument artifact the report should **state** rather than a baseline the gate should move: the measured plateau and its own drift are printed beside the verdict, the fraction stays the gate, and the 5% budget is untouched. §7 carries the rule and the three detector designs that were tried and rejected.

  **Answered, and this is the day-long number in one place.** Reference machine, release, 100 x 500, **86,400 seconds** from 2026-08-05 22:22: 288 samples, **1,850,935 frames**, 1.15 TiB read, 36.3M lines highlighted, peak 36.32 MiB, 4 failed frames of 1.85M. Quarters **30.95 → 34.73 → 35.02 → 35.06 MiB**, one step between the first pair and **+0.33 MiB across the entire back half**, decelerating to ~0.02 MiB/h against the **+81.3 MiB/h** a deliberate 1 KiB-per-frame leak calibrates at. The gate reads **13.26%** from the 10% fraction, whose baseline sits on the ramp; from the measured plateau at sample **97 of 288**, 8.08 hours in, the same statistic reads **1.85% against the 5% budget**. **I3's day-long claim is met**, and the 288-sample series is a fixture in `crates/vigia/tests/soak.rs` so that is checkable without running another day. Closed by [#47](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/47).

  **First scheduled run, 900 seconds, 40 x 500, all three tier-1 targets, green:** Linux 17,804 frames at 2.56% drift, macOS 6,053 at 2.49%, Windows 20,427 at 0.00%. Every one hit the viewport bound at equality, and Linux reports **9 descriptors at the first sample and 9 at the most**, which is the first time that metric has been observed at all. The reference machine's own two runs were 0.17% and 2.18% over the same statistic. So the observed spread of a **healthy** process is roughly **0 to 2.6%** against a 5% budget, which is real headroom and not a lot of it: a longer window could cross the line on variation rather than on a leak, and the answer if it does is a measured warmup or a measured budget, never a wider one.

  Two narrower gaps travel with this one. File handles are only countable from `/proc`, so the descriptor gate has never run on the machine these numbers come from and is exercised for the first time by CI on Linux. And the measured window deliberately contains no index writes: the loop reaches mass eviction by reverting files instead, which is what keeps `git` out of the private temp directory the retained-file gate asserts on, so staging over a long run is soaked by nothing.
- [x] ~~Every number here comes from a fixture, and §2 buys the product class on a **workload** that has never been run.~~ **A working session has now been recorded, 2026-08-07 by [#72](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/72), and the instrument is worth more than the window.** `crates/vigia/tests/observe.rs` is `vigia::run` with the terminal taken out and **no writer of its own**, pointed at an existing checkout with an agent writing to it. §7 carries the rules its shape rests on. It gates nothing, deliberately: a window that is whatever a session happened to be cannot carry a threshold, and the soak stays where a resource claim is gated. #72 itself stays open on the Shelf, because what it produced is one session and what [#50](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/50) needs is a column of them; the next window now costs a process start rather than an afternoon.

  **The recorded session.** Reference machine, release, this repository (93 tracked files, a 5.0 GiB `target/`), an AI coding agent building, testing and writing to it. Four windows, each quoted with its span and its harness because they are not all the same claim: **850s** and **650s** and **1000s** on the harness as first written, and **675s** on the corrected one described two paragraphs down. Alongside them the sampler §11.2 B7 point 2 names, `git status --porcelain` at 1s over the whole pass: **3,960 samples across 4,942 seconds**. No frame failed in any window.

  **The changed set is nothing like the fixtures, and that is the headline for the shelf.** Max **3** changed files, mean 0.85, and a **clean tree 41.3%** of the time. At most **one** file was inside the two-second settle margin at any sample, in **7.63%** of them; the frame path saw the same thing from inside, at `tracked diffs max 3` in every window. Every deferred cost on the shelf is a function of that count, and the fixtures they were argued against are 100 and 2000 and ten thousand. What the number does **not** license is a narrower budget: this is one agent on one small repository, and a `git reset`, a rebase, a formatter over a large tree or a generated-code sweep are each one command from four figures. It is evidence about likelihood, which is exactly what [#19](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/19), [#16](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/16), [#48](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/48) and [#73](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/73) were deferred on, and each now carries its re-reading.

  **I1 is doing the most visible work in the whole report, and no fixture could have shown it.** The watch took **91,449 wakeups and filtered 91,252 of them, 99.8%, to produce 55 ticks**, and 58,909 to 58,338 for 151 in the corrected window. That is a 5.0 GiB `target/` under a running build, and a synthetic worktree has no such thing anywhere in it. The redraw path saw none of it: for the first **530 seconds** of the first window there were **zero frames** while tens of thousands of events arrived and were discarded, which is I1's claim stated by a real workload rather than by an idle fixture.

  **What breached, and it is not the thing anyone was watching for.** One frame at **591.74ms** against I9's 16ms, fifty-four frames into a settled session, on one changed file and nine drawn rows, with **no key pressed**: the tick wrote the first Markdown this process had drawn, and RSS stepped 27.7 to 44.5 MiB across the same sample. The corrected harness reproduced it at **591.09ms**, frame 140 of 151, two paths in the tick. This bullet's own first-touch entry below lists the ways a reader meets a cold grammar and every one is a gesture. Probed on a scratch tree rather than assumed: first `.md` write **575.03ms**, second write to the same file **0.77ms**, first write to a *different* `.md` **4.70ms**, and the two `.rs` writes **5.52ms then 115.35ms**, which is §6's per-pattern finding arriving from a new direction. [#129](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/129), and it is a Phase 8 row rather than a shelf entry because what is left of it is a hitch a reader feels.

  **RSS is set by which grammars have compiled, not by the diff**, which is the question the soak's own plateau could not have separated and which four starting levels settle. A window that started on a **clean** tree began at **8.96 MiB**; one with a single dirty `.rs` began at **23.08**; one with `.rs` and Markdown both dirty, so the warmer had both to warm, began at **44.99**; and each climbed as further grammars compiled, to peaks of 21.98, 44.5 and 47.69. The soak's plateau over its own 20 x 200 fixture is 18.5 to 22.0 MiB and over 100 x 500 is 30.4, so a real repository sits **above** a fixture five times its size and the term that separates them is not the diff. No drift is claimed from any window: all four are far under I3's, and this instrument prints levels and refuses verdicts.

  **The `git diff` oracle came back clean, on the checkout that most deserved it.** Thirty-four sweeps, one every 120 seconds, each in its own process against its own `Worktree` so it cannot warm what is being measured, comparing per-file `+N -M` against `git diff --numstat -z` in **both directions** and the changed path set against `git status --porcelain -z`. Zero disagreements. The tree is `core.autocrlf=true` with LF blobs and CRLF on disk, which is [#65](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/65)'s exact configuration and the one no fixture had until §7's fixture-axis rule was written.

  **Three numbers were taken by an instrument that was wrong, and which of them survive is stated rather than left to be worked out.** The harness left `Shell::draw`'s repaint out, so its **first** frame was the plain half of a frame the reader sees painted twice and the colour landed on the frame after it; §7 carries the mechanism and the gate that now holds it. **Unaffected**: everything at frame three or later, which is where all four windows' headline figures sit — the 591.74ms at frame 55, the 591.09ms at frame 140, every wakeup and filter count, every RSS level, and the whole sampler and oracle record, none of which reads a frame index at all. **Withdrawn**: a smoke run's *"max 6.48ms, none at or over budget"* over 13 frames, quoted on #72 while the instruments were being started. That maximum **was** the first frame, so it is the plain half and not a frame any reader sees; the corrected harness measures the same shape at **558.83ms**. **Suspect and not quoted**: two second-frame figures, 146.12ms and 53.01ms, which sit exactly where a displaced first-frame debt would land and cannot be told apart from one.

  **Two things one session cannot do, stated rather than stretched.** #50 asks for a week and this is one datum in the column it was waiting on; the evidence is on that issue and the ruling waits for more of it. And #72's own item 5, *"what was reached for and not found"*, needs a person: the two entries it has came from a human reader and no harness adds a third. What changed is the cost of the next one.
- [ ] **An attributes change is a fourth way a cached artefact goes stale, and two of the three ways it can arrive are still invisible.** Writing a `.gitattributes` changes what git's clean filter does to files it does not touch, so a `FileDiff` or a `FileSpan` computed under the old rules describes bytes that are no longer the ones a diff would compare, while every term in the reuse rule reports "unchanged". `Frame::advance` therefore drops both caches when a `.gitattributes` is in the changed set, which is what an agent in the other pane writing one looks like. What that does **not** see is a change to `core.autocrlf` (configuration, not a file the status walk reports) or to `.git/info/attributes` (inside `.git`, which the walk does not enumerate). Both leave a stale diff on screen until something else touches the file, and neither has a gate, because reaching them from a test means changing configuration mid-session rather than writing a file. Measured before the guard existed: a file committed LF and held CRLF reported **80 rows where a cold frame computes 8**, indefinitely. `crates/vigia-core/tests/normalise.rs::a_running_frame_drops_what_it_cached_when_attributes_change` covers the file-shaped case on both caches, and mutation confirms it: clearing neither map, only the spans, or only the diffs each turns it red. The other two are tracked by [#111](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/111), which also holds the three ways they could be noticed and why picking between them is a change to how the frame path decides staleness rather than a line to add. Found by an adversarial audit on [#101](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/101), and the diff half of it predates that branch.
- [ ] **The height walk is incremental now, and what remains is a syscall count rather than a read count.** [#101](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/101) took a tick over a hundred undrawn changed files from 16.98ms p50 to 9.40ms by replacing a read per file with a stat per file ([`RULINGS.md`](RULINGS.md)'s I4 entries hold the numbers), and the term that is left grows the same way: **500 changed files is 501 stats a tick, 15.01ms p50 and 16.82ms p99**, which is *over* I9 rather than at it, and no gate runs at that scale, and 2000 is 26.06ms of stats before anything is drawn. That is [#19](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/19)'s finding reached from a second direction, which is worth saying plainly because #19 was filed against the *diff* path's revalidation and the fix landed on a different one: the wall is the same wall, it has moved from a hundred files to about a thousand, and it is a property of how many `stat` calls a tick can afford rather than of either caller. #19 already names the way out and it is not a faster stat: take the fingerprints from the walk `advance()` already performs. Left to #19 rather than absorbed here, because doing it means changing what `gix`'s status hands back and that is a wider change than the one this ruling needed.
- [ ] The frame path walks status to completion before it reports a file list, so it does not stream the way the raw change iterator does. Two reasons it costs nothing today: rename tracking cannot stream either and is on by default, and a scrollbar needs the file count regardless of how few files are drawn. What is open is whether both hold at ten thousand changed files, where the walk itself could exceed I4. Revisit together with rename tracking above, since they stand or fall together. Tracked by [#48](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/48).
- [x] ~~The header counts changed files and not changed lines. A repository-wide `+`/`-` total needs every file's diff, and I4 makes first paint independent of total diff size, so the two cannot both hold on the first frame. §5's counters are per-file and cost nothing extra, since a file has to be diffed to be drawn; only the total is affected. What is open is whether it is worth computing behind the frame and revealing when it arrives, which belongs with the rest of §5 in Phase 3.~~ **Ruled 2026-07-31: the header does not carry one, and the first reason is the product class rather than the cost.** [#49](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/49).

  **A repository-wide total is a reviewer's number.** It summarises a changeset for someone deciding about it, which is the other product class in §2. What a monitor is asked is what is happening *now*, and the header's file count together with §5's per-file counters answers that already, at no cost and with nothing rounded off. §5.1's own rule points the same way: the picture had a header to spend and spent it on `watching · 3 files`, so the published artifact answers this by omission rather than leaving it open. That header now reads `vigia · 3 changed` … `watching` ([#67](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/67)), which moves two of the three facts and adds none: the omission this ruling rests on is unaffected.

  **The cost is what makes it unaffordable rather than merely unnecessary.** The total needs every changed file's diff, so drawing it puts first paint back in proportion to the size of the diff, which is the single thing I4 exists to forbid. At the ten thousand changed files [#48](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/48) names, the walk alone could exceed the budget.

  **And the one variant that dodges I4 is forbidden by I1.** Computing behind the frame and revealing when it arrives is a wake no filesystem event caused, and in the interval between the tick and the reveal the header would carry a number for a diff that has already moved: a stale claim that looks live, which is the frozen-clock failure §11.1 rejected for the pulse decay on the same grounds. The pulse escaped by being redefined against **event identity** rather than elapsed time. Nothing here can, because an event names paths and never magnitudes, so there is no cheap identity to define a total against.

  **The by-product check §5.2 asks for came back negative, and that is the part worth recording.** That rule has paid three times in this project — I5's follow target, I10's burst paths, and the heat strip's line count were all already being computed and thrown away — and this is the first time it has not. `FileDiff.added` and `removed` exist only where a diff was computed, and I10's history buckets count ticks per path rather than lines. Nothing already in flight adds up to this, so there was no cheap version to find.

  Conditional variants fall together: drawing the total only while the worktree is small enough to afford it puts an element on screen whose presence rule is invisible to a reader, and it would disappear at exactly the moment a tree got busy enough for anyone to want it.

  Gated by `crates/vigia/tests/render.rs::the_header_carries_no_changed_line_total`, over the **drawn row** rather than over what a frame cost. `tests/reads.rs` cannot see this one, and that was confirmed rather than assumed: with a total drawn into the header, all eleven of its gates stayed green, because a total computed on a handle of its own never touches the frame stats they read.
- [x] ~~Is `syntect` fast enough incrementally to hold I2b, or does it force tree-sitter — and with it a C toolchain — back in?~~ **Answered 2026-07-30: it holds, and tree-sitter stays out.** Release build, with `regex-fancy`; `syntect`'s *default* engine is `regex-onig`, which is oniguruma, so the defaults would have put `cc` in the graph on their own. Loading the bundled grammars is **318µs** against I7's 50ms, so nothing has to be deferred to first use. **Loading a grammar and compiling one are different costs, and only the small one is that 318µs**, which "nothing has to be deferred to first use" read as covering and does not: see §6 and [#51](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/51). One screenful of Rust is **1.53ms** against the 16ms I9 budget, and a real shell frame under continuous edits over the 100-file, 100k-line fixture is **10.52ms p99** (p50 7.95ms, max 11.62ms), against **6.97ms** for the core frame path alone before any of this existed. What is not affordable is parsing a hunk *whole*: the 1006-line hunk that fixture produces costs **60.97ms**, which is 3.8x over budget and would be paid on every frame under I9's own shape. So parsing forward only as far as the screen has asked is load bearing rather than an optimisation, which is the same shape I2a found for re-diffing. Revalidation is a hash of the hunk rather than a counter, because inside the settle margin the frame path re-diffs an untouched file every frame and a counter would re-highlight files nobody edited. The cost that stays: the bundled grammars take the release binary from **3.20 MiB to 5.04 MiB**.
- [ ] Highlighting a hunk is forward-only, because `syntect` parses a line from the state the line before it left. The **first** frame that draws deep inside a large hunk therefore pays for everything above it there: landing on the last row of the 1006-line hunk above costs the whole **60.97ms** parse, once. `G`, a follow jump into a large file, and scrolling **up** into the bottom of the previous file all land there directly; scrolling down never does, because a screenful at a time is a screenful of parsing. §7 puts the cold path outside I9 by definition, so this is a first-touch cost rather than a breach, but it is a real hitch on one keypress and is recorded rather than assumed away. The fix, if daily use asks for one, is to bound the parse per frame and leave the tail unclassified until the next. **The reason recorded here for why that could not be done was false in both of its halves, and is corrected rather than deleted, because it was cited to refuse things.** It said the redraw was one the event loop had no reason to schedule and that I1 forbade inventing a timer to get one. **The half about the timer is false and the half about scheduling is only half false, and the difference matters.** There is no timer to invent: [#262](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/262) added `Wake::Warmed`, a wake the frame path sends itself off a background compile, and [#51](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/51) closed by having the shell draw a second frame immediately (`App::owes_repaint`). Both are scheduled on **work** rather than on a clock, so I1's *0 wakeups while idle* does not reach either, and citing it against them is the mis-citation §11.2 records twice. **But neither is the mechanism a bounded parse would need.** `owes_repaint` is drained by an `if` in `Shell::draw` and is capped at exactly one extra frame, structurally: written as a `while` it spun at 100% CPU with the alternate screen held, because `paint` swallows a failed collect and the debt never clears. That is the same spin `RULINGS.md` cites against bounding the parse per frame, so the two must not be read as one supporting the other. A parse bounded across N frames still has no mechanism here, and the honest statement is that it needs one built rather than that the loop refuses to schedule redraws at all. What is genuinely open is whether daily use asks for the bound; what is settled is that I1 is not the reason it cannot be had. Tracked by [#51](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/51).

  **The half of that sentence about the redraw expired on 2026-08-21 and the half about the parse did not.** [#129](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/129) needed the same redraw for the same reason and found it without a timer: a warm that finishes sends on the channel the watch, the input and the signal handler already share, so the loop looks again because something happened rather than because time passed. I1 is untouched, and its own words are why (*no filesystem event and no git index change means no work*, budget *0 wakeups while idle*). So a bounded parse is now **schedulable**, and what is still open is whether the deep-landing hitch is worth bounding at all: it is one keypress into one large hunk, where #129's was every write of a language the session had not met.

  **Daily use did ask, and the half that was fixable is fixed.** [#45](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/45) reported a fast scroll dropping frames, and the measurement split this bullet in two. A *first* entry from below still costs the whole walk and is still the cold path: **25.01ms** for a 120-row hunk of wide-character Markdown, against 139µs for a frame that reused it. That is unchanged, because the timer objection above is unchanged. What was not the cold path is a hunk re-entered after leaving the screen, which was paying the same 26.39ms for a parse that had been in memory one frame earlier, and `RETAINED_HUNKS` fixes it: four hunks are kept after they scroll off, so reversing direction over ground already read costs **397µs** instead. Bounded by a constant added to the viewport, not by the session, so I3 sees a slightly higher plateau rather than drift.

  **And the per-line cost is a function of line length, not of the script.** The obvious hypothesis was `fancy-regex` backtracking over CJK, and it is wrong: 660-byte Markdown parses at **0.32µs a byte** against **1.76µs a byte** for the 34-byte Rust lines above, so wide content is *cheaper* per byte and expensive only because the lines are nineteen times longer. Nothing here is Japanese-specific. Attributed by running the identical bytes under an extension `syntect` has no grammar for: **708µs of a 764µs collect** at p99 is the parse.
- [x] ~~Does a drawn row cost the pane it is drawn into, or the whole line behind it?~~ **Answered 2026-07-31: the whole line, and it is fixed.** [#45](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/45). `printable` walked every character of a span and `put_runs_marked` clipped only afterwards, so an 80-column pane showing a 531-column line walked and allocated all of it. Measured over a 22-row body of Japanese: **8231 source characters examined to fill 1600 columns, 5.1x**, and unchanged by pane width, which is the signature of a cost that follows the content. Bounded now at the pane, and the same body examines **1342**. What it is worth in wall clock is small and is stated rather than rounded up: the paint went from about **104µs to 60µs p99**, against a 16ms frame. It is fixed anyway, for the reason the `heat_of` bullet below gives: the shape is the one I4 exists to forbid, and leaving a known instance of it both unfixed and ungated is what let this one reach a reader. The gate is `crates/vigia/tests/paint.rs`, and the counter it reads is `PaintStats`.

  Two things fell out of it that are worth more than the microseconds. **No gate in this repo had ever painted** (§7 now says so as a rule), and **no fixture had a line wider than a pane**, so neither tier could have caught this whatever it cost.
- [x] ~~A trackpad reports one flick as a stream of scroll events. Is one redraw each affordable?~~ **Answered 2026-07-31: no, and it was the larger half of the report.** Each wheel event was its own full frame, so a fast gesture rendered every position it passed through, and over a large diff most of those positions enter a hunk nothing has parsed. The loop now drains what is already queued, handles every wake in arrival order, and paints **once** per batch, capped at 64 so a faster event source cannot starve the screen. Measured structurally: a hundred notches drawn one at a time highlight 4x more lines than the same travel drawn once, and the reader lands in the same place. It costs nothing to I1, because a drain is `try_recv` and never a wait.

  > [!NOTE]
  > **What is *not* open here, because it was a breach and is fixed**
  >
  > The **repeat** of that cost was, and it was found by auditing rather than by
  > using the tool. A hunk whose content changed used to throw its whole parse
  > away, so the walk above was paid on *every* frame for as long as the file
  > being read was the file being written — 520 lines per frame for a 22-row
  > body, **53ms p99** four screenfuls in, sustained and with no input. A changed
  > hunk now rewinds to the deepest parse position the new content still agrees
  > with, so a frame costs a screenful plus one `CHECKPOINT_STRIDE` at any depth:
  > 40 lines and **11.47ms p99** on the same fixture, against 10.02ms at the top.
  >
  > Two lessons went into §7 rather than staying here. A budget measured at one
  > position is measured at its cheapest one, and the caller-side gate §7 asks for
  > can be the *absolute* tier rather than only the structural one.
- [ ] **The heat projection is the one drawn thing whose cost follows the file rather than the window.** Placing a change needs its working-tree line number, and a line number is only known by counting the lines before it, so `heat_of` walks every line of a drawn file's hunks even though the viewport shows a screenful. Everything else in the shell follows the screen: rows above the window are counted rather than built, highlighting is asked for only on a row that is pushed, and a file the viewport never reaches is never diffed. This is not a read, so `reads.rs` does not move and I2a is untouched; it is arithmetic over lines already in memory, and it runs only for rows actually drawn. **The count that "actually drawn" means changed on 2026-08-01**, and the measurement below predates it: it used to be one or two headings a frame, and the pinned list of §11.1 adds one per row it draws that the diff walk did not already build, so the bound is now the list's **drawn height**, plus the headings in the diff, plus the one file the viewport is sitting inside: [#257](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/257) lands follow below a file's heading routinely, and §11.1 records why that file's entry is built anyway rather than asked of the frame a second time. It is not built on a pane with no list, where nothing would read it. That was `LIST_ROWS`, a flat six, and it is a share of the pane since [#160](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/160), so the bound follows the window here the way every other one does rather than being a constant: twelve rows on a fifty-row pane, six on a twenty-four. **And the cap is gone entirely beside a rail**, ruled 2026-08-23 by [#252](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/252): from 134 columns the list is a column of its own and its rows cost the diff nothing, so a fifty-row pane draws forty-six rows where the share drew twelve, and forty-three of them with the band drawn. The shape of the question is unchanged and its worst case grew twice with the pane it is measured on, which is what this bullet has to be re-measured against when it is next picked up. What the rail did not leave unmeasured is the *frame*: a 200x40 pane drawing thirty-six pinned rows is **0.71ms p50 and 0.79ms p99 against I9's 16ms**, worst of three rounds interleaved with the 80x24 screen every other budget is taken on, which read 0.99ms and 1.36ms. So the worst case grew and the frame it sits in did not, which is the number a reader of this bullet would otherwise have to take on trust. The walk hands its entries to the list precisely so the overlap is not paid twice, but the non-overlapping rows are new walks. Measured, release, over the 100-file 100k-line fixture **before the region existed**: the shell frame went from **7.66ms to 8.14ms p99** against the 16ms I9 budget, and on the same runs the **core** frame path, which does not execute any of this code, moved 6.96ms to 7.29ms. So most of the difference is run-to-run variation and what is left is a few tenths of a millisecond. Recorded rather than assumed away, because the shape is the one I4 exists to prevent even where this instance of it is affordable. If it ever matters, the fix is to carry the line number on `Line` rather than recomputing it, which is the same information the gutter already derives twice. Tracked by [#55](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/55).
- [ ] Default view: unstaged only, or working-tree-vs-HEAD? Unstaged is the thesis; confirm against a week of real use. Tracked by [#50](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/50), and the week of real use it waits on is [#72](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/72), **which recorded its first session 2026-08-07 and moved to the Shelf beside this question rather than closing.** The blocker it was is gone and the evidence it owed is arriving one window at a time, which is the change: a session now records one for the price of starting a process rather than for the price of somebody sitting and watching. What #72 could not supply is the half only a person can, *"what was reached for and not found"*, and that is the half this question is: the two entries on it so far are a reader expecting to see the repository's **worktrees** and a reader reading committed files as missing rather than as done, and the second is this question arriving from outside. Still open, and now open on evidence that accumulates rather than on a prerequisite that had nobody to do it.
- [ ] Windows: supported target or best-effort? **Half answered 2026-07-31 by [#11](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/11), and the halves are separated the way B5's were.** The technical half is closed: degradation is a mechanism with a gate over it rather than a hope, so a console that cannot draw 24-bit gets 256 by detection and not by accident, and the colours it does draw were chosen for it. That was the part of this bullet that named an actual risk. What is left is a **posture** question with no work in it, which is whether the README and the release notes say *supported* or *best-effort*, and it belongs with the first release rather than here. `ROADMAP.md` has recorded that placement since before this was half-answered. **Reopened 2026-08-03 on a technical half that was never in it: colour degrades and glyphs do not.** `Depth` is a ladder with a gate on every rung, and there is no equivalent for characters, while much of what this tool draws is outside CP437, which the legacy Windows console still uses under some code pages. Measured against the code page rather than assumed, because the assumption was wrong in both directions: **outside** are the sparkline ramp's `▁▂▃▅▆▇`, the caret `▸`, the follow marker `▶`, the pulse mark `●`, the elision `…`, the continuation `›`, the rename arrow `←` (which IBM's graphic table does place at 0x1B, and which is unusable anyway because a console consumes that byte as an escape introducer, so it is outside in effect if not in letter) and, from 2026-08-15, the scrollbar's step buttons `▲` and `▼` ([#166](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/166)); **inside** are `█`, `▄` and, from 2026-08-16, the heat strip's `■` at 0xFE ([#196](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/196), which changed that element's glyph and picked the candidate on **this list**: `▪` U+25AA looks the same and is outside, so choosing it would have made this sentence false while nothing failed), so the heat strip and the scrollbar thumb survive whole, along with the header separator `·`, the rule `─`, the sparkline track `_` and, from 2026-08-15, the scrollbar track `│` ([#175](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/175), which moved it off the outside list by centring it: the glyph that looks right on every terminal is also the one that survives this one). So the loss does not fall along the line anyone would guess. A reader on that console keeps the heat strip, the thumb and the track this issue added, and loses the sparkline's *shape*, the caret marking which file the diff is inside, the pulse, and every marker that says a label was cut. Nothing detects or reports it. **The step buttons were added in full knowledge of this and are the first element chosen *because* of it rather than in spite of it**: every candidate for an arrow is outside, including `▴ ▾ ↑ ↓`, and the two triangles IBM's graphic table does carry are at 0x1E and 0x1F, which is the `←` trap again. What settled it is one column over. The track they sit on is already outside, so the console that loses a button lost the shape of that column before there were any, and the pair adds no new class of loss to weigh. The same reasoning is what keeps this bullet open rather than closing it: a rule that happens to be free once is not a ladder, and the next element to face this will not have a neighbour already conceded. Recorded rather than fixed because the fix is a second ladder and this has no evidence behind it yet: `crates/vigia/src/render.rs` has claimed for three phases that this bullet leaves the console open, and until now the bullet did not mention it, which is the drift `CLAUDE.md` rule 1 exists to catch. It is a question about which terminals are in scope before it is a question about glyphs, so it sits with the posture half rather than getting a bullet of its own. **The glyph half is closed 2026-08-17 by [#159](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/159); the posture half is what keeps this bullet open.** The second ladder called for two sentences up exists: `Glyphs` picks `block`, `braille` or `octant` the way `Depth` picks a colour rung. **What made it decidable is that this bullet was wrong about its own mechanism.** *"There is no equivalent for characters"* is true of **probing** and false of **deciding**: no terminal reports which glyphs its font carries, and `Depth` never asked one either, it reads `TERM_PROGRAM` and `TERM` and consults a table of what somebody checked. The same shape works here, measured rather than assumed for the third time on this bullet: **Consolas, which is what the legacy console draws with, carries none of U+2800**, while Cascadia Mono and Cascadia Code carry all of it, so Windows without `WT_SESSION` takes blocks and Windows Terminal takes braille. That is the exact inverse of `Depth`'s Windows rung, which claims 24-bit for the whole platform, and the asymmetry is the finding rather than an inconsistency: **colour aged out on that console and glyphs did not, because the font did not age with it.** Octants are in the ladder and **detection never returns them**, since no font measured carries U+1CD00 including the newest Cascadia and the terminals that draw them do so natively; they are reachable only by `VIGIA_GLYPHS=octant`. What is left, and is the honest residue rather than a closed question, is everything on the outside list that is **not** the sparkline: the caret, the pulse and the elision markers are still drawn unconditionally, so a legacy console still loses them in silence and nothing reports that either.

## 11. Behaviour

§3 says how well `vigia` does things. Every number in it is defensible and every one has a test. Nothing above says **what happens when you press a key, or when nothing has changed** — and that gap is why the product reads as a screenshot with budgets attached.

Two parts: what the shell already does, recorded because it was decided in code first, and what is still undecided.

> [!WARNING]
> **Behaviour decided in code without a line here is a defect**
>
> The README says this file is the source of truth, written before the code. That
> was **not true** for the whole interaction surface on 2026-07-30: the keymap and
> the treatment of untracked files were both settled in implementation and appear
> nowhere above. §11.1 repays that, and the rule it leaves behind is the same
> shape as §3's: **an invariant without a failing test is a wish, and a behaviour
> without a spec line is an accident.** Neither is caught by `take-next`'s
> pre-flight, which compares invariant tokens rather than behaviour.

### 11.1 What the shell does today

Most of this was back-filled from the implementation on 2026-07-30 rather than newly decided. **Follow mode is the exception**: it was ruled on 2026-07-30, as B1 and B2 below, and written here *before* the code existed — which is the order the README claims for everything and which §11's warning exists because this file did not keep. Where this disagrees with the code, the code is the bug.

**What the diff contains.** Working tree against the index. **Untracked files are included** and diff as all-additions — load-bearing rather than incidental, since creating new files is among the most common things an agent does, and a tool blind to them would miss its own use case. Rename tracking is **on by default**: showing a move as an unrelated delete plus add misdescribes what the agent did. Its streaming cost is the open question in §10, not a settled trade.

**The working-tree side is normalised the way git's clean filter would normalise it**, and the diff is taken against that rather than against the bytes on disk. Git does not compare raw bytes either: it runs the working-tree side through the clean filter first, so on a checkout with `core.autocrlf=true` — what the Windows installer writes — the CRLF on disk is turned back into the LF the blob holds before anything is compared. Skipping that made every line of every such file differ from its stored form, which is [#65](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/65): a 21-line edit drawn as **+905 −885**, and a file `git diff` calls unchanged drawn as a whole-file rewrite, on the installed default of one of three tier-1 targets. Two shapes, and the smaller is the one that was reported: with `* text=auto eol=lf` only an editor writing CRLF creates the discrepancy, while with no `.gitattributes` at all the checkout itself does, so *every* edit to *any* text file read as a rewrite.

**A file whose only difference is its line endings reports no change, and stays listed while doing so.** The second half is a ruling rather than a leftover. Dropping it from the list needs its content during `Frame::advance`, which is a read per changed file on the path I4 governs and is exactly what that invariant forbids; and a header count disagreeing with the body under it is the contradiction B3's empty state exists to remove. Both halves are also what git does, which is the tie-breaker rather than a coincidence: `git status` lists such a file and `git diff` reports nothing for it.

**The rules the filter applies are re-read once a frame, not once a process.** `.gitattributes` and `core.autocrlf` decide what a diff normalises, and both are things the agent in the other pane is free to write: a monitor that resolved them at startup would go on drawing the old answer indefinitely while a restart drew a different one, which is I5 failing silently over a window I3 measures in days. So `Frame::advance` drops the filter and the next working-tree read rebuilds it, which bounds staleness to one frame without giving up the laziness: a frame whose diffs are all reused reads nothing and so rebuilds nothing, and only a frame already going to disk pays. `gix` rebuilds its own attributes stack on every status walk, so this matches the freshness of the walk it is paired with rather than inventing a policy. `crates/vigia-core/tests/normalise.rs` gates it **through `Frame::advance`** rather than through two bare calls to `Worktree::diff`, because nothing in the latter marks a frame boundary and a gate written that way passes whatever the filter's lifetime is.

**`core.safecrlf` is not consulted, because it guards writing.** It makes `git add` refuse a file whose conversion would not round-trip, so that history cannot be corrupted by one; `git diff` ignores it entirely, which is checkable and is checked, since the same mixed-terminator file git **refuses to add** is one it reports as unchanged. `vigia` never writes, so the guard covers an operation it does not perform, and inheriting it meant failing the frame over one file with mixed line endings, on that frame and every frame after it, because the file stays mixed. One such file anywhere in the tree stopped the pane working. Found by auditing rather than by using it, which is the only reason it is not a third row in the table above.

**External filter drivers are not run, and that is §6 rather than an omission.** `gitattributes` can point the clean filter at an external program, and Git LFS is the common one: a repository using it sets `filter.lfs.clean` to a command. Running those would mean spawning a process per file per frame, which is precisely the shape §6 rules out when it takes an in-process diff over a `git diff` subprocess per tick. `eol`, `working-tree-encoding` and `ident` are pure Rust and do apply. What the ruling costs is recorded rather than hidden: under LFS the worktree holds real content while the blob holds a pointer, so an LFS-tracked *text* file diffs as a rewrite. That is not a regression, since it is what happened before any filter ran at all, and it is [#69](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/69). What decides that a file is **binary** is a separate question this file does not yet answer, and is [#68](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/68): the engine sniffs content for NUL bytes, which is git's fallback and not its rule, so a path the attributes declare `binary` is diffed as text anyway.

**The body is three regions since 2026-08-17: a masthead, a pinned file list, a rule, and the scrolling diff** ([#158](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/158)), and the first of them is drawn only where a reader has asked for it ([#204](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/204), whose paragraph is below). The masthead is the worktree churn band with a blank row above and below it, and everything the next paragraphs say about the list holds one region up with one difference, which is the clamp order: **the band gives way before the list does**. The list is a map of the diff and yields to the diff; the band is a fact about the worktree and yields to both. A pane that could afford a band by shrinking the list draws its whole cap of list and no band, deliberately, because §5.3's *richness is the reward of space* means extra space rather than space taken from the map. That cap is a share of the pane since [#160](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/160), which couples the two in one direction only: the list may deepen into rows the band was going to be offered, never the reverse. What keeps a taller pane from ever losing a band it had is the cap's step, one row of list per four rows of pane, so the rows left after the list never fall.

**The band is drawn only where the diff keeps a whole hunk, and that number is derived rather than chosen.** `git diff -U3` at its smallest is a hunk header, three lines of context, one changed line, three more and the file's own heading, which is nine rows; the floor is ten. Below it the masthead is not drawn at all, and below eight columns of drawable width it is not drawn either, because a band narrower than the samples it holds would drop columns and a projection that drops items is the shape §5.1 refuses for the heat strip in as many words. **Both floors live in the split rather than in the drawer**, which is the correction that shipped with the element: the width floor lived only in the painter for one commit, so a narrow pane reserved four rows for a region the drawer then declined to draw and the masthead was four blank rows pushing the list down for nothing.

**Superseded the same day, and kept because a measurement is still a measurement.** The next four paragraphs describe the band as #223 built it, from *"A band column is eight seconds"* down to *"What this does not change"*, and every claim in them about columns, periods and constants is history: `GRAPH_COLUMNS`, `GRAPH_PERIOD` and the `const` assertion over them are all retired. The ruling in force is **"the band is drawn the way a system monitor draws one"**, which is the paragraph immediately after that block, and it says which part of the measurement survived. **A band column is eight seconds, not one, and the pane's width buys wider bars rather than finer time.** Ruled and implemented 2026-08-18 ([#223](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/223)), reported from a live pane as *"it doesn't translate to anything to me, just noise"*. The store holds 120 samples over a 120 second window, so a sample is a second, and `Churn::projected` clamped the drawn width to the sample count: on any pane at or above 120 columns **one column was one second**. A save is instantaneous, so it occupied a single column and left both neighbours empty, and eight saves in two minutes drew eight hairlines across a band that was otherwise blank.

**The band already re-projected; the defect was that it stopped.** That function aggregates samples per column at narrow widths and became the identity at wide ones, so a bigger pane bought time resolution nobody asked for. The fix is a floor on the aggregation rather than new machinery: the band draws at most `GRAPH_COLUMNS` of them, each stretched across the cells its share of the width gives it, so the period is `HISTORY_WINDOW / GRAPH_COLUMNS` and never finer. Fifteen columns of eight seconds, and 120 divides by 15 exactly, which a `const` assertion holds the way `history.rs` already holds the sparkline's own division.

**A sample weighs the bytes a write moved, not the fact that it happened.** Ruled and implemented 2026-08-18 ([#232](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/232)), reported from a live pane one release after the period above was fixed. `Track::bump` added one per path per coalesced tick, so a sample counted *files written*. In a worktree where one file is saved repeatedly that puts exactly one in every sample, which makes the window its own peak, and heights are scaled against that peak: **every active column drew at full height**, and the element said *when* and could never say *how much*. The masthead read as a row of identical blocks with gaps, which is the opposite of the *change density over time* §5.1 names this store for, and the same arithmetic reached the per-file sparkline. **The period fix is what exposed it**: at one second a column a save drew a hairline between blanks and the whole thing read as scatter, so the flat heights were invisible underneath. Widening the bars is what made them legible. **The size is taken by the shell and not by the store**, one `symlink_metadata` per changed path per wake, because this crate has no working directory and because putting the syscall at the call site is what keeps its cost inside the frame the budget gates measure. **The cap that was written here twice is the finding, and it is gone.** Sizing a full 256-path burst measures 2.60ms of thread CPU in isolation, which was used to bound it; measured in the frame it sits in, interleaved over thirty rounds of a hundred-file bulk rewrite, that frame costs 18.43ms sizing nothing and 17.93ms sizing all of them, and the *unsized* run is the slower. `Frame::advance` walks status on the same wake and stats every one of those paths to decide they changed at all, so the metadata is warm and the marginal syscall costs nothing measurable. A component measured alone is not a budget, and `CLAUDE.md` carries the rule that came out of it. The duplicated work is still real and is [#233](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/233), an efficiency row rather than a reason to draw a worse graph. `sizing_a_whole_burst_does_not_change_the_frame_it_sits_in` compares the two arms rather than one number against a ceiling. **The floor is one and not the unit**: a write whose size cannot be read, and the first write of a path, both weigh one, so a store fed no sizes behaves exactly as it did before and every gate written against that still holds. A first write is a baseline rather than a change, because charging a file's whole size on first sight would spike the peak on the first save of a session and flatten the two minutes after it. The difference is **absolute**, so a deletion weighs what it removed: signing it would draw the largest edit a reader can make as the quietest.

**And both glance elements draw a *level* rather than the events that made it, ruled 2026-08-18 by [#242](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/242).** Reported from a live pane as spikes on a flat line, and the picture is the ruling rather than a new one: `assets/preview.svg` draws every sparkline as a wave, its first row `4 8 14 20 24 16 8 4`, monotone up then monotone down with the colour ramp following it. A write is a **point event**, so the retained samples are zero almost everywhere and an area chart of them is a spike train; §5.1's own rule is that a published artifact answering an open question is the answer, so these elements were specified as levels before the first commit and drawing the events raw was the defect. **A causal decay is the obvious first idea and is the wrong shape**: an impulse rises instantly and falls slowly, which draws `█▇▃▁`, a sawtooth. The picture rises *and* falls, so the kernel looks both ways, which is what reading a point process as a density means. `HISTORY_LEVEL` is **six seconds**, and the number is a trade rather than a taste: measured against the aggregation these values are actually read through, two bursts thirty seconds apart leave a trough at **0.23** of the peak there, 0.39 at eight seconds, 0.52 at ten and 0.62 at twelve, where they stop being two things. Those figures were re-derived when [#234](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/234) halved the source bucket, because every one of them is read through a bucket: the constant did not move and its margin improved, a finer bucket smearing less, and this file and the gate's own docblock stopped quoting two different numbers for eight seconds while they were being checked. Derived twice, and the first derivation was wrong in a way worth keeping: modelled against a maximum per bucket it read as eight, and a drawn bucket is a **sum**, which smears further. **It is a view rather than a correction**: `Churn::projected` and `History::churn` keep their meaning and their gates, and `Churn::levels` and `History::level` are what the shell reads, so nothing the store retains changes and I10 and I3 are untouched. One kernel and one constant serve both elements, which is half of [#234](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/234)'s coherence requirement met by construction; the other half is the **window**, and §11.1 carries it. **The kernel is edge-normalised**, dividing by the weight actually available at each sample, because without it a uniformly busy window droops at both ends and the newest end drooping means the present under-reports itself. **And one is still the floor**: a level divides by the kernel's weight, so a single recorded write became a fraction and rounded to nothing until this was said a second time. Two consequences are recorded rather than absorbed, and the second is **ruled by [#244](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/244) on 2026-08-22**: a dense cell's two halves now differ by at most about one rung, so [#232](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/232)'s horizontal resolution buys little, and the braille rung therefore draws a **coarser** band than the block rung, which took the band back off the glyph ladder. §11.1 carries the ruling. The figure first recorded here was *4 against 6*, and it is corrected rather than repeated: that counted distinct **glyphs** on one row, and a braille glyph spells a *pair* of sub-columns, so it conflated two columns with a height. Counted as heights the direction is the same and the gap is wider, **7 against 16**. Ageing the graph so a quiet window slides rather than freezing was [#243](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/243), ruled 2026-08-22 and carried by §11.1 and by I1's own row.

**And the band is drawn the way a system monitor draws one, which supersedes [#223](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/223)'s coarsening.** Ruled 2026-08-18 after the reader asked for the reference class §2 names to be studied rather than recalled. Three things it does that this band did not. **One value per sub-column**: a 2x4 cell carries two, older on the left, which is why such graphs read as a line where a stretched value reads as a block; the projection here does the same job from the other side, since I10 fixes the window where a scrolling monitor sizes its buffer to the pane. **An axis**: a graph of a signal that is bursty and zero most of the time forces one mark on the bottom row, so a quiet stretch draws a floor rather than nothing. **A scale that is not the maximum**: the denominator sits above the ordinary value rather than at the largest one, and anything past it saturates. **#223 diagnosed the right defect and reached for the wrong fix.** A save drawing a one-column hairline between blanks is real, and the answer is the floor rather than a wider column: with the axis drawn, a narrow spike stands on something, and coarsening then costs resolution and buys nothing. Without it, every filled column floats, which is what a reader saw and reported as blocks. **#158 is narrowed rather than reversed**: an empty column inside a live window draws the axis, and a window holding nothing at all still draws nothing, which is the case that ruling actually came from, since every session opens with a dirty worktree and no tick behind it. **The scale is not the window's peak either**: one `cargo build` rewriting a lock file is two orders of magnitude above an ordinary save, and against that denominator every edit for the next two minutes draws one level high, which is a graph going blank because something happened. `crates/vigia/tests/masthead.rs` gates the axis, the scale factor, the level count and the two samples a dense cell carries; all five mutations to the drawer die.

**And the scale is not the mean either, since 2026-08-22** ([#256](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/256), reported from a live pane). *"It has a nice wave, but the wave goes missing after"*, then *"Just spikes"*. Scaling above the ordinary write is the right rule and a **mean** is not a robust way to find the ordinary write: agent work is heavy tailed, a test run rewriting thousands of bytes sits in the same window as the 180-byte edits around it, and the outlier this rule exists to let saturate was setting the denominator from inside it. Measured at eighty columns on a trace shaped like the report, **36 of 76 columns were pinned on the band's lowest level and the band drew 7 distinct heights**; the same trace now pins none and draws 11 to 14 across the widths measured. **The rule is that a value more than ten times the median of the non-empty values is left out of the mean**, and it moves both glance elements because it lives in `vigia-core` beside the store rather than in either drawer: they divide by different quantities, worktree-wide and per file, and may not differ about the rule. **It is a no-op wherever nothing is outlying**, by construction rather than by care: nothing above the cut means the arithmetic is the thirteen tenths it has always been, and measured across eleven pane widths the steady, wave, four-to-one and uniform fixtures draw identically, as do 40 of 40 held-out flat-tailed traces. **Ten was measured and both ends of its interval redden a gate.** Sweeping the multiple over `crates/vigia/tests/masthead.rs`'s fixtures, at **7** a four-to-one series stops drawing what it drew before and at **18** the floor comes back on a burst filling a third of the window, so the safe band is `[8, 17]` and a fixture binds each end. Ten rather than that band's middle because the band widened: the first shape of this row cut the projection and measured `[8, 12]`, whose midpoint is ten, and taking the cut before the projection raised the ceiling without moving the floor. **And what is outlying is decided once, at the source resolution, rather than wherever the values are summed.** That ordering is what keeps a coarser rung's yardstick from falling below a finer rung's, and a drawn bucket summing more source buckets cannot be worth less than one summing fewer, so a width rung stays a change of resolution rather than of height. The proof is one line: grouping puts every source bucket in exactly one group, so the kept sum is the same whichever rung is drawn, and merging buckets can only reduce the number of non-empty ones, so thirteen tenths of sum over count is non-decreasing as the rung coarsens. Take the cut per grouping instead and each rung keeps a different sum, the shared term is gone and the ordering goes with it: measured on `tests/rows.rs`'s one-path fixture that shape gives 418, 837 and **302**, a coarser rung drawing every bar taller, where this one gives 418, 837 and 1116. `crates/vigia-core/tests/history.rs` gates the ordering on a one-path window and on a populated one. **The band takes the same order for the same reason**, one element over: its drawn series is a projection of the window onto the sub-columns a pane affords, so a cut taken on what it draws changes membership every time the pane is resized. Measured across widths 36 to 200, one column of resize moved the figure by up to **41.3%** that way, and by **91.4%** on a longer burst, against 2.9% for the plain mean. Cutting the samples and projecting what is left costs 6.5% and 7.3%, and leaves a window with nothing outlying in it drawing exactly what the plain mean draws at every width. `Churn::scale_at` is that order and `crates/vigia/tests/masthead.rs` gates both halves. **What it costs, measured in situ rather than in isolation**: interleaved at the 256-path cap over a forty-file diff on a 120x40 pane, a whole frame is **294.0µs p50 and 299.3µs p99** with the cut against 291.1 and 298.9 without, which is 1.84% of I9's 16ms against 1.82%.

**Eight seconds was tuned rather than chosen**, over forty seeded series across three work patterns, and the number is recorded here because the shape of the answer is not obvious: emptiness falls monotonically as columns coarsen, but the count of *distinct drawn heights* peaks near four seconds and collapses by fifteen, so both ends lose the shape for opposite reasons. At one second a steady worktree draws 72% empty; at eight, 25%; at fifteen it is 8% empty but down to five and a half of nine rungs, which is a solid block rather than a graph. Eight is the knee, and it is one constant with an assertion under it.

**Two reasons, and the second was found while checking a reader's question rather than while diagnosing the complaint.** The first is aggregation: the sparkline sums ten samples a bucket and therefore reads as a shape where the band read as scatter, which is a ten-fold difference between two elements on one row. The second is that **the window slides and the newest sample is always partial**, since `Track::shift` zero-fills the newest end while `opened` advances a whole sample at a time. At one second a column the newest bar was up to **100%** incomplete and therefore always short until the second closed; at eight it is at most 13%, which is the order the sparkline has always lived at. Neither element has any *latency*: a write is bumped into the newest sample and the series rebuilt in the same pass, so it is on screen the next frame.

**What this does not change.** Not `HISTORY_WINDOW`, not `HISTORY_SAMPLES`, not the sparkline's buckets, and not I10, whose bound is the window and the path cap rather than the drawn resolution. And not the ruling below, which is the one a coarser column might have looked like an excuse to revisit.

**An empty column of the band draws the axis, and an empty *window* still draws nothing.** #158 ruled that an empty column drew nothing, because a full track of `_` *"reads as a dashed rule across the pane"*, reported from a first real run. That is exactly what a graph's axis is, and #232 is what made the difference legible: a graph of a signal that is zero most of the time draws the floor anyway, and without it every filled column floats with nothing to stand on, which is what a reader saw and reported as blocks. **So the ruling is narrowed rather than reversed.** A quiet stretch inside a live window is a floor; a window holding nothing at all is still nothing, which is the case #158 actually came from, since every session opens on a worktree that may already be dirty with no tick behind it. The distinction it does not draw is between *not started* and *quiet for two minutes*, deliberately: both are an empty window, and this ruling is about what an empty window draws rather than about how it got there. #78 is untouched either way, since the sparkline's track answers a different question in a slot with no edges of its own.

**The window ages while it has something to age, and stops when it does not.** Ruled 2026-08-22 ([#243](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/243)), asked for from use alongside #242: *the graph should age*. It did not. `History::roll` is reached from one place, the tick path, so a quiet worktree left the last burst pinned where it was. **That is worse than stale.** The window's axis is time, so a frozen window keeps its newest sample at the right edge and a burst from ninety seconds ago still draws as *just now*, with `Recency` frozen beside it so a file that went quiet keeps its pulse. A monitor whose thesis is *correct with zero interaction* was then incorrect with zero interaction, which is I5, and I1 and I5 are both product-class claims rather than preferences.

**It is an instance of I1's licence rather than an exception to it**, and the reason is the one `RULINGS.md` already records for the first clock: *every other timer the spec refuses runs while nothing is happening*. This one runs only while a burst is still decaying through the window, and the window empties `HISTORY_WINDOW` after the last write, so when nothing is happening the wait is untimed again by construction. What the licence's second condition gains is that the thing arming a clock may be a change in the worktree rather than only a reader's gesture; that it may **outlive** what armed it by a bounded time was already true, since `SCROLL_LINGER` keeps the direction arrows lit for 220ms after the gesture that armed them.

**The period is `HISTORY_SAMPLE`, one second, and it is derived rather than picked.** That is the store's own quantum, so it is the finest interval at which any drawn cell can change and anything faster redraws identical pixels. A coarser period derived from the drawn cell was considered, since a band cell at 109 columns covers 1.1 seconds and a sparkline bucket covers five, and it is **declined on the measurement**: an ageing wake costs **165µs** in release at the path cap, against I9's 16ms, so a width-derived period would cap 1% of one frame budget. A cap is a feature restriction and carries a refusal's bar.

**Ageing costs a paint rather than a tick**, which is what makes that number small: an ageing wake is not a filesystem event, so it does not walk status. The same fixture measures **529µs** for an ordinary tick, and the walk is most of the difference. At most `HISTORY_SAMPLES` wakes follow any burst, so ageing one burst to nothing costs about **19.8ms of CPU spread over two minutes**.

**The sparklines age with the band by construction**, which is [#234](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/234)'s coherence requirement met by the mechanism rather than by two elements being kept in step: there is one store and one roll, and the pulse moves with them. Gating the clock on the masthead being up was the shape the issue proposed and is refused for exactly that reason: the sparklines and the pulse are drawn whether or not the masthead is, so a clock that ran only with the band up would leave the two elements disagreeing about what time it is.

**The pulse's life becomes a grid remainder, and that is the ruling rather than a side effect.** `Recency::Pulse` means a path was named by the newest burst *and* has ink in the newest sample, so once the window ages on its own the mark expires at the next sample boundary rather than surviving until something else is written. Its life is therefore uniform on `(0, HISTORY_SAMPLE]`: half a second on average and arbitrarily short for a write that lands just before a boundary. That is a real change to what a reader sees and it is preferred to both alternatives. Before this row the mark did not expire at all on a quiet tree, which is the freeze itself wearing a different face: a file written two minutes ago drew at full brightness beside a band that had almost drained. Giving the mark a duration of its own would mean a second clock per track, running on wall time while the element beside it runs on the sample grid, so the two would disagree about how long ago *now* was, which is the one thing [#234](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/234) forbids. A mark that means *there is ink in the newest cell* is bounded by that cell, and the cell is a second wide. **The frame a burst causes always keeps its pulse**, which is the case that matters and is held by construction: a turn of the loop takes one instant and both the tick and the roll read it, so a boundary can never fall between a write and the paint it triggered.

**`m` shows the masthead and hides it again, and it starts hidden.** Ruled 2026-08-17 from use: *"I see it is not always needed"*, which is the honest read of an element that costs four rows of the thing this tool exists to show. **The default stood at on for one day and is off since [#204](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/204)**, reversed the same way and by the same reader: the four rows come out of the diff on every pane that can afford the band at all, and an element with that price should be asked for rather than dismissed. What the first ruling was right about is that an element nobody discovers may as well not exist, and that is now paid in the README's key table, which named neither the band nor `m` on the day the toggle shipped. It is not paid in the hint bar, for the reason at the end of this paragraph. Either way it is a keystroke rather than a setting, which is §11.2 B6 keeping this tool configured by gesture. **Nothing leaves the pane with the band**: the branch is the header's since #158 and is drawn on every frame whatever `m` is doing, which is also what keeps the every-frame `.git/HEAD` read a read of something the frame draws. **A keypress is licensed to move content where a notice is not**, and the rule below already draws that line: the split refuses to move for a *transient* thing so that a flicker cannot jog a reader's diff, and an instruction is not a flicker. Unlike `f` it does not jump, because re-engaging follow is a move (a reader asking to follow is asking to see what changed) and asking for the masthead back is asking for the masthead back. It is deliberately **not** in the hint bar, and that is settled rather than open since #80 cut the bar to three items: `m` is named on the sheet below, which is where every gesture is written down now.

**`?` draws the gestures sheet over the pane, and `?` advances it until it takes it away.** Ruled as §11.2 B12 and shipped by [#206](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/206); the ruling and its rejected branch stay in B12 and what the shell does is here. A centred, bordered table of every gesture the pane binds, composited over cells the regions have already drawn, with a close control in its own title bar. **It moves nothing**, which is the property it was chosen for: no rect is smaller for it, no region yields a row, and closing it needs no relayout, so a reader who opens it and closes it is looking at exactly the screen they left. `crates/vigia/tests/sheet.rs::the_sheet_moves_no_content` is that claim as a gate, comparing every cell outside the sheet's own rect with the sheet up and with it down.

**It is not a mode, and that is what keeps it out of B4's refusal.** No key changes meaning while it is up: `q` still quits, `j` still scrolls the diff behind it, a digit still jumps. **`?` itself keeps exactly one meaning, *the sheet*, and B13 is what that costs**: on a pane the whole table does not fit, pressing it again is the next page rather than the close, and the last page is what closes it. On every pane whose sheet is one page, which is every pane the first three rungs serve, that is the toggle it has always been. Only `?` and the close control dismiss it, and **the control is its own action rather than the key's**: `?` means *the sheet* and advances, the control means *close* and closes from any page, and a pointer has no `?` to fall back on. They were one action until #286's audit, which found the sheet's only pointer escape walking a reader forward six pages while this sentence and the code's own docblock both promised the opposite. **`Esc` is untouched** because `Esc` is Quit on every other frame, so teaching it to dismiss would put *dismiss this* one keystroke from *end the program*. The pointer is told where the sheet is, so a click on the control closes it and anything else landing on the sheet does nothing at all: falling through would let a click seek a scrollbar the reader cannot see and a wheel scroll a diff the sheet is covering, which is the one way something that moves no content could still move content.

**It is opaque, and the first version was not** ([#211](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/211), reported from use within an hour of the release that shipped it). Every cell the sheet covers is **reset** before it is styled, so the sheet is a hole punched in the diff rather than a tint over it. `ratatui`'s `Cell::set_style` merges into the style already in the cell, and the chrome's own style carries no background, so the first version kept the `added_row` and `removed_row` washes underneath and drew as green and red bands with a table on top. The gate injects a theme that washes its rows rather than borrowing the shipped palette, because `Theme::default` sets no row background and a gate written against it would have been green while the reported screen was striped. **Closing it restores every cell exactly**, symbol and style, which is the other half of *it moves nothing* and is gated as such.

**The close control takes B10's three rungs**, the same ladder the scrollbars' step buttons use: the chrome's weight at rest, `bar_hover` under the pointer, `bar_active` while pressed. It shipped in one weight, which left a reader guessing whether the glyph was a control at all (#211). A pointer resting anywhere else on the sheet marks **nothing**, since the sheet swallows what lands on it and a mark on something underneath would say otherwise.

**It is retained state rather than a one-frame draw**, because the pane wakes on filesystem events and an agent's write redraws the frame underneath it. A sheet that lived for one frame would be dismissed at random by somebody else's build, which is the constraint B12 named as likeliest to be missed and which `a_write_under_the_sheet_does_not_dismiss_it` holds.

**It degrades on two axes and trades between them on one rung, and both floors live in the layout rather than in the painter.** The ladder is four rungs in order, and the order is the contract: the roomy rung ([#285](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/285)), then every row in one column, then the two-column rung ([#220](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/220)) that spends width to buy height, then the **paged** rung ([#286](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/286)), which draws as many rows as the body has and puts the rest on the next page. Width picks the spelling for the whole table, wide or tight, because a table whose rows each chose their own width is not a table and a truncated verb has no identifying half to keep. **The height axis no longer drops anything**, which is B13: it splits. **Below the floor `?` still toggles and nothing is drawn**, exactly as `m` behaves on a pane that cannot carry the band, and no gesture is swallowed by a sheet nobody can see. The floors are `Body`'s rather than the drawer's for [#158](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/158)'s reason, paid for once already.

**What still drops is width, and there the drop order is unchanged.** A pane too narrow for the whole table at the tight spelling gives up the mouse group, and then keyboard rows in the drop order, leaving `f`, `m` and `?` last: the unguessable outlives the reflexive, which is the hint bar's own rule one surface over, and `q` goes first because the hint bar spells `q quit` on every frame. Measured over every width from 20 to 144 against every height from 6 to 38, and before the change over 20 to 140 against 3 to 40: **38 columns and up reach all sixteen gestures**, 35 to 37 reach eleven, 32 to 34 reach eight, 30 to 31 reach four, and below 30 no sheet is drawn. Forty is the width the promise is stated at, because forty is I6's. `crates/vigia/tests/sheet.rs::every_gesture_is_reachable_at_forty_columns_and_up` is that promise as a gate, walking `?` from the first page to the close on every pane in the sweep, and `the_display_order_is_the_readers_and_the_narrow_floor_keeps_the_unguessable` pins the three narrow sets by name rather than by count.

**The page counter rides the title bar, and it is what makes the omission stop being silent.** ` a-b of 16 ` between the title and its rule, drawn only when the page is not the whole table, so no screen that fits everything today gains a character. It costs no content row, which is the property B12 chose an overlay for and which a say-so taking a row would have spent on the gestures it was apologising for. The ordinals are positions **within what this pane can reach**, not within the sixteen: a width-dropped set is not contiguous in display order, since `q` goes first and `q` is at the bottom of the table, so a last page reading `9-11 of 16` says *this pane reaches nine of the sixteen* and the reader's arithmetic is the say-so. True positions with gaps would read as a rendering fault rather than as a count. The total is `KEYBOARD.len() + MOUSE.len()` rather than a literal, because [#288](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/288) adds a sixth mouse row and a counter reading *of 16* on a table of seventeen is a lie no gate over the drawn output would catch.

**Every rung charges the counter's widest spelling whether or not it draws one, and that is what turned a guard into a rung.** The floor is the title plus ` 16-16 of 16 ` plus a border, a space and the two-of-gap at each end: **thirty columns**. What the charge buys is that the counter **fits**: the title bar's rule run is `width - title - counter - 5` through a saturating subtraction, so on a rung too narrow for it the rule goes silently to zero and the ordinals run into the close control. It is **not** what keeps the box the same size between pages, which is what this section said until a mutation checked it: `sheet_fields` measures over the whole row set and every page of a pane shares that set, so the width was page-independent already. It was seventeen and no rung reached it. Now the deepest dropping rungs are raised to it and a pane under thirty draws no sheet at all, where it used to draw four gestures of sixteen with nothing saying so. That is the trade B13 states out loud rather than discovering. `crates/vigia/tests/sheet.rs::the_floor_is_a_rung_now_and_the_narrowest_sheets_are_the_sizes_the_ruling_states` holds the three numbers: 30 narrowest anywhere, 38 narrowest at I6's forty and up, 71 narrowest two-column.

**The roomy rung, at the head of the ladder: the same one column with its sections headed and air around them, sixty-eight columns by twenty-nine rows.** Ruled and implemented 2026-08-24 ([#285](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/285)), from the reader's own mock. The sheet was shrink-wrapped to its table at every rung, frame, one space, keys, two of gap, verb, one space, frame, with nothing between the groups but the `mouse` rule, so on a pane with room to spare it still read as a dense block. Here the keys cells stand four columns in, the verbs eight after the keys field, the headings two columns in so they stand back from their own rows, and a blank row sits above and below every section. **The headings are plain text rather than rules**, which is the one departure from every other rung and follows from the air: a rule under a blank row is a second separator doing the first one's job, and reads as a divider rather than as a heading. The sections are `moving` (`j k`, `Space`, `d u`), `files` (`g`, `n p`, the digits, `J K`), `view` (`f`, `m`), `mouse` (the whole mouse table) and `leaving` (`?`, `q`), in that order, and **`leaving` at the bottom is the ruling this rung was blocked on**: quitting is not what a reader reads first. The four keyboard runs tile the table exactly once and in order, which is asserted in a `const` block beside them rather than by a test, because bad bounds are an index panic on the frame path and a build that will not compile beats a suite that goes red. The mouse group sits fourth here and stays last wherever the groups are stacked, because a rung with no headings cannot put two unlabelled keyboard rows after a labelled mouse group. **It takes the wide spelling always**, and that is derivable rather than chosen: air is surplus, `level` is tight exactly when the wide one-column sheet does not fit the room, and this rung is that sheet plus six columns of gap and six of inset. It needs a room of sixty-eight columns, which a pane of seventy leaves, and a body of twenty-nine rows. **Being first is what makes it additive**, the same property the widening rung has by being third: it needs strictly more of both axes than the full one-column rung, so a pane that takes it would have drawn all sixteen gestures anyway and a pane that does not take it is untouched. `crates/vigia/tests/sheet.rs::the_roomy_rung_is_additive_and_costs_no_pane_a_gesture` is that claim as a gate, over the same swept grid, and a heading costing a gesture is what it fails on.
