**Mock A drew it seventy-six columns wide and the shipped rung is sixty-eight, which is this file's own rule applied a second time.** The mock leaves twelve blank columns after its verb field against four before its keys, and that asymmetry is the box the reader drew rather than a table they designed. §11.1 already corrected B12's mockup in exactly this direction, *the widths are the shipped drawer's, not the ruling's estimate*: the fields are derived from the content of the rows a rung draws, and the trailing inset matches the leading one as it does at every other rung. What the mock rules is the **air**, and every one of its numbers for that is kept.

**Display order and drop order are two orders, and they were one until #285.** `KEYBOARD` is what a reader reads and `DROP_ORDER` is what the height ladder gives up, and the second is a ranking rather than a direction through the first. They were the same sentence by construction before, because the ladder dropped a prefix of the one array, so *the rows a reader meets first* and *the rows the ladder gives up first* could not disagree. They are not the same claim: a reader meets `j k` first and the ladder gives up `q` first. Keeping them conflated is what made the sections unbuildable, and it is worth stating as a trap rather than as history, because the failure is silent: reorder the one array for a new section and the keep-set is silently re-ranked, so the pane that loses a gesture is one of the two or three shortest on the whole ladder and nothing else on screen changes. With the reader's order in place, dropping from the top would have kept `q`, which the hint bar names on every frame, and dropped `f` and `m`, which this section names as the unguessable. The drop sequence itself is unchanged from before the reorder. `crates/vigia/src/render.rs`'s `sheet_tables` module holds both ends on the tables and `the_display_order_is_the_readers_and_the_floor_keeps_the_unguessable` holds them on a screen, which are two claims: a drawer free to iterate the table backwards satisfies every assertion over the constants.

**One column while one column fits, and two where the pane is short and wide** ([#220](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/220)). This read *"one column at every width"* until 2026-08-24, and both halves of that reason were checked rather than inherited. *Sixteen rows already fit a twenty-four row pane* is **true and does not reach the case**: the failing pane is any shorter than twenty-four rows, and the one reported was 120 by 20, where the sheet drew eleven gestures of sixteen and dropped the entire mouse group in silence, with sixty of a hundred and twenty columns going to nothing. *A hundred and twelve columns to draw what fifty-six draws* was arithmetic about a different shape, one that charges both columns the widest fields of either; split on the group boundary the mouse group's widest cells are nineteen and twenty-five against the keyboard group's twenty-two and twenty-eight, so the real figure is **104 by 14**, or **71 by 14** at the tight spelling (**76 until [#286](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/286)** shortened two tight mouse verbs so the whole table would fit I6's forty columns in one column: this rung measures the same cells, so it narrowed by the same five). And at the heights where the rung is selected, fifty-six columns does not draw what it draws. *Reads as two unrelated tables instead of one list* was written about two columns of key-and-verb **pairs**; the group split is the one two-column shape without that problem, because the sheet already drew a `mouse` heading at exactly that seam. They were two labelled groups before they were two columns, and both carry a label now, on one rule.

**The rung sits below the full one-column rung and above every dropping rung, which is what makes it additive.** A pane on which one column already fits never reaches it, so nothing that draws every row today changes. It picks its own spelling rather than the pane-wide one, because the pane-wide level was chosen against a one-column sheet, and that is what lets a pane of eighty take the tight two-column rung instead of falling past it into dropping the mouse group entirely. Measured through `render` rather than through the layout alone: **heights 17 through 21 at width 78 and above go from 11 gestures to 16, and no pane outside that block changes**, over the widths 60 to 200 and the heights 14 to 24 that grid covers. A pane outside it is untested rather than known unchanged, which is the same distinction the sweep below draws. **73 since [#286](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/286), and 78 before it, and the first draft of this ruling said 80.** `margin_of` returns 2 from 44 to 78, 3 at 79 and 4 at 80, so a pane of 73 leaves 71 columns of room and the tight two-column rung is exactly 71 wide. Arriving earlier is additive in the direction that matters: the block of panes between 73 and 77 columns drew eleven gestures and now draws sixteen on one page. The probe that produced the before-and-after sampled 60, 70, 80 and stepped over its own boundary, which is a sweep failing the way a superlative does: the falsifying case was inside the claim and outside the sample. `crates/vigia/tests/sheet.rs::the_two_column_rung_arrives_at_the_width_the_ruling_states` walks 70 to 84 a column at a time so that number cannot drift again. The claim that growing a pane in either axis never takes a gesture away is `crates/vigia/tests/sheet.rs::the_widening_ladder_is_monotone_and_never_leaves_the_body`, which walks every width from 40 to 144 against every height from 6 to 38 rather than sampling three sizes. Those bounds are the claim's, not decoration: 40 is I6's floor, 144 is above every width the rung arrives at, and the height ceiling rose with [#285](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/285) because the roomy rung needs a body of twenty-nine, so a pane outside them is untested rather than covered. **It snaps** in both directions, per §5.3's *snap, never ease*. What was still open here is closed by [#286](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/286): the pane that is short **and** narrow has no column trading, and the height axis now pages rather than dropping, so the only content a pane loses is what its **width** cannot spell and the counter says how much that is.

**The old two-region wording follows, and every word of it still holds of the list and the diff.** This is B4, ruled 2026-08-01 as a **layout** question rather than the navigation one it was written as ([#66](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/66)). The proposal that stood here for two phases — *"one continuous scroll, list as map"* — answered *navigable?* and settled *is it a region at all?* underneath it, in the direction the code happened to have taken. `assets/preview.svg` draws the other answer and has since before the question was asked: `src/engine/watch.rs` appears **twice** in it, once at `y=92` in a block of three summary rows and once at `y=202` as the diff heading, with a rule at `y=178` between them, which one stream never does. §5.1's rule that a published artifact answering an open question **is** the answer applies to layout exactly as it applied to `f`. **The rule between them stays bare, ruled 2026-08-15 as §11.2 B11** ([#124](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/124)), and on what a heading *is* rather than on where the reader has scrolled: it is a `Painter::file_row` carrying the glance elements through the drawer both regions share, so folding it into a border line either strips those elements or puts a whole glance row in a rule. B4's own reason settles the rest, since the pinned list is already this repository's answer to a heading scrolling out of sight, and `Body::split`'s `rule: list > 0` makes the two coextensive so that answer is never absent.

**The footer gets a rule over it, and both rules run edge to edge.** Ruled 2026-08-18, reported from a live pane. Every other boundary on this screen is drawn: the list gets a rule over the diff and the masthead gets a blank row either side. The bottom bar had neither, so chrome sat directly under content with nothing saying so. It is the same `─` the list already uses, on a reserved row, which is what the boundary above the list costs too. **Full bleed is the half worth stating**, because it is the half a reader sees: §5.3 rules that furniture runs to both edges, and `Painter::rule`'s own reason is that a rule stopping short reads as a box someone forgot to close. Both marks are drawn from the pane's rect rather than an inset one, so neither takes the margin a text row takes. **It yields on a short pane** like the band and the list before it, since a rule that cost the diff its last row would be chrome announcing a region rather than separating one, which is the argument `Body::split` already makes for the rule under an absent list. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::both_rules_reach_both_edges_of_the_pane` gates the pair, including the two-line footer where a mark placed a fixed row up would land inside the bar rather than above it.

**What it buys is the thing §5 calls the differentiator actually staying on screen.** Three of the four glance elements — sparkline, heat strip, counters — ride a `Row::File` heading, so in one stream they are visible only while that heading is, and scrolling two hundred lines into a file takes every file's glance signal off the screen at once. That is the inverse of what a monitor is for: §2 buys *readable at a glance*, and a glance surface that survives only at scroll position zero is a glance surface that is absent whenever anyone is actually reading.

**The list stays not navigable, which is the half B4 proposed and which stands.** There is no selection, no focus and no second mode: `▸` marks the file the diff is inside and is not a cursor, and no key changes meaning depending on where anything is. B4's own rationale is the reason — *selection implies focus, focus implies a second mode, and modes are reviewer-class* — and it is honoured here rather than overridden. The region is a **map**, which is what "list as map" wanted and what a stream of headings could not supply, because §11.2's phrasing presupposed a list to be a map *of* and there was none, there were headings.

**That row's path is drawn bold, and the weight is the caret's second channel rather than a second mark.** Ruled and implemented 2026-08-16 ([#193](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/193)), because every other durable distinction in this region is carried by weight and the one row a reader most needs to find carried a glyph alone. `Painter::list` resolves the glyph and the weight from **one** predicate, so they name the same row or neither is drawn, which ties the weight to `affords_caret` for free: below seventeen columns the caret is dropped and a bold row with nothing pointing at it would be a weight nobody can attribute. **It does not make the list navigable and does not contradict the paragraph above**: it says which file the diff is *already* in, which is what `▸` has always said, and no key moves it. It shares `BOLD` with the recency ladder's pulse rung, and the two stay separable because each carries a glyph of its own, `●` against `▸`, which is also what survives `NO_COLOR`. The mark is confined to this region by construction rather than by geometry: both regions draw through one `Painter::file_row`, and the stream's call site passes `false` outright, so a heading for the very file the caret marks stays plain. A weight the painter carried would have been confined only by the two regions never sharing a `y`, which is a property an edit to the layout removes silently. `crates/vigia/tests/render.rs::the_file_the_diff_is_inside_is_drawn_bold_beside_its_caret`, `::the_weight_arrives_and_leaves_with_the_caret` and `::a_diff_heading_never_takes_the_current_weight` are the three that fail when any of that stops holding.

**Its height is the changed-file count, capped at a quarter of the pane and never below six, plus the rule.** Three changed files draws three rows, which is the picture exactly; a formatter touching two hundred draws the cap and scrolls. On the 24-row pane this tool is built for the cap is six and it leaves fourteen rows of diff after the header, the rule and a one-line footer, which is what the region has always drawn; a 50-row pane draws twelve. The clamp order is unchanged and is what makes the deepening safe: one row per changed file, then the cap, then whatever leaves the diff `MIN_BODY`.

**The share is the rule and six was its instance, which is a correction to the sentence this paragraph used to carry** ([#160](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/160)). Six was recorded as *"the largest block that still reads as a glance rather than as a list to be searched"*, an absolute, and then justified by arithmetic about **one** pane: 24 less a header, the lead blank, six, the rule and a footer is fourteen exactly. Six is a quarter of twenty-four. So the number was a proportion stated against a single height, and a 50-row pane keeping it drew void where the map could be, which is what [#125](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/125) filed. What reads as a glance is the region's share of the screen rather than its row count: at a quarter the map never dominates, and the diff keeps three quarters at every height. **A rule that reproduces the shipped number at the pane it was chosen against is a rule**, which is the same evidence the glance elements' own two-in-five share was adopted on one region out.

**A rung is worth one row of list per four rows of pane, and the step bound is load bearing rather than a rounding.** The band is paid out of what the list leaves, so a cap that gained two rows for one row of pane would take a band off a pane that had just grown: the "a bigger container holds less" failure the margin ladder is a written-out table to avoid. Stepping by one keeps the rows left after the list non-decreasing, so a taller pane can never draw less of anything. The row a pane gains at a rung boundary goes to the map; the other three go to the diff.

**Read from the pane, never from the body.** The same ruling the inset ladder and the caret's floor already make one axis over. The body is the pane less its header and a footer whose own height depends on the changed-file count, so a cap read off the body would deepen the list because a seventh file appeared, which is exactly the jog the paragraph below forbids.

The height is a function of **pane geometry, follow state and changed-file count**, which is exactly the set `Footer::plan` takes, because the list divides whatever the footer leaves. None of those is transient, and that is the property being bought: the region cannot jog a reader's diff the way a notice or a passing failure would. It does move when the footer does — `f` on a forty-column pane takes a second footer line and the list gives up a row for it — and that is a reader's own keypress changing the shape of the screen, which is the same thing pressing `f` already did before this region existed. It gives way on a short pane the way the footer gives up its second line, and the diff keeps `MIN_BODY` rows before the list gets any.

**The list is ordered the way the stream is**, which is `Frame::files()`, which is status order. That is what makes it a map rather than a second opinion: the caret's place in the list and the thumb's place on the diff's scrollbar then describe the same position. Ordering by diff size was rejected on I4 — it needs every changed file's diff, which is [#49](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/49)'s argument against a repository-wide total arriving in a second place, landing here identically. Ordering by recency from `History` is genuinely free and was rejected for a different reason: it decouples the list from the stream, so the caret stops corresponding to the scroll position and the map stops being one.

**A list row draws exactly what a heading in the stream draws**, through the same `Painter::file_row` and the same degradation ladder. This is the **second deliberate departure from `assets/preview.svg`**, beside the header's worktree name, and it is recorded rather than absorbed: the picture splits the elements across the two regions, giving the summary rows no kind letter and the diff heading no counters. That split stops being defensible the moment the list scrolls, because a file scrolled out of the region would lose its counters altogether and there would be nowhere left to read them.

**A region's file rows draw their glance elements in columns**, which is [#77](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/77) and which the picture had promised since before anything was built. Every element used to come out of a budget that had already lost the elements outside it, so its position was a function of *their* widths on *that* row, and the counts cell is the widest variable thing there. Three sparklines then failed to read as one small-multiples chart, which is the reading a list exists to give. So the ladder runs **once for a region** and every row draws into the slots it produced. Its order is unchanged: counts, then the pulse, then heat, then sparkline.

**A row that has no such fact keeps the column rather than closing it.** This is the half that makes it universal rather than occasional: a file with no line diff has no heat strip, and until [#78](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/78) a file that had not been written since launch had no sparkline either, which at launch is every file. Under right-packing each absence let that row's remaining elements slide into the space and pulled it out of line with its neighbours. The sparkline's half of that is now a **track** rather than a blank; the heat strip's is still a blank, and the paragraph below on the two strips' empty states says why the difference is a ruling rather than an inconsistency.

**The counts cell is two columns and not one field**, `+N` right-anchored in its own and `-M` in its own, one space apart, which is what the picture draws. A single field aligns whichever end it is anchored to and leaves the other ragged, so an eye running down three files' additions gets the same non-comparison this ruling exists to remove.

**Each half is drawn in its own diff colour, and a half with nothing to say is drawn in the row's dim grey.** `+42` green, `-7` red, `-0` grey ([#157](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/157)). It is §5.3's rule rather than the picture's, though the picture draws it too: green and red are lent out only where they restate the same fact, and a zero restates none. Drawing it red anyway would cost the element its whole reading, because a worktree an agent is only adding to would then carry red on every row and answer nothing. **Presence is shared and ink is per half**, which is the one place these two rules pull apart: the pair is drawn or dropped together for the reason the width paragraph below gives, and a renderer that greyed a whole cell because one half was zero would be applying the wrong one of the two.

**The slots come from the pane and never from the rows**, and that is the ruling the first attempt got wrong. Sizing the counts field to the widest count among the rows a region was about to draw holds *within* a window and moves *between* windows: scrolling a list until a file with `+1500 -1500` enters it widens the field by six columns and slides every heat strip and sparkline on every row. Reported from use within an hour of the branch running, which is the sixth time a defect on this surface has been. Intermittent movement is worse than a layout that is simply wrong, because there is nothing on screen to argue with. So every slot is a constant or a rung of the pane's width, and nothing a reader scrolls past can move anything. Measuring over every changed file instead would also be stable, and is forbidden for [#49](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/49)'s reason exactly: it needs every changed file's diff, which puts the frame back in proportion to the size of the diff against I4.

**Each region plans its own, and from forty-three columns up the two answers are the same.** They were entitled to differ and did: the list insets by the caret, which was two columns, so its status sigil sat two columns right of the same sigil on a diff heading and every glance element downstream of the path inherited the offset. Reported from use, and visible in `rows__a_real_repository_draws.snap` for months before anyone read it as a defect ([#173](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/173)). **The caret is one column standing in the pane's own margin now**, so the list charges the row only what the margin cannot lend it: nothing at or above forty-three columns, where `margin_of` keeps a blank there anyway, and one column below it, where the ladder has no floor left to lend from. The two regions still plan separately, because they are still permitted to differ and because `Painter::file_row` must stay one drawer that knows nothing about which region called it. Neither reads a row, so a heading scrolling past cannot move the pinned region above it. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_sigil_sits_in_one_column_in_both_regions` and `::the_path_starts_in_one_column_in_both_regions` sweep both sides of that rung.

**The residual below forty-three is one column and it is a decision, not a tolerance.** Three branches were weighed. Extending the margin ladder down so the caret always has a blank to stand in was refused on [#119](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/119)'s own recorded reason, which is still true when checked rather than inherited: that floor exists to protect I6's forty-column pane, and paying it here would spend a column of *code* at exactly forty, which is the width every column has already been bought and sold over twice in the paragraphs above. Dropping the caret below forty-three buys alignment at every width and costs the narrow pane the one mark saying which file the diff is inside, which trades information for tidiness at the width that can least afford the trade. Keeping the old two columns made the gap between caret and sigil appear at forty-three, vanish, and reappear at seventy-nine as the pane widened. So the caret narrows, takes the margin where there is one, and bills the row a single column where there is not.

**The caret is the one glyph licensed to stand on the pane's own edge**, which is a stated exception to the inset ladder four paragraphs down rather than a hole in it. #173 asks for the marker *"flush against the pane's left edge with no margin of its own"*, and taking that literally is what makes the gap between caret and sigil grow with the pane instead of coming and going: the marker sits at column zero at every width, and what follows it is whatever margin the ladder bought. `assets/preview.svg` keeps a cell to the left of its own caret, on a canvas about a hundred and nine columns wide, and §5.3 already rules that the picture binds the element set and each element's promises rather than glyph-for-glyph fidelity. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_pane_insets_its_text_at_every_rung` carries the exception in the assertion rather than around it: the caret is stripped and everything after it is still measured against the margin, so a list row that lost its inset *and* drew a caret still fails.

**The threshold that drops the caret is the row's own floor, asked as a question rather than kept as a constant.** It was `CARET_WIDTH + BAR_WIDTH + ROW_FLOOR`, a sum written by hand beside a drawer that spelled the same pieces out again, and what the caret costs a row is a function of the width now, so a fixed sum would have been wrong above the ladder's floor and wrong below it in the other direction. `affords_caret` asks whether the row still clears `ROW_FLOOR` after the bar, the inset and the caret's own gutter, reading the same expression the drawing plans against. The floor it works out to is seventeen columns, one narrower than the eighteen the constant named, because the marker bills one column rather than two. Both are far below the forty I6 is named for. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_caret_threshold_is_the_row_floor_it_claims` asserts the comparison rather than the pixels, and in both directions: *never drawn where the row cannot afford it* was already held one file over, and *always drawn where it can be* is what fails when a floor is left overstated after the pieces it sums get cheaper.

**The body opens with one blank row under the header, and it is coextensive with the list.** The header states a fact about the worktree as a whole and the list is a map of it, which are different classes, and the boundary *below* the list already spends a whole rule saying so; this one had neither a rule nor a blank, and was the only boundary on the pane drawn with nothing at all ([#174](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/174)). A blank rather than a rule, because the ask was separation and not a second boundary mark, and because one glyph doing two boundaries of visibly different weight says less than nothing does. It also needs no new character, so it raises no CP437 question one ruling after [#175](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/175) took a name off §10's degradation list. Given the shape of `rule: list > 0` that B11 already leans on: a body with no map has nothing to separate the header from, so the row goes back to the diff. The changed-file count, the notice and follow mode cannot move it, which is this section's own rule against a transient thing jogging a reader's diff.

**When the band is drawn that blank *is* the band's leading air**, so the masthead screen keeps the four rows it always kept between its header and its list and nothing on it moves. `GRAPH_AIR` is the blank below the band alone now, and the band's fit test drops by the same one row the lead was charged, so no pane height gains or loses a band. The cost therefore falls on the masthead-off screen, which is the default since [#204](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/204): one diff row of roughly fifteen on the 24-row pane this tool is built for. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_body_opens_with_one_blank_row_under_the_header` holds the rule and `::the_lead_row_is_the_mastheads_air_when_a_band_is_drawn` holds the claim the design rests on, which no snapshot in this repository states.

**Every slot is reserved whether or not anything can fill it**, including the sparkline in a region where no file has a history yet, which at launch is every file ([#78](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/78)). The alternative was tried: reserving only what some drawn row could fill moves every column on the tick the first file is written, which is precisely the moment a reader is looking at the screen. What it cost when this was written was a row that is mostly blank until the first write, and at forty columns that was most of the row. **That price was paid twice and is now refunded once**: [#78](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/78) fills the reserved sparkline slot with a track instead of leaving it blank, so the slot a reader could not see the point of is the one that now says *no churn in the window*. Reserving the column was the decision that made saying so free.

**And a region is planned against its own width less a scrollbar column, drawn or not.** *Its own width* was *the pane's* until [#252](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/252) and the two were the same number, because every region spanned the pane. Beside a rail they are not: a seventy-column rail planned against a two-hundred column pane would lay a row out for a screen it does not have. What stays the pane's is the *inset's rung*, which the paragraph on the margin ladder rules and which this does not touch. A bar appears when `scrollable` finds the region cannot show all it holds, so its presence is a fact about the **contents**: a seventh changed file, or a diff one row taller than the screen, narrowed the region by two columns and re-planned every row in it. At some widths that slid the elements sideways and at others it crossed a rung and took the counts cell off every row of the list. That is the same defect this section rules out one axis over, and it survived the first fix because a scrollbar does not look like content. §11.1's own caret floor had already ruled the identical hazard the same way, counting the bar's columns on a screen with no bar so the caret cannot appear and vanish with the file count.

**What it costs is the sparkline between 40 and 41 columns**, on top of the 36 to 39 the counts cell took, so the sparkline now needs 42. That is the second time this ruling has been paid for in the same element, and it is worth stating plainly rather than burying: at exactly the width I6 is named for, a pane shows the heat strip and the counts and no sparkline. The alternative is not "a sparkline at forty" but "a sparkline at forty until a seventh file changes", which is the intermittent layout this whole section exists to refuse, and an intermittent view is worse than a smaller one because there is nothing on screen to argue with. The two blank columns on a pane with nothing to scroll are the visible price, and they are the same price the reserved-slot ruling above pays for the slots a row can still fail to fill, which since #78 are the heat strip's, the pulse's and the counts cell's: the sparkline's is the one that stopped being among them.

**A row's band ends at the scrollbar's own column, not two short of it.** Ruled 2026-08-17 ([#214](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/214)), reported from a pane where the columns beside the bar read as neither wash nor track. `BAR_WIDTH` is `reserved(1)`, a bar column and a blank one in front of it, and the wash used to stop before **both**. On a context row that is invisible, because the whole row is pane background; on a changed row it is one column of pane background between the band and the bar, so the band reads as clipped and the bar as standing in a notch. **The reserve is about glyph adjacency and survives intact**: it exists so a full-block thumb never sits flush against a count and reads as `-6█`, and a blank cell whose background matches the band is still a blank cell, so nothing becomes adjacent to anything it was not before. The wash therefore spans the content region **plus** that reserved column and stops at the bar's own. `crates/vigia/tests/render.rs::a_wash_stops_before_the_scrollbar_column` holds both halves, and it held the old ruling correctly, which is why this is a change to the ruling rather than a fix to the gate. **Superseded 2026-08-18 by [#239](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/239), which is this ruling finished rather than reversed: the band spans the bar's own column too, and the bar draws over it.** The sentence above stopped one column short of its own reason. *One column of pane background between the band and the bar* described the reserve, and the bar's own cell was the next one along and identically pane-coloured, so the notch this ruling removed was one column narrower and still running the height of the diff. Reported from a real pane at 176 by 87 and probed rather than argued: every row's bar cell read `Reset` while its neighbours carried the wash. §5.3's furniture rule says the same thing from the other side, and `assets/preview.svg` draws every washed row `x="8" width="884"`, the pane exactly, with the scrollbar rects inside that span painting over it. **The reserve survives untouched a second time**, for the reason it survived the first: it is about glyph adjacency, not background. **It costs one line and a colour, and the colour is the half worth recording.** `Buffer::set_style` merges, so a wash carrying a background and no foreground repaints the bar cell and leaves the track's glyph and colour where they were: the band ends up *behind* the bar rather than instead of it, with no reordering. But every ratio in `theme.rs` was measured against the pane, and `bar_track` at `#57606a` falls to **1.88:1** on an added row, where that file calls `#21262d` at 1.24:1 absent and chose 2.96:1 to escape it. So the track moves with the band, to `#656c76` on dark (3.57 / 2.27 / 2.61 against pane, added and removed), which is a grey this palette did not hold: the two constraints together, a 2.0:1 floor on the added row and the thumb ahead by half again on that same row, bound the value to roughly 2.0 and 2.61 there, and nothing already in the palette sits inside that window. **Light is the opposite case and moves to `#7d8590` (3.73 / 2.95 / 2.77), which is `spark_track`'s own value there**, so that palette invents nothing, which keeps the thumb ahead by a uniform 1.72x and 1.64x where the separation used to be 2.08x on a context row and undefined on a washed one. `heat_track` is deliberately unmoved: the heat strip never draws on a washed row. **And the bar draws after the content rather than before it, which is the half this ruling got wrong first.** The band was widened on the reading that `Buffer::set_style` merges, so a wash carrying a background and no foreground would repaint the bar's cell and leave its glyph and colour alone. That is true of `fg` and `bg` and false of **modifiers**: `Cell::set_style` inserts `add_modifier` gated on nothing, and `VIGIA_THEME` accepts a trailing modifier word on every key `Theme::KEYS` names, row washes included. So `removed_row = on #45222a reverse` put `REVERSED` on the thumb and swapped the two colours every contrast gate exists to prove, reachable by configuration and impossible before this change. Found by this ruling's own audit, on the change that introduced it. `Painter::bar_cell` now assigns rather than merges: the band's background is kept and the bar owns the symbol, the foreground and the modifier, which makes the guarantee structural instead of resting on every palette declining to author a field. The gate is renamed to `a_wash_runs_under_the_scrollbar_column` and inverted, which is the same sentence this paragraph already contains, and `crates/vigia/tests/palette.rs::a_bar_track_is_visible_on_every_row_it_crosses` is the new half: the old palette gate measured all three tracks against the pane and structurally could not see a track that had stopped being drawn on one. **Five gates hold this between them and the last three are the ones a reader will want**, because they cover what the first two could not see: `render.rs::every_row_of_the_bar_carries_its_own_rows_background` sweeps every row of the bar's column at both bar shapes, the step buttons included, where the gate above reads one cell of one shape; `render.rs::a_row_washs_modifier_never_reaches_the_scrollbar` holds the configuration-reachable regression this ruling introduced and then fixed; and `palette.rs::every_bar_style_says_what_bar_cell_reads` holds the other half of that bargain, that a bar style must carry a foreground and must not carry a background, on all three palettes including `ansi`.

**And the pane stands back from its own edge, on a ladder of its own.** `assets/preview.svg` draws its furniture to the window edge and its glyphs one to three cells inside it: the row washes span `x=8 width=884` and the region rule runs `x1=8` to `x2=892`, which is the window exactly, while the text proper begins at `x=32`. The shell drew everything from column 0, and by §5.1's own law that made it a fourth undocumented departure from the picture rather than a choice. Ruled 2026-08-10 towards the picture ([#119](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/119)): **one cell a side from 44 columns, two from 80, and nothing at all below 43.** The floor is I6's, not a rounding, since the sparkline has been bought and sold twice in the paragraphs above over two columns at exactly forty. **Forty-three rather than forty-four is the odd rung and not a slip:** the total climbs one column at a time for the reason the next paragraph gives, so the first of the pair lands a width before the second. What the floor protects is the forty-column pane, three columns clear either way. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_pane_insets_its_text_at_every_rung` sweeps the drawn column at every width, and `the_inset_never_reaches_the_forty_column_pane` is the floor. **The caret is the one exception and is stated as such above** ([#173](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/173)): it stands on column zero at every width, and the margin this ladder buys is what separates it from the sigil rather than what it stands inside. That is a licensed departure from the picture, which puts its own caret a cell in, and the sweep carries it in the assertion so a row cannot lose its inset under cover of drawing a marker.

**The margin is a ladder of the *total*, split between the two sides, and that is a ruling rather than arithmetic.** A per-side ladder steps both sides on the same column, so it spends two columns for the one a widening pane just gained, and the content width goes *down* where it should go up. That is what the layout table above is written out to refuse, one element further out, and it is not a hypothetical: taking both sides at once grew the footer to two rows at 44 and at 80, spending a body row on a **wider** pane, and walked the header's worktree name from marked back to whole as the pane *narrowed* across 80. Both were caught by gates that already existed. So the total climbs by one per rung and is split with the odd column going left, which leaves the pane square at every width except 43 and 79. The left is the side that goes first because a reader registers it as the edge of the page, and because the regions already take their left from this ladder while their right is the scrollbar's.

**The inset is charged to a region on its left alone**, and that is what makes it affordable. A glance row already stops two columns short of the pane's right edge for the bar, drawn or not, so the columns a right-hand margin would want are standing there; `planning_width` spends them once. A row therefore loses its rung's width in path and never twice it, and at 80 columns the pane's two margins come out equal. This holds only while no rung's **right half** exceeds the bar's reserve (the rungs count the whole margin across both sides, so the widest is four and its half is two), and `the_inset_never_outgrows_the_scrollbars_reserve` is what fails if one ever does, because a wider half would reinstate the trailing reserve the paragraph below refuses and needs that ruling re-decided rather than silently worked around.

**The diff's content rows are the one class this does not describe, and they get their own rule.** A hunk header, a note and a content line keep the *region's* right edge rather than the pane's, which is older than the margin and is why they move by two columns when a diff outgrows its pane and takes a scrollbar. The margin does not compound with that: a content row stops at whichever of the region's own edge and the pane-less-margin is already further in, so wherever a bar is drawn it lands exactly where the heading above it lands, and where none is drawn it stands back by the margin like the chrome. Charging both put a content line two columns left of its own heading on precisely the screens where the diff was long, which is the contents deciding the layout that this section refuses everywhere else. `a_diff_outgrowing_its_pane_does_not_move_the_content_rows_edge` gates the agreement, and `the_pane_keeps_its_trailing_margin_with_nothing_to_scroll` gates the other screen, because the first draws only barred panes and the margin term is unreachable from it. Giving these rows the bar's reserve unconditionally would make them agree at every width and is deliberately **not** done here: it costs every content line two columns at forty, which is I6's question and not this one's.

**So a residual survives, and it is stated rather than left to be rediscovered.** The movement is the scrollbar's reserve less the trailing margin: two columns below forty-four, one from forty-four to seventy-nine, and none from eighty up. Forty-three is not in the improved band, because its rung is the odd one and spends its single column on the left, so its trailing margin is zero and it behaves as before. Reduced and bounded is what this issue buys; removing it outright is the I6 trade above.

**`r` puts the pinned list beside the diff as a left rail, from a hundred and thirty-four columns up, and the strip above the diff is what ships.** The layout is [#252](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/252)'s, ruled and implemented 2026-08-23; **the gesture is §11.2 B14's**, ruled 2026-08-24 ([#295](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/295)), and until then the rail arrived on its own. Below 134 `r` changes nothing drawn and eats no gesture, exactly as `m` behaves on a pane that cannot carry the band, and the request is **kept** rather than cleared, so a reader who narrows a railed pane and widens it again gets their rail back instead of the question. `Chrome::rail` is what was asked for and `Body::rail` is what the pane could give, which is `Chrome::masthead` and `Body::graph` one region over. On a wide pane a path ends near column 40 and its glance cluster is pinned to the right edge up to 150 cells away, so the columnar slots [#77](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/77) bought keep rows comparable *down* the list while the void destroys the association *across* one, which is the thing a reader is actually looking for. Stretching one column does not fix it. Beside a rail the cluster sits against its own path, the diff takes the remaining width as its own region, and the two share every row of the body.

**The arrival width is derived and there is only one of it, at the block rung.** Both regions read the layout table above, so splitting a pane costs each half the width the whole had, and a split costs **no rung** only where both halves and the undivided pane one column below sit on the same plateau of that table. The settled ladder's plateau runs from 54 to 129 planning columns, which ends at a pane of 133; the only other plateau is the table's top rung, which needs 160 planning columns in each half and therefore a pane of 328. So the rail is offered from **134**, the first width at which the stacked list would have left the settled ladder and spent its new columns on a twenty-four slice heat strip. **Offered rather than arrives, since B14**: the derivation decides where the gesture can be honoured, and it is untouched by the gesture existing. Spending them on adjacency instead is the swap. One column later and no rail narrower than 134 columns could have matched the wider strip, so every later arrival costs a rung in **both** regions rather than in neither. `tests/rail.rs::the_rail_arrives_where_the_stacked_list_would_have_climbed` re-derives the number from a drawn screen rather than restating it.

**What the split costs is the diff's content column, and that is the feature rather than a defect.** At 133 the diff plans against 129 columns and at 134 against 60. A rail at *any* width narrows the diff, because there is nowhere else for its columns to come from, and the choice was between narrowing it here and halving the heat strip in both regions at a later width. Stated with the number so it is met in this file rather than on a pane. The two columns are within a few of each other at 134, 70 against 64, and the diff is the wider from 141 up.

**The rail is 70 columns until the pane reaches 213, and a third of it after that.** The floor is composed from what it is a floor of: the scrollbar's reserve, the pane's leading inset, the kind letter every file row opens with, twice `MIN_PATH_WIDTH` of path, and the settled glance cluster the picture draws. Twice the floor rather than the floor, because at exactly twelve columns a path elides to its bare tail and a rail whose paths have lost the directory they are in has given up half of what it is for. The share is the smaller half of the pane on purpose: below 213 columns every column the pane gains goes to the code, which is what a monitor of a diff should spend a wide pane on, and above it the rail earns width the way §5.3 rules richness is earned. A path longer than the budget still elides to its tail, which is the same rule every label on this pane already follows.

**And the derivation is the block rung's, which is a limit rather than a caveat.** `Columns::plan` takes the glyph rung, a dense cell draws two buckets per column, and the same layout table is therefore reached at different widths: on a terminal carrying braille or octants the stacked ladder climbs at a pane of **119** and is already past the settled rung by 133, so crossing into the rail costs twenty-four heat slices for twelve, in both regions. No arrival width is loss-free at every rung, and that is arithmetic rather than a choice: the braille plateau that would allow one is empty, because the rail and the diff each need 48 planning columns and the stacked ladder has climbed by 119, and the next plateau is the ladder's top at a 268-column pane. [#284](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/284) is the row for an arrival that knows its rung; it needs the glyph rung to reach the layout, which [#159](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/159) settled is a property of the terminal rather than of a frame. What holds at **every** rung is the floor, and it is §5.1's own: no glance element is taken away, and neither region falls below the complement `assets/preview.svg` draws at that rung, which is twelve slices and is where a dense terminal lands. `tests/rail.rs::crossing_into_the_rail_keeps_the_pictured_complement_at_every_rung` sweeps all three and pins how many rungs fall, so a ladder that moves reddens this paragraph rather than outliving it.

**The rail draws the pictured glance complement below a 402-column pane, and climbs at it.** The table's share clamp needs 130 planning columns for the next rung up, which the rail's third of the pane reaches at exactly 402. That is a boundary rather than a ceiling, and it is stated as a number because the sentence it replaced (*"does not climb at any pane anyone runs"*) is one no sweep can fail: the width where it stops being true is simply outside the sweep. Below it, twelve slices and twelve buckets is exactly what `assets/preview.svg` draws, so §5.1's picture binds the rail as well as the strip; at it the rail has earned more, which is what §5.3 rules space buys. `tests/rail.rs::the_rail_grows_with_the_pane_and_keeps_the_pictured_complement` sweeps past the boundary and pins it in both directions.

**Beside a rail the list's height cap does not apply.** [#160](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/160) made the cap a share of the pane because the list's rows come out of the diff's; in this layout they do not, so the region takes every row under the band and a forty-row pane draws thirty-six changed files where the stacked layout draws ten. Both at the masthead setting that ships, which is off ([#204](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/204)); with the band drawn the rail's figure is thirty-three and the stacked one does not move, because a capped list never pays for the band. Each visible row still costs one `Frame::diff` and no more, which is the I4 claim `tests/reads.rs` already owns, and the deeper region was measured against I9 rather than assumed: a frame on a 200x40 pane drawing 36 pinned rows is **0.71ms p50 and 0.79ms p99 against the 16ms budget**, worst of three rounds interleaved with the 80x24 screen every other budget is taken on, which read 0.99ms and 1.36ms. The deeper region is not the more expensive one.

**The band is the one thing both regions pay for, and that is an exception to the clamp order above rather than an oversight.** The rule is that the band gives way to both and is paid out of what is left rather than out of either, which the stacked layout keeps by sizing the list before the band is considered at all. Beside a rail it cannot: the band spans the pane above both columns, so its three rows are unavailable to the map as well as to the diff. The cost is one step, at the height the band arrives: at 134 columns the rail draws fourteen files at eighteen rows of pane and twelve at nineteen. The **diff** has always taken exactly that step at exactly that moment, and `GRAPH_KEEP` is the floor that bounds it; what is new is that the map takes it too. So the rail is monotone in pane height with the masthead off, and with it on falls only where the band arrives and only by the band's own rows, which `tests/rail.rs::the_rail_is_monotone_in_pane_height` asserts in both halves.

**The horizontal rule is dissolved and no vertical one replaces it.** §11.2 B11 ruled the rule between the regions stays bare, and `Body::split` makes the rule and the list coextensive; there is no boundary here for a horizontal rule to sit on, which is the outcome B11's own body and [#125](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/125) both name. A vertical rule was not added: the rail's scrollbar reserve and the diff's leading inset already leave the boundary blank, and a rule carrying no fact is chrome announcing a region, which is B11's argument one layout over rather than a new one.

**Three rulings the vertical layout never had to make.** The **wash** follows the region and not the screen, which §5.3 now carries as a law. The **margin** keeps its rung from the pane and is charged to each region's leading edge, so the pane stands back from its own two edges and the interior boundary gets the same inset: `margin_of` stays decided from the pane and never from a region, which is the ruling four paragraphs up. And the **caret** is afforded from the region rather than the pane, because the question it asks is whether *the row* still names its file after the marker has taken its column, and a 210-column pane says nothing about a 70-column rail. It still stands on the pane's own leading column, which in this layout is the rail's, so the licensed-glyph exception is untouched.

**The counts cell is two fixed halves of five columns**, `+9999` and `-9999`, with magnitude giving way to a shorter form past that rather than to more digits: `+10k`, `+999k`, `+1M`, `+4G`. Five is the narrowest width at which that abbreviation is **total**: a narrower rung was tried and drew `+0k` for a 250-line change, because two characters have no truthful form for it and the search fell through to the thousands unit. It cost the sparkline between 36 and 39 columns to fix, which is the honest trade against a number that says none when it means two hundred and fifty. That is the rule the status bar's frame and memory cells already follow with `>1s` and `>1GiB`. A field wide enough for `+4294967295` would spend eleven columns a row forever on a number no file reaches. **The halves do not narrow**, and they are the only thing on the row that does not: a narrower cell is what drew `+0k`, so the width is the correctness rather than a preference. They go together or not at all, because `+42` with no `-7` beside it reads as a total rather than as half a pair, and below the width that holds the pair the row draws its path alone.

**A rename's label is a rung, not a token to be cut.** A row draws `new ← old` whole or it draws the new path alone; it never elides the pair. Every other label on screen loses its *head* when it does not fit, because a path's tail is what identifies the file, and that premise is false of a pair: cutting the head of `new ← old` leaves a tail belonging to `old`, so the row names the file the rename came **from** and never the one it is about. That is worse than a truncation, because nothing on screen says it happened. The rule was latent while the row right-packed and left the path more room, and ordinary once the columns above took it.

**The layouts are written out as a table rather than allocated element by element**, and that is what makes narrowing monotone. Allocating greedily in priority order is what the rest of this document's ladders do, and for a *shared* layout it oscillates: measured across every width, the row lost its sparkline at 37 columns, got it back at 40 and lost both glance elements at 41, because each element took the widest rung it could afford and starved whatever came after. Those three widths were measured while the counts cell still had a narrow rung, so they do not reproduce against today's table; they are kept as the evidence that retired greedy allocation, not as a claim about the current one. Widening a pane must never take something away. Each row of the table gives up exactly one thing against the row above it and gains nothing, so the whole ladder is monotone by construction rather than by argument. The drop order, narrowing: the sparkline's resolution, then the strip's, then the sparkline, then the strip, then the pulse, and the counts last, because they are the row's content rather than a signal drawn beside it and because the cell no longer has a narrow rung to give up first. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_glance_columns_collapse_in_one_order` asserts the monotonicity as a rule and pins the walk with the width each state begins at, because a rule alone cannot see a boundary that moved.

**The table answers two questions, and until 2026-08-18 it only ever asked the first** ([#161](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/161)). Below the widest layout that shipped, the question is *what survives* a narrowing pane, and the twelve-column path floor decides it. Above it the question is *what a wide pane is worth spending*, and there "does it fit" is the wrong test, because a fixed-sum table takes a rung the instant it fits. The widest heat rung fits inside **69** columns, which is narrower than the 109-column render `assets/preview.svg` is measured from, so a rung added on the fit test alone would have made the published picture false while every gate stayed green. **So a rung above the settled ladder is taken only when the glance elements fit inside two columns in five of the row.** That puts the 24-slice strip at **134** columns and, once the projection can carry it, a 48-slice strip at **194**: the two widths #161 asked for, from a rule derived from neither, which is the check that it is a rule and not a number fitted to the constraint. Half the row was the other candidate and clears the picture by a single column, which is a coincidence rather than a margin. The clamp is floored at the settled ladder's own widest layout, so **no boundary that shipped can move** whatever the share is set to or however many rungs are added above, which is a property of the mechanism rather than of the sweep that checks it. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_widest_strip_waits_until_the_path_keeps_the_row` derives the boundary from the layout's own slots rather than reading it off a failure, and asserts that merely fitting and deserving are different widths with the picture's between them.

**The drop order is the sparkline's resolution first and then the strip's, all the way down, and it took two corrections to get back there** ([#234](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/234)). [#161](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/161) amended the top step to the *strip's* resolution, because the strip was the element that gained a rung and the sparkline had none above its widest to give up. The reason for that was the churn band's fixed `GRAPH_PERIOD`: a drawn bucket had to stay coarser than a band column or the two elements would draw one store at crossed scales, which made twelve the largest division of the window available. [#232](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/232) retired the band's fixed period, so it is finer than a drawn bucket at every width and cannot be coarser, and the bound went with it rather than being replaced. That left a conclusion outliving its stated basis, which is the shape `CLAUDE.md` names, so the question was reopened as #234 rather than given a second reason for the same answer. **#234 gave the sparkline its rung**, twenty-four source buckets halving to twelve and six, so the two elements alternate from the top of the table to the bottom and the amendment retires with the reason for it. What replaced the retired bound is the **window**, which every rung now covers; §11.1's layout rule carries it.

**The ladder lost its top rung on 2026-08-03 and no boundary moved with it.** It used to open by dropping the pulse **label**, which is the rung §5.1 has now retired everywhere; the pulse is one column at every width it is drawn at all, so the table was six steps rather than seven and the widest layout the one that used to be second, until [#161](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/161) added a rung above it. Every boundary below it is unchanged, because a layout's width is the sum of its own slots and none of the surviving six changed: what went is a fourteen-column rung that only existed above sixty-five columns, and the walk `the_glance_columns_collapse_in_one_order` pins is identical. That is the check worth stating rather than the arithmetic. **A rung removed from the top of a monotone ladder can only be free**, and a rung removed from anywhere else cannot be, so this is the one edit to that table that does not have to re-derive every width under it.

**Every width in the paragraphs above is the block rung's**, and that is a qualification rather than a correction: they were measured when there was only one rung, and they are still exactly what a terminal drawing eighth-blocks does. At a dense rung the same layout table is reached earlier, because a slot's cost is `spark_cells` of its bucket count rather than the count itself, so the sentence about forty columns showing no sparkline is true of blocks and false of braille. The *ladder* is unchanged; only the price of one of its slots moves.

**The sparkline has a glyph rung above the block ramp, and it buys width rather than height.** Ruled and implemented 2026-08-17 ([#159](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/159)). The eighth-blocks are one bucket per cell at eight levels; braille and octants are a 2 by 4 grid, so they draw **two** buckets per cell and a window costing twelve columns costs six. That is the whole gain, and it lands where this ladder already hurts most: the sparkline's resolution is the first luxury narrowing drops, so at the dense rung a row keeps its full twelve-bucket window **one rung, six columns**, further down than it does on blocks. Counted from `ROW_LAYOUTS` rather than estimated: the widest layout costs 40 budget columns on blocks and 34 at a dense rung, and 34 is exactly what the block ladder's *second* layout costs, so across that band braille still draws twelve buckets where blocks have halved to six. **It is not free, and both costs are stated here rather than left to be discovered.** A cell carries one style, so the pair of buckets sharing it also shares a colour band and takes the **busier** of the two, which never understates activity but does merge two stops into one. And the bottom dot row is spent on the **baseline**, so the ramp is three levels rather than four: the sparkline's track may not be the ramp's own floor, and inside a shared cell a per-column track has nowhere else to live. A column therefore draws one dot when nothing happened and four when it is full, which is the same separation by height that `_` buys under the block ramp and the same ruling ([#78](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/78)) reaching a second glyph. **The churn band joined on 2026-08-18, left on 2026-08-22, and is back the same day**, which is [#244](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/244) twice. It joined ([#232](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/232)) because once it drew one value per sub-column there was *"a great deal of unshown horizontal detail for a denser glyph to reveal"*, and a 2x4 cell holds two samples for exactly that.

**The removal is reversed, and the reason it was made is the reason it is reversed.** #244 rested on a live report quoted as *"the masthead read as scattered dots"*. That is not what was reported. The reader's words:

> scattered was never about scattered dots, was scattered waves, and those waves had become spikes, single lines going upwards instead of something gradually going up and down

> I could have the same spikes with blocks.

Scattered dots are glyph **texture**, and texture points at a rung. Waves becoming spikes are the signal's **shape**, and shape points at the denominator. That denominator is [#256](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/256), which reached the same conclusion from the code three days before #244 shipped and disclaimed it by name: *"it would still bite at the block rung with eight levels to spend."* So the band follows the rung the pane detects, as it did before, and `VIGIA_GLYPHS` reaches it again.

**The ruling is the reader's and it is not conditional on a measurement.** Blocks are more faithful and that is measured rather than disputed: mean absolute error between the drawn column and the true level, over five series at ten widths from 36 to 124 on a fixed denominator, is 1.2% to 4.0% for blocks against 4.3% to 8.8% for a dense cell. Removing a reader-facing rung is a product decision, it was taken without being asked for, and a reconstruction-error table is not a licence to keep it. What the measurement decides is the **shape** the rung returns in, never whether it returns.

**What the same measurement did open.** The figures #244 removed the rung on were taken at 80 and 109 columns, where the band asks for more sub-columns than the window holds samples and the dense rung therefore *repeats*: 95% and 97% of cells drew two identical halves. Below 60 columns it repeats nothing, and measured at 40 the dense rung's twin rate is 0% on a bursty trace and 45% on a wave, so the second sub-column carries genuinely different values there and the horizontal gain is real. What costs it is six vertical levels against sixteen. That is an argument about how the 2x4 cell is *spent*, and #244's own open question is the live end of it: four dot rows exist, three are used, and the fourth is the baseline. Whether a level still needs a drawn axis, now that a quiet stretch is a low level rather than an absent one, would return a rung and is unanswered.

**So the band draws at the block rung whatever the pane detects, and the sparkline keeps the ladder.** Three measurements, because a ruling that reverses a ruling should carry them. Across widths 40 to 124 on a wave, blocks draw **16 of a possible 17** distinct column heights and a dense rung draws **7 of its 7**, saturated because seven is all a 2x4 cell has over two rows. What the second sub-column buys in exchange is nothing a reader can see: **98% of dense cells draw two identical halves** at 80 and 109 columns, because the band asks for `width * density` slots against a 120-sample window and therefore *repeats* above width 60. And the sparkline is the opposite case rather than the same one: its rung is capped at the window's own 24 buckets, five seconds apart against a six-second kernel, so **0 of 12 pairs are identical** where the band's sub-columns at 109 columns sit 0.55s apart. The two elements spend density in different currencies, which is why one keeps it and one does not.

**Spending the cell's fourth dot row instead is closed by arithmetic rather than declined by taste**: four levels over two rows is eight against the block ramp's sixteen, so it does not close the gap, and the row it would take is the axis [#78](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/78) requires. `Glyphs::BAND` is where the ruling lives, so the band cannot half-follow it, and `the_band_draws_at_the_block_rung_whatever_the_pane_detects` and `the_bands_heights_are_the_block_rungs_and_not_a_dense_cells` are the two halves of the gate: which graph, and at what resolution. **The original reason, which is what now holds again, follows.** At two rows a 2 by 4 glyph *halves* its vertical resolution, and [#198](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/198) already sums every stored sample onto the width it has, so there is no unshown horizontal detail for a denser glyph to reveal. It joins only if it ever grows past two rows.

**There is no trailing reserve beyond the scrollbar column ruled above.** The picture leaves about a sixth of the row blank on its right, and *that* is an artifact of a fixed canvas at a width where nothing degrades rather than a promise; spending it at forty columns is what I6 forbids. The two columns a row does stop short of the pane's edge are the bar's, paid whether or not a bar is drawn, and they are the price of the ruling above rather than a margin. **#119 does not change this and is the reason it is worth re-reading:** the pane's inset is a *leading* margin, and the reason it costs a glance row one column per rung instead of two is precisely that this ruling stands and the trailing columns stay the bar's. A reader who takes the two blank columns on the right of a wide pane for the inset's other half has the arithmetic backwards, and a future rung wide enough to need columns of its own would be reinstating the reserve this sentence refuses. The claim that *is* load bearing is the other one, and it holds: the columns are decided before the path is drawn and the path takes what is left, so scrolling a long path into view cannot move them.

**A visible list row costs one `Frame::diff` and no more**, which is what keeps the region inside I4 rather than beside it. The bound is the region's own height, never the changed-file count, so the cost follows the window exactly as the body's does. That is an I4 claim rather than a new invariant, and `crates/vigia/tests/reads.rs` is where it is gated, **beside a rail as well as under a stacked list since [#252](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/252)**, alongside the ones that already bound the body.

**What that row costs depends on which side of the settle margin the file is on, and both halves are worth saying.** Settled, it is a `stat` and a cache hit reading **zero bytes**, which is I2a doing its job and is the state a monitor sits in for almost all of its life. *Inside* the margin it is a real read, because `Frame::diff` cannot prove a file written in the last two seconds unchanged and re-reads it by design. So a six-row region on a tree being actively written costs six reads a frame rather than six `stat`s, and the earlier claim here that the region's doubled ask was "a count that doubles and not work that does" was true only of the settled half.

**The two regions therefore hand entries to each other rather than asking twice.** The file an agent has just written is the one file the margin is certain to cover, it is always the file the diff is inside, and it is always in the window while the list follows, so asking the frame for it a second time read and diffed it again on **every frame this tool exists for**: 258,790 bytes where 36,970 will do, measured over twenty files of five hundred lines. `View::collect`'s walk now records the entries it builds and `take_list` draws from those, which is the same one-pass rule that section of the code already followed for the diff. `a_screen_a_single_file_fills_reads_that_single_file` gates it as a relation, that the region computes one fewer diff than it asks for, so it survives a change to the region's height.

**The record is taken whether or not the heading fits, and until [#257](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/257) it was not.** The walk built its entry on the row it drew the heading on, so a viewport sitting *inside* a file left that file out of the record and the list asked the frame for it again. The paragraph above was true only because every jump landed on row zero: the followed file's heading was always drawn, so the file the margin is certain to cover was always in the record by luck of where follow put it. The landing rule below spends that luck deliberately, so the entry is now built for every file the walk reaches on a pane that has a list, and drawn for the ones whose heading fits. On a pane too short for a list there is no `take_list` to serve and the undrawn one is not built, which `a_pane_with_no_list_builds_no_entry_it_cannot_draw` gates through `View::recorded`. That counter exists because nothing else can see the difference: building an entry reads nothing, so no `FrameStats` figure moves for it. It costs one `entry_of` for at most one extra file per walk, which is two history probes, a clone, and the heat projection's walk over that file's diff lines. Two on a frame that backs up, because that runs the walk again and records the file it placed each time, measured at exactly two on the gate over a landing above a short tail. Against a `Frame::diff`, which inside the settle margin is a whole-file read allocating a string per line, and outside it a `stat` and a cache hit. So the file being written saves a read, and a settled tree trades a syscall for a walk over lines already in memory.

**The list follows the diff, and `J`/`K` move it without moving anything else.** It slides on its own to keep the caret visible, so the region is correct untouched the way I5 requires of everything else. Shift is the modifier because `Ctrl-J` is LF and `Ctrl-C`/`Ctrl-D` already quit, Alt is intercepted by terminal emulators and by macOS Option, and `G` has already taught a reader that case is load bearing here. A plain letter also reaches terminals that never report a modified arrow, so neither binding is the only way in.

**`J` takes the window over, and anything that moves the diff hands it back.** That is the list's own version of follow mode, and it is a mirror rather than an analogy: a manual scroll takes the *diff* away from following and gives the *list* back to it, in the same line of the same function. It was ruled the other way first, as *snap back when the diff lands outside the window*, and that rule is wrong in a way only building it showed: the window can never be away from the current file in the first place, because the frame after `J` drags it straight back, so the keys do nothing. The two halves are both load bearing. Without the takeover `J` is inert; without the handback a reader who browsed once has a map that never agrees with the diff again. **The caret marks the current file whenever the window still shows it, and is simply absent once a browsed window has moved off it.** It is not suppressed by the takeover, and that distinction is worth stating because the opposite reads as the tidier rule: the caret says *the diff is in this file*, which stays true while a reader browses, and hiding it on a window that is showing that file would withhold a fact the reader can act on. What the takeover changes is where the window looks, not what the mark means.

**Scrolling the list does not disengage follow mode**, and that is a ruling rather than an omission. Follow is a claim about the **diff** viewport, and moving a window over a map expresses no intent about what the diff should show — the same reasoning that already exempts a terminal resize one paragraph down. Browsing the changed set while the diff goes on following what an agent is writing is the monitor behaviour, and the two would fight if one disengaged the other. `Action::is_manual_scroll` is an exhaustive match precisely so that a new action has to answer this rather than inheriting a default.

**Both regions carry a scrollbar, and both are exact.** The list's thumb spans the visible window over the changed-file count, both of which are free. The diff's spans the screen's rows over the diff's **total** rows and sits at the rows above it, which is what every other scrollbar means.

**A bar tall enough for one carries a step button at each end, and one click is one step.** Ruled and implemented 2026-08-15 ([#166](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/166)). The buttons come out of the track, so the ladder is a floor rather than a preference: `STEP_FLOOR` is two buttons plus the shortest track that can still express more than one position, which is four rows, and it is written as that sum rather than as `4` so the threshold cannot keep an old total while every gate over the drawn output stays green. Below it the column is byte for byte what it drew before there were buttons, which matters because `MIN_BODY` is 2 and a diff region can legitimately be two rows tall: two buttons in two rows would leave no track, no thumb and nothing to click between them. The floor is per region rather than per pane, decided from the region's own height, and that is what lets one drawer serve a six-row list and a thirty-row diff without either of them being a special case.

**Holding a step button repeats it, and that is a clock this program is allowed to own.** Ruled 2026-08-15, reversing the same day's first ruling, which refused it. The mechanism has to be built rather than asked for: **no mouse protocol reports a button that is still down.** `crossterm` delivers `Down`, `Up`, `Drag` and `Moved`, a drag needs motion, so "the button is held" arrives as nothing at all until the pointer moves or the button is released. Repeat is therefore a timer, and the reason one is admissible here is a distinction I1 does not draw for itself and now does. **I1's budget is *0 wakeups while idle*, measured over a sixty-second idle window, and a held mouse button is not idle.** Every other timer this spec refuses (the header's idle word, the memory readout, the poll loop `lib.rs` rejects, §10's highlight tail) is a clock that would run *while nothing is happening*, which is exactly the state that measure protects. **The pulse decay was in that list until [#243](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/243) and moved to the other side of the distinction this sentence draws**, which is the strongest thing that can be said for the distinction: the window's clock is admitted on the same test that refused this one, because the window empties and the clock stops with it, and the refused version was a wall-clock decay that would have kept ticking against an empty one. This one exists only between a press on a step button and its release, is bounded by the reader's own finger, and cannot outlive it. `Held::wait` is where that stops being an intention: with nothing held it returns `None` and the loop blocks on an **untimed** receive, byte for byte what it did before this existed, and `nothing_held_means_no_timer_at_all` is what fails if that changes. What I1's row gains is the qualifier its first sentence was missing: *never a fixed timer* means never one that runs unbidden, not never one a reader is holding down.

**And I1 carries that as a rule, not as a licence for this control**, which is a correction to how it was first written down. The row said *the one clock this program owns runs only between a press on a scrollbar's step button and its release*, which is an enumerated exception: it names the instance and leaves the next one to be re-litigated even where the argument is identical. Two are already visible. A selection dragged past the edge of its region should scroll ([#177](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/177)), and a press held on the **track** rather than on a button is the page-repeat every desktop scrollbar has; both are clocks bounded by a gesture, and neither is a step button. It also disagreed with the code, which is the thing `CLAUDE.md` rule 1 says to stop on: `Held` repeats whatever action it is armed with and `Action::repeated` is an exhaustive match precisely so a later held control inherits the mechanism. A spec that licensed one control while the code offered a facility was the two describing different programs. I1 now states the three conditions and this paragraph is an application of them.

**500ms before the first repeat, then 50ms, and the numbers are taken rather than chosen.** Qt's `QScrollBar` presses a control with `initialDelay = 500` and runs its repeat at `50`; GTK exposes the same pair as `gtk-timeout-initial` and `gtk-timeout-repeat`, the latter defaulting to 50ms. Two toolkits, one shape, so a reader arriving from any desktop scrollbar finds the cadence already in their hands. The delay is what keeps a single click a single step. **The rate does not accelerate**, which is a ruling and not an omission: a step button is for placing the viewport precisely, acceleration makes precise placing harder by overshooting, and every desktop scrollbar this was measured against holds a constant rate for the same reason. A reader who wants travel has the wheel, `j`/`k`, `d`/`u`, `n`/`p`, the digits, `g`, `G` and the thumb.

**A late repeat folds into one action rather than a backlog, and that is a correctness claim.** `Action::repeated` multiplies a relative step by the number of whole intervals that have actually elapsed, so a tick arriving four intervals late applies `Scroll(5)` through one `apply` and one paint instead of five of each. The rate is then a fact about time rather than about paint speed: a slow pane scrolls the same rows per second as a fast one with coarser granularity, and can never keep moving after the finger lifts, working off a queue. The deadline advances by the steps it took rather than to *now plus one interval*, so a tick handled late cannot push the next one later and walk the rate downwards under load.

**The mechanism is general, not scrollbar-shaped.** `Held` repeats whatever action it was armed with, and `Action::repeated` is an exhaustive match in which every action states how it composes with itself. The ones that return themselves are rulings: a page or half page held down is the reader's own key repeat, and `Top`, `Bottom` and `File` name a destination that does not accumulate. An action added later stops that match compiling and asks.

**A hold ends five ways**, and the third and the fifth are the ones that close a hole rather than restating the obvious: a release of any button; any key press, because a hand that has reached the keyboard is not holding a mouse and `q` must not be answered by a program still stepping; a **pointer move**, since `MouseEventKind::Moved` is motion with no button down and is therefore positive evidence of a release, which is what catches an `Up` that never arrived; a drag off the button, where a twitch inside the same cell keeps it; and **the window losing focus**, which was the fifth and was missing until [#186](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/186), because a release is not the only way a gesture ends and a reader who tabs away with a button down sends no `Up`. That last one is owed to I1's second condition rather than to symmetry: without it the repeat goes on stepping and repainting a pane nobody is looking at, on a clock. Resuming on re-entry is what a desktop scrollbar does and is deliberately not done, because it needs a suspended state `input.rs` has nowhere to put. **A drag onto a button is still inert**: the reader has grabbed the thumb and pulled past the end of the track, the last track row already reaches the last window, and both stepping and clamping would need to know the drag *began* on a button.

**A gesture in progress is lit, and it is one reading in three places.** A step button held down, a thumb being dragged, and the arrows while the keys scroll all take `Theme::bar_active`, brighter than the mark's resting style rather than a different hue, because the element has not changed meaning, only state.
