It matters most where nothing else can answer: pressing *up* at the top of a diff moves no row, and without the lit cell the control is indistinguishable from a decoration. It costs no row, no rect and no wake, since the frames it changes are frames the gesture was already painting. **The arrows answering the keyboard is the one lit thing nobody is touching**, and it earns its place because the arrows are the element whose whole job is *which way*: `j` lights the down arrow, `k` the up one, and magnitude is not direction, so a half page lights what a single row lights. **On one bar, and it took a correction to say so.** The two regions move different things and answer different keys, so the mark carries the region as well as the direction: `j`/`k`/`d`/`u`/`Space`/`g`/`G` and the file steps are the diff's, `J`/`K` are the list's, and a jump or a drag is neither. 0.5.0 shipped a bare direction and lit the matching arrow on both bars at once, while a *drag* was correct throughout because it had carried its region from the start.

**That last one expires and the other two do not**, which is the honest cost of it. A release ends a hold; a key burst simply stops and sends nothing, so without an expiry the last arrow of a burst would stay lit on an idle tree, which is a claim about the past. `SCROLL_LINGER` is 220ms, long enough to bridge the gap between two key repeats and short enough that a reader who stopped does not see one. It is the one number here that is a feel judgement rather than a measurement, and it is stated as one. The clock is bounded by the burst that armed it and fires once, so it meets I1's three conditions like the repeat does, and `input::patience` is where every clock is asked together so that *is there a timer at all* has one answer and one gate. This said *both* clocks through the commit that added the third, which is what a sentence counting the things beside it costs.

**The axis that paragraph turns on is the one §11.2 B10 was reversed on, so it is stated as a rule here rather than left to be noticed.** What decides how a transient mark is cleared is **what the program can still observe about its subject**, and there are three answers rather than two. Its **end**: a press finishes with an `Up`, so the lit button and the dragged thumb need no expiry and get none. Two functions rather than one, and both are free functions for the same reason, that the loop owning the state cannot be driven by a test: `Held::ends` covers a lost release for a step button, and `Grabbed::ends` ends a drag on anything that is not more of the same drag. The second sat inline in `run` from [#183](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/183) until [#186](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/186) added a third mark and made the omission visible. (The repeat clock a *held* button owns is a different clock, bounded by that same `Up` **and by the window losing focus**, which is I1's second condition rather than tidiness: a clock may not outlive the gesture that armed it, and a reader who has tabbed away is not holding anything. Without that bound the loop steps and repaints a pane nobody is looking at, on a timer, which is the state I1's measure protects. `Held::ends` carries it from [#186](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/186), which is what made `FocusLost` arrive on Unix at all. This axis is about expiry, not about whether a timer exists.) Its **replacement**: a hover's subject does not stop, it moves, so the next motion event says where the pointer is now and retires the old mark for free, inside the pane, which is where the ladder below picks up. **Neither**, which is the only case that earns an expiry: a key burst has no end and nothing supersedes it, so the arrows need `SCROLL_LINGER`, and §11.2 B9's footer notice is the same shape one region over, cleared by the next `Wake::Tick` and by nothing else.

**So hover gets no expiry because it does not need one, and it is worth saying out loud that I1 does not refuse it either.** A linger armed by a motion event would meet I1's three conditions exactly as `SCROLL_LINGER` does: it could not start on its own, it would fire once, and the idle path would stay untimed by construction. Reaching for the invariant here would be the cite-without-checking that B10 was reversed for, one page after the reversal. What refuses it is what it would **do**: an expiry puts the mark out while a reader rests on a row, and a reader resting on a row is the one case hover exists for.

**Stated as one rule because B10's decline was read as a rule against pointer-derived marks in general**, under which the lit step button [#166](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/166) shipped hours earlier should not have existed. It should: it was never what B10 refused. The hover case draws on the bars ([#186](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/186)) and not yet in the list ([#189](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/189)), and the rule was stated whole before either, because it is what the other two are instances of and an enumerated list of the marks that exist today is what `RULINGS.md`'s I1 entry records going wrong the last time a ruling here was reversed. **That was worth the order it was done in**: writing the rule first is what made the third case a lookup rather than a design question when the build reached it, and `input::hover_after` is the rule with nothing added.

**A drag keeps the bar it started on, wherever the pointer goes.** Ruled 2026-08-16 after a reader found the opposite: leaving the bar's one column ended the drag. `action_for` asks what is under the pointer, which is the right question for a press and the wrong one once a hand is already moving, and a one-column target is not something a hand stays inside. So a press on a track takes hold of that region and every motion after it resolves against that region alone, with the row **clamped**: pulling above the track holds the first window and below it holds the last, which is what every scrollbar does and what a reader dragging past the end is asking for. A press on a step *button* grabs nothing, because that gesture is already a step.

**A button is chrome, in the bar's own dim style rather than the thumb's.** Shape is what tells one from the track it shares a weight with, so the thumb stays the only lit thing on the column and the readout is unchanged. The glyphs are `▲` and `▼`, which are outside CP437. **The argument this was first ruled on no longer holds, and saying so is the point:** #166 justified the pair by observing that the track they sit on was outside too, so a console losing a button had lost the column's shape already. [#175](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/175) centred the track hours later and moved it *inside*, which dissolves that reasoning entirely. They stay, on the narrower ground that every candidate arrow is outside (`▴ ▾ ↑ ↓` alike, and CP437's own two triangles sit at control-code positions), and a direction is the one thing a shape has to say here. What changed is the size of the loss: it is now two cells on a column that otherwise survives whole, rather than two cells on a column already broken, and that is a stronger case for [#159](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/159) rather than a weaker one for these. §10's Windows bullet carries the list; [#159](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/159) owns the ladder, and this ruling deliberately does not invent one inside the scrollbar.

**The diff's was ruled coarse first, and that ruling was wrong.** It read that a total needs every changed file diffed, which I4 forbids, so the bar approximated the whole from the current file's height. Reported from use within the hour: it vanished on a short file, ballooned on a long one, and never reached the bottom, because the trailing files are rarely the height of the one being read. The argument was right that *diffing* every file is unaffordable and wrong that a total needs it. A height is hunk boundaries and line counts; a `FileDiff` is those plus an owned `String` per drawn line. Counting instead of building took the reference fixture from **442.71ms to 8.76ms**, against `git diff --numstat`'s 46ms for the same work, and §3's I4 now carries the narrowing that admits the walk.

**Keys.**

| Key | Action |
|---|---|
| `q`, `Esc`, `Ctrl-C`, `Ctrl-D` | quit |
| `j`, `↓` | scroll down one row |
| `k`, `↑` | scroll up one row |
| `Space`, `PgDn` | page down |
| `PgUp` | page up |
| `d` | half a page down |
| `u` | half a page up |
| `g`, `Home` | first changed file |
| `G`, `End` | last changed file |
| `n` | next changed file |
| `p` | previous changed file |
| `1`–`6` | put the diff at the Nth listed file |
| `J`, `Shift-↓` | scroll the file list down one row |
| `K`, `Shift-↑` | scroll the file list up one row |
| `f` | engage follow mode, or disengage it |
| mouse wheel | scroll the region under the pointer |
| drag either scrollbar | move that region, until the button comes up, wherever the pointer goes |
| click either scrollbar's end | step that region by one |
| click a listed file | put the diff at that file |
| terminal resize | redraw, no state change |

**The half page is `d` and `u`, and it keeps no overlap row where a whole page does.** They are `less`'s own bindings and the follow-mode paragraph below already claims `less +F` semantics, so the precedent is internal as well as cultural; both letters were free. A page steps `height - 1` so that one row survives the seam and a reader does not lose their place; a half page already leaves half the screen standing, so taking a row off it as well would pay twice for one anchor and leave `d` and `u` out of step with each other. Both directions floor, which is what makes the pair an exact inverse on an odd body as well as an even one. **`Ctrl-D` and `Ctrl-U` are refused rather than overlooked**: `Ctrl-D` is one of four ways out, and rebinding a way out to a scroll is the surprise this map has avoided everywhere else. `D` and `U` are unbound for the reason `F` is, one row above a pair where case is what tells the two apart. [#121](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/121).

**And the half page is not hinted, because at eighty columns the footer is exactly full.** [#121](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/121) proposed `du half` as a bonus rung above the ladder below, on the argument that it would ride at widths with room. There is no such width where it matters: with follow engaged, a position and both readouts, the row is 40 columns of hints, 2, 19 of readouts, 2 and 13 of state, which is the 76 that eighty columns leave after the inset, to the column. A fifth hint is therefore paid for out of the readouts — the frame time would first arrive at 81 columns instead of 69, and the pair at 89 instead of 77 — and `render__the_status_bar_carries_what_a_frame_cost.snap`, the picture whose job is to show this layout is *good* at one width rather than merely legal at every width, would have lost both cells. Refusing it costs little: `Space`, `PgDn` and `PgUp` have never been hinted either, and the bar's four hints are the way out, the state whose absence is invisible, and the two vi-dialect keys for the rows and the region. A half page is a pager reflex of exactly that unhinted family. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::a_wider_hint_bar_cannot_quietly_push_the_readouts_out` pins the two boundaries so the next hint added above the baseline has to argue for what it spends rather than take it quietly. **What stays open is the ruling underneath, and it is not this one**: whether a bonus rung is exempt from the drop order below, which puts advice above instrumentation at every width, or whether being unowed makes it the one thing that yields to a readout. [#147](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/147).

**The file is a granularity, and until [#122](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/122) the map had every other one.** Rows are `j`/`k`, screens are `Space` and `d`, the two ends are `g` and `G`, and not one of them is the unit the pinned list draws, the header counts and the reader thinks in: reaching the next file meant holding `j` through the rest of this one or reaching for the mouse, which already had the gesture. `n` and `p` step one changed file. `1` to `6` put the diff at the Nth row the list is drawing. All of them land on the file's **heading**, which is the resolution a click on a listed file uses, and none of them costs a diff: a heading is row zero, so nothing here asks how tall anything is and I4 never sees it. **A follow jump is the one that no longer shares it**, ruled by [#257](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/257) below: these keys name a *file*, which is the unit the list draws and the header counts, where I5 promises the *change*. The debt is cleared by every gesture `Action::is_manual_scroll` calls one, so a landing armed by follow can never be inherited by one of these. That predicate rather than the position write, because `n` and `p` at either end of the changed set move nothing and still express the intent. **B4 is untouched.** All three move the *diff*, exactly as a click does, and nothing is remembered: no row becomes special, no focus appears, and the event after one means what it would have meant.

**`p` from inside a file goes to the previous file, never to the top of the current one**, and at either end of the changed set the step moves nothing at all. The pager reflex of "this section first" is refused because it would make one key mean two things depending on where the viewport happened to be, which is the rule this whole map is built on, and because `g` is already the key that reaches a top. Clamping the file index rather than refusing the step is refused for a neighbouring reason: it would give `n` at the last file a *backwards* jump to that file's heading and `p` at the first a forwards one, a key undoing what its own letter says. **Follow mode is still disengaged at both ends**, where nothing moved, because on this map follow yields to a reader's intent rather than to whether the arithmetic landed somewhere new. `g` at the top and `G` at the last file already behave that way, and the alternative reading is the one `J` at the last window takes: that one is right there because the state it protects, the map having quietly stopped following, has nothing on screen to announce it, where `follow ▶` leaving the footer is visible.

**The digits address the window, not the changed set.** `3` means the third row the list is drawing, so after browsing with `J` it names a different file than it did before: what you can see is what you can name. Two bounds make that true, and **which one does the work was settled by mutation rather than by argument.** A digit past the **drawn rows** is the ordinary case and is one only a keystroke can reach, because a pointer is bounded by the region and a keyboard is not: a nine-row pane gives the region four rows while `5` and `6` stay under the reader's hand. The **file count** bound then looks redundant, and reading says it is: with the window bound in place, no digit on a freshly drawn screen can name a file past the end, because the window is never taller than what is left below it. What it actually guards is the screen being **out of date**. The row count is what the last frame drew, so between a paint and the keystroke that follows it the changed set can get shorter, which is `git reset --hard`, a branch switch, or the agent in the other pane reverting its own work: six rows were on screen, two files are left, and `6` still passes the window bound because the window is remembered from the frame that drew six. Deleting that bound left every other gate in this file's suite green, which is how the reason above was found to be the wrong one and `crates/vigia/tests/list.rs::a_digit_after_the_diff_shrank_names_no_file` is what now holds it. Both bounds refuse loudly nothing, with no bell and no error. `0` and `7` to `9` are not bound at all, which is a different thing again and deliberately so: an unbound key is no action, where a bound one naming a row that is not drawn is a jump that lands nowhere and spends the reader's follow mode doing it. `N` and `P` are unbound for the reason `D`, `U` and `F` are.

**The digits address the rows every pane drawing a list has, and the reason they stop at six was replaced rather than inherited on 2026-08-20** ([#160](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/160)). It read *a row that can never exist should not spend a reader's follow mode*, and a seventh row exists on any pane of 28 rows or more now that the cap is a share. What survives is the other half, sharpened: six is the deepest list **every** pane draws, so `3` means the same thing at every height, where a key live only above some pane height would be the intermittent affordance this section refuses one region over for the caret's own column. The rows a taller pane adds are addressed by `J`/`K`, `n`/`p` and by clicking, which is bounded by the region rather than by the grammar and therefore reaches every drawn row at every height. `crates/vigia/tests/list.rs::clicking_a_listed_file_sends_the_diff_to_it` runs at 24 rows and at 50 for that reason, and `crates/vigia/tests/input.rs::the_digits_cover_the_settled_cap_and_stop_there` is the other half. That test was called `every_row_the_list_can_draw_has_a_digit` until this ruling, and the rename is the ruling: not every drawn row has one now.

**None of the three is hinted, and the reason is [#121](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/121)'s rather than the one [#122](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/122) gave.** That issue floated `n p files` as a bonus rung and argued the digits needed no hint because "a drawn numbered list *is* the affordance", by analogy with the scrollbar thumb. **The analogy does not hold, because the list draws no numbers**: `rows__a_real_repository_draws.snap` is the region itself, and it draws `▸ A src/added.rs` over `  M src/lib.rs`, a caret column and then the kind letter, with no rank on any row. So the digits are the first binding here with no drawn affordance at all, which the thumb, the list and every hinted key each have in their own way. The conclusion survives anyway and on firmer ground: the eighty-column footer is exactly full, so a fifth hint is paid for out of the frame-time and memory cells whichever hint it is, and `Space`, `PgDn`, `PgUp`, `d` and `u` are an established unhinted family. **This paragraph cited the wrong picture for a day, and the correction is kept rather than absorbed**, because a citation reads as verification whichever region it is of. It named `render__the_glance_elements_at_eighty_columns.snap`, whose fixture `glancing()` carries an empty `list`, so that snapshot has no pinned region in it at all and its `M src/engine/watch.rs` rows are **diff-stream headings**, which take no caret column by the rule two paragraphs up. What read there as the gutter is [#119](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/119)'s pane inset, which is coincidentally the same two blanks at eighty columns and is none at forty. The claim was true and its evidence was of the other region, which is worse than a false claim because nothing downstream of it can tell. What the correction left open was whether the list should draw the rank it is now addressable by, which is [#149](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/149) and is ruled below. [#147](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/147) still owns the rung question underneath.

**The list does not draw the rank the digits address. Ruled 2026-08-14 ([#149](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/149)), and refused on what it says rather than on what it costs.** The cost was nil and measured when the ruling was made: the caret column was two columns wide on every pane at or above the eighteen the old `CARET_FLOOR` named, and blank on every row but the one the diff is inside, so a rank spent no column at any width I6 is concerned with and no frame time on a frame that is already drawing. **That gutter no longer exists** ([#173](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/173)): the caret is one column standing in the pane's own margin, and the list's rows begin where a diff heading's do. The correction is recorded rather than absorbed because it cuts *for* the ruling and a reader should be able to see that it was checked: a rank would now have to buy a column of its own at every width, so the argument this paragraph opens by setting aside has come back and the refusal that follows it does not need it. **The region is a map, and a rank makes it a menu.** B4 keeps the list not navigable because selection implies focus, focus implies a mode, and modes are reviewer-class; the caret is a fact about *state*, that the diff is in this file, where a rank is a fact about the *keymap*, that pressing this goes there. It would be the first thing in the region that is neither content nor location but instruction, drawn on every row permanently, and numbered rows are the file-manager idiom, where a file manager is a surface you operate. `btop`, which §2 names as the class this tool is in, numbers nothing. **A full footer is a reason not to have a fifth hint, not a reason to move the hint somewhere cheaper.** §5.3 puts advice in the footer and [#121](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/121) measured that bar exactly full at eighty columns, so drawing the digit onto a content row relocates advice out of the region this spec meters into one it does not, to dodge a budget — and the gradient is the objection, because every later binding that cannot afford a hint would acquire the same claim on a body column. **And a rank is read**, in the leftmost cell, first in the eye's path and last in what it says about the worktree, which is the §5 glance law that already retired the pulse's label ([#99](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/99)). The unhinted family settles the remainder: `Space`, `PgDn`, `PgUp`, `d` and `u` are undrawn and unhinted, and the digits are the **fourth** way to a file after `j`/`k`, `n`/`p` and a click, so a reader who never finds them loses a shortcut rather than a capability. `J`/`K` is deliberately not in that count, and the near-miss is worth recording: it is the one binding here that moves the *window* and leaves the diff where it was, which `crates/vigia/tests/list.rs::scrolling_the_list_leaves_the_diff_where_it_was` is what holds, so counting it would have cited as a peer the key this spec says reaches no file at all. **The honest cost is that `README.md` is the only place a reader meets them**, exactly as it is for the pager keys, and it is stated here rather than left to be discovered. **The picture needs no change and was never the authority on this**: `assets/preview.svg` draws the caret on its first summary row and nothing in the gutter of the other two, so it already agrees, and §5.1's rule that a published artifact answering an open question *is* the answer does not reach a question the picture predates. The digits landed the day this was ruled; nothing drawn before them can have been answering for them.

**The case for drawing is not discoverability, and it is the one to reopen on.** The digits address the **drawn window**, so which of them are live is a function of pane height and changed-file count, and nothing on screen says so: a rank would carry that fact for free and keep it current, which no footer hint could, since a hint would have to read `1-6 files` and be wrong on every pane drawing fewer than six rows. It loses today on how benign the failure is that it prevents — a digit naming no drawn row refuses silently, moves nothing, and costs only the follow mode, which is already its own open question — and because the measured zero-column cost is not sufficient: §5.3 prices **ink** at zero on the ground that the frames happen anyway, and does not price **attention** at zero, where the glance surface is exactly the place attention is the budget. **A third option is refused rather than left lying around as a compromise**: drawing the rank and the caret together at widths that afford a third column would make the region's left edge width-dependent and hand a reader an affordance present on some panes and absent on others, which is the intermittent layout this section refuses one axis over and refuses again for the scrollbar's own columns. **Three things reopen it.** [#72](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/72)'s week of real use, if it reports that readers do not find the digits or reach for a number and get nothing: this ruling has no use-evidence in either direction and the binding was one day old when it was made. ~~[#125](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/125) drawing a list deeper than six on a tall pane, past which the wanted row stops being countable at a glance and the region has become the list-to-be-searched the cap exists to prevent, and past nine of which a rank stops being one column~~ **that trigger fired on 2026-08-20 and was answered** ([#160](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/160)): the list is deeper than six from 28 rows up. Struck rather than deleted, for the reason the third one below is, because a condition that quietly vanished would read as one still pending. And ~~[#124](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/124), if a heading in the border line turns out to be somewhere an address can live without riding a content row~~ — **that door closed 2026-08-15**: §11.2 B11 ruled the border line stays bare, so there is no heading in it for an address to ride, and this trigger cannot fire unless B11 is reopened first. Struck rather than deleted, because a reopening condition that quietly vanished would read as one that was met. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_caret_column_draws_a_mark_and_never_a_rank` is what fails if the gutter ever gains one, so the next session to find this change cheap has to argue for it rather than take it quietly.

**Re-ruled 2026-08-20 with the rung that fired the trigger, and the rank still is not drawn.** A trigger that fires gets the question re-opened rather than the old answer repeated, so the reasons were checked rather than inherited. Two survive unchanged: a rank is a fact about the **keymap** where every other thing in that region is a fact about the worktree, which height does not touch; and §5.3 prices ink at zero and does not price **attention** at zero, which a deeper region makes a stronger argument rather than a weaker one. The trigger's own words are what have expired: *"past which the wanted row stops being countable at a glance"* assumed the region would become a list to be searched, and at a fixed quarter of the pane it does not, because what makes a block scannable is its share of the screen rather than its row count. **And the rung produced a new reason against, which is the one that would settle it alone**: the digit grammar stops at six, so a rank on a deeper list would draw numbers on rows that are not keys, which is worse than drawing no number at all. Drawing it on only the first six is worse again, since the rows would then differ in a way that says nothing about the worktree. The remaining trigger is [#72](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/72)'s week of real use, and it is now the only one.

**The caret travels the window; the window moves the least it can.** It is held while the current file is inside it and pushed by exactly the overshoot when the file leaves. Scrolling from the start therefore walks the caret down the rows, and only then does the list move under it. **Two fixed positions were ruled first and both were wrong**, in opposite directions and for one reason: a constant row is not following a file, it is dragging the window on every step. Ending the window on the current file showed the six files *before* the six the diff was drawing. Starting the window on it fixed that and pinned the caret to the first row, which reads as the list scrolling while the marker never moves. Both reported from use. Minimal movement subsumes them: at the top the caret is on the first row because the file is first, and at the end the pull-back rests the last file on the bottom row.

**A click on a listed file puts the diff at it.** The gesture a reader tries without being told, next to a region that draws the names of the things it is a map of. It is **not** selection and B4 stands: nothing is remembered, no row becomes special, no mode appears, and the event after it means what it would have meant. That is the same argument that already licensed dragging a scrollbar, and the caret is still a marker rather than a cursor: it cannot be moved on its own. A click on the diff below stays inert, because nothing there is selectable.

**The pointer is acknowledged when it arrives as well as when it acts: what it is over is marked, on the surfaces a click already acts on and nowhere else.** Ruled 2026-08-16 as §11.2 B10, reversing the decline of the day before ([#123](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/123)). The surfaces are **a step button, a scrollbar, and a listed file**: [#186](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/186) drew the buttons and [#189](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/189) drew the other two. **Nothing in the diff body**, which is not clickable and must not imply it is, and which is the one region `Regions::hover_at` answers `None` for at every row. The reason it is not a cost question is unchanged from the decline and still worth knowing: motion already reaches this loop and already draws, so **the mark itself** adds no wake, no paint and no frame, only an `Option` of a target and a style on cells that were being redrawn anyway.

**What the build did add, which the ruling never priced, is the focus step's own traffic.** Asking for `?1004h` makes `FocusGained` and `FocusLost` arrive on Unix and macOS where they previously never did, and `run` draws once per drained batch whether or not a wake in it produced an action, so each one costs a wake and a frame. `FocusLost` earns it, since clearing the mark is a visible change. **`FocusGained` does not**: it retires nothing, produces no action, and repaints a byte-identical screen. It is bounded by how often a reader tabs back, it is an external event rather than a clock so `Shell::patience` never consults it and the idle receive stays untimed, and Windows has been paying the same repaint since before this branch, because its console delivers `FOCUS_EVENT` records whether or not anyone asked. Recorded rather than fixed: the repaint-per-batch is [#154](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/154)'s, and this is that gap reached by a second road rather than a new one.

**A click is always brighter than a hover, and that rule decides which elements may carry a mark at all.** A gesture has to outrank a pointer merely resting somewhere, or *you are doing this* stops being distinguishable from *you could*. On the bar that is three weights: `Theme::bar_track` at rest, `Theme::bar_hover` under a pointer, `Theme::bar_active` under a press or a drag. The ordering is the load-bearing half rather than the middle rung, because [#166](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/166) added the pressed mark for the case where nothing else can answer, since pressing *up* at the top of a diff moves no row, and a hover that won there would take that answer away in the one case it exists for. `crates/vigia/tests/render.rs::a_gesture_is_always_brighter_than_a_pointer_at_rest` asserts it as a rule across every surface rather than at the one element that forced it.

**The thumb and the track are one target, and the rung they needed was built rather than waited for.** A press anywhere on a track seeks, so the surface a click acts on is the whole column, and the thumb answers because it is what would move. [#186](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/186) shipped the thumb unmarked on the reasoning that it rests at `Theme::bar` and drags at `Theme::bar_active` with no rung between, and **that reasoning was sound and its conclusion was not**: *there is no rung* is a reason to add one, not to decline, which is the same shape B10 itself was reversed for. `Theme::bar_hover` is that rung. **A listed file takes `Theme::path_hover`**, which underlines and is what §5.3's amendment is about: intensity belongs to recency for anything durable, and a mark that follows the pointer is not durable. **It holds `Theme::bar_hover`'s own colour, corrected 2026-08-16** ([#193](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/193)) from a fourth weight *above* the whole ladder, which made the pane's stalest fact its loudest one and contradicted the quietness rule §5.3 states two paragraphs earlier. One colour for the pointer everywhere it lands, and the underline is what holds on `ansi`, where the foreground is now `Gray` in both places and therefore equals `Theme::path_cold`'s exactly.

**Clearing is a ladder, and naming its rungs is the ruling rather than a caveat on it.** Motion inside the pane clears exactly, because `?1003h` reports at **cell** granularity, so leaving one row arrives as entering the next: this is the common case and it needs nothing new. **It is available wherever the mouse is**, which is not quite the same as everywhere, and [#188](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/188) is the open question about that: a reading of tmux's source says a non-active pane may not have motion requested for it at all under the default `mouse` setting. That is a question about the wheel and the click this shell already ships rather than about hover, it is unverified, and it is filed rather than asserted here. Leaving the window is what needs asking for, and the asking is one step: `TAKEOVER` is `RawMode`, `AlternateScreen`, `MouseCapture`, `FocusChange`, `Cursor`, and the fourth of those was added by [#186](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/186) for exactly this. It is what the decline's surviving reason claimed was impossible, and `terminal.rs::the_takeover_takes_every_step_there_is` is what fails if it is ever taken out again, which the two gates beside it cannot see: one compares the walk against `TAKEOVER` itself and stays green whatever that array holds, and the other proves only that each command means what it is named. Every terminal checked implements the mode, and the Windows console delivers the records without being asked. **The word is *checked*, and this section's own "How colour degrades" is why it has to be**: that rules a list of terminals to be *"evidence about terminals someone checked rather than a claim about the ones nobody has"*, and the first draft of this paragraph said *every tier-1 terminal*, which is a quantifier over a set this repository has never defined. "Tier-1" means a build target here and nothing else. `RULINGS.md`'s B10 section names the six. **The rung that is missing even where the terminal has it is tmux's**, whose `focus-events` defaults to off. §1 asks for a pane beside an agent and names no multiplexer, so this is not every reader: a terminal's own split has the rung and tmux without that setting does not. It is called out because tmux is a common way to get that pane and because a reader cannot tell from the screen which case they are in. **A fourth rung was added by the build rather than by this ruling, and it is the one that keeps the residual honest: any repaint that moves the bars retires the mark.** A mark is a claim about the screen it was resolved against, so when a tick changes the geometry every cell it named may belong to something else. Without it the residual is not what the paragraph above prices: a pointer parked on the list's down button, two files reverted, and that row becomes the *diff's up button*, lit on a control nobody pointed at. `input::hover_repainted` is the rule and it deliberately does not try to tell one button from another, because the version that did was a tautology. It runs **between the layout and the paint that uses it**, not after the frame, since correcting the field after drawing fixes the state and not the screen, and on an idle tree there is no next paint to correct.

So what is left is a residual: the pointer leaves the pane with the window still focused, on a tree nobody is writing to, and the last target stays lit until the pointer comes back. That is **accepted rather than fixed**, and what pays for it is a constraint on the mark itself: **a hover mark is the quietest thing that is still visible in its region**, so a stale one costs a glance nothing and never competes with the caret, which means *the diff is in this file*. Inventing a clock to expire it is refused on what it would do rather than on an invariant, for the reason given where the three marks are stated as one rule above: I1 would license such a clock, and what refuses it is that it would put the mark out while a reader rests on a row.

**It is view state and never an action, which is what keeps B4 intact.** The mark is resolved from `Regions` in the loop and drawn, exactly as a pressed step button is; it is not a keymap entry, so no focus appears, no selection exists, nothing is remembered that a key acts on, and every binding means what it would have meant. `crates/vigia/tests/input.rs::pointer_motion_over_a_laid_out_screen_is_still_no_action` is what holds that line, and it is the gate the decline rested on: it went green through the build rather than being retired with the ruling it once protected, which is what a gate over a *mechanism* does when the ruling above it changes sign. The update rule is in two free functions, and both are free for the reason `Held::ends` is: the loop cannot be driven by a test, so a rule written inline in it is a rule with no gate. `input::hover_after` answers what an *event* does to the mark, and `input::hover_repainted` what a *repaint* does, which is a different question with a different subject. The split was found by mutation rather than by design: the first version put the second half inside `Shell`, which owns a `Session` and can therefore never be unit-tested, and reverting that body to a plain field read passed the entire workspace.

**The wheel is what the pointer's position decides today**, and the click on a listed file is the other: both read the row, neither remembers it. A reader hovering the map and turning the wheel means the map. §2 makes `btop` the reference and that is what `btop` does. It is not selection and it is not focus: nothing is remembered between events, so B4's ruling stands. **The hover mark reads the same row and still does not make it selection**, which is the distinction to keep: these two read the position to decide what an act *means*, the mark reads it to say where the pointer *is*, and none of the three gives any key a second meaning. That is what B4 forbids, and nothing here approaches it.

**A drawn thumb is a published affordance, so both are draggable**, and a press anywhere on a track jumps to it. The scrollbar column is tested before the region containing it, or every drag would read as a wheel.

**A track maps onto travel, not onto the whole.** The list's travel is its file count less its own height; the diff's is its total rows less a screenful. Mapping onto the whole instead leaves the last screenful's worth of track dead, since every fraction past the bound clamps to the same window, and the pointer then reaches the bottom with the view still short of the end. **The diff's drag resolved against the file count until 2026-08-02**, which is what its bar had counted before the narrowing above made the bar row-exact: three long files gave a track dozens of rows tall three landing spots. Reported from use, which is the fifth time a defect on this surface was, and the gates are `dragging_the_diff_bar_resolves_to_a_row_and_reaches_the_end` and `dragging_the_list_bar_reaches_the_first_file_and_the_last`. Both check the **middle** of the track as well as its ends, because the clamp makes the ends agree either way.

**Scroll position is `(file, offset within that file)`, never a row index.** A frame that changes something above the viewport therefore does not teleport the view. This is a correctness property, not an implementation detail: with a row index, an agent writing to a file earlier in the list would yank the reader's position on every keystroke it makes.

**A position past the end of the diff resolves to the last screenful, not the last row.** The two are not the same place, and taking the second for the first draws one line of content over a blank screen while the header goes on truthfully saying how many files changed: one thing on screen contradicting the other two, which is the ambiguity B3's empty state exists to remove. A pager rests the final row at the *bottom* of the viewport and so does this. Resolving it is `View::collect`'s alone, because `App::view` stores the resolved position back on every frame, so there is one rule in one place rather than a clamp in the scroll path and another behind it.

Reachable two ways, and **only one of them is scrolling**. The other is the diff shrinking under a position that was reasonable when it was taken: `git reset --hard`, a branch switch, an agent reverting its own work. That is an ordinary event on the pane this tool exists for and it is exactly when someone looks over, which is why it is here rather than filed as an edge case. [#57](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/57).

The walk back reads only the files the screen is about to draw, so I4 is untouched. It is deliberately **not** what `G` does: `End` goes to the last *file* from its top. That was ruled on cost, and **the cost argument expired on 2026-08-01** when I4 was narrowed: adding up every file's height is what `Frame::height` now does once a tick, so a row-exact `End` is affordable in a way it was not when this was written. The behaviour stands on the other reason, which is what it should always have rested on: `g` and `G` are the two ends of the *file list*, the pair the header counts and the pinned region draws, and a `G` that landed mid-file would make the two keys mean different units. Backing off from an end already in hand still costs a screenful.

**Follow mode**, which is I5. `less +F` semantics, and the toggle the README mockup already published: follow is **on at startup**, **any manual scroll disengages it**, and **`f` re-engages it and jumps straight to the newest change** rather than waiting for the next one. The footer shows `follow ▶` while it is engaged.

Two boundaries are load bearing, because each is a way for the mode to be quietly wrong rather than visibly broken. A **terminal resize does not disengage**: it moves no viewport and expresses no intent, and a monitor beside an agent is resized constantly. And **`G` disengages rather than re-engaging**, because "jump to the last changed file" and "resume following" are different intents that would otherwise be the same key, leaving a reader unable to look at the newest file without also re-arming the view.

**The newest change is the file whose write landed last in the settled batch**, and the batch is the coalesced tick. A monitor cannot find that file by looking: `stat`-ing every changed file is the cost [#19](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/19) already records as breaching I9 at scale, and I4 forbids reading files the frame does not draw. It does not have to look, because the filesystem event already carries the path. `Tick` reports it, so following costs no read, no `stat` and no diff. When the named path is not in the diff — an index write, or an edit reverted before the tick landed — the view stays where it is, because there is no newest *change* to follow.

**And follow lands on the change rather than on the heading, where those are not the same place.** Ruled 2026-08-22 from a live pane ([#257](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/257)): *"follow mode goes to the file changes, not the line change in the file. If file change is big, it can be invisible on smaller screens."* A file whose diff is one screenful keeps I5's promise by accident of size, because the heading and the change are the same place. A Swift test file carrying a 76-line deletion under three one-line tweaks does not, and the element built to show a reader what an agent just did showed them a filename and some unchanged context instead.

The rule is one sentence. **Follow keeps the heading on the top row while the first changed line of the file's busiest hunk is already drawn from there, and puts that hunk's header on the top row when it is not.** The heading carries the path, the counts, the sigil and the heat strip, so it is spent only where it buys the change, and a reader with a taller pane is moved off it less often.

**The busiest hunk rather than the first**, which is the obvious reading and which cannot reach this case at all: a block is a heading and then a header plus lines per hunk, so the first hunk's header is always row one and landing on it moves the pane by a row. **And a hunk rather than the heat strip's busiest slice**, which is the same intent in the wrong unit: a bucket names a working-tree line where a viewport needs a row, and the thing a row falls inside is a hunk. Ties keep the earlier hunk, and a file with nothing to land on keeps its heading: a conflict, a type change and a binary file draw a note rather than hunks, and a hunk of pure context is not a change.

**The test is the first changed *line* twice over, and testing the header instead is a real edge rather than a rounding of it.** A hunk opens with up to three unchanged lines, so a pane one row short of them draws the `@@` and none of what it changed, which is the reported symptom with the gate reporting it handled. So a landing needs the change to be **not drawn from the heading and drawn from the landing**, and the second half is what settles `Body::split`'s floor: a one-row diff region cannot show a changed line from anywhere, and one bare `@@` is strictly less than the heading it would replace, which carries the path, the counts, the sigil and the strip. The row landed on is still the header, because a change arrives with the line that says where it is.

**A landing fills the pane, where a jump does not.** §11.1 already rules that a viewport reached by scrolling backs up rather than resting a short tail over blank rows, and that a viewport reached by a *jump* does not, because a jump is a claim about which file belongs on the top row. A landing's claim is the weaker one: that the change is **visible**, which any row it is drawn from satisfies. So it backs up, the change moves down the pane rather than off it, and the rows below it are the diff's rather than blanks. **Only a landing that moved the viewport**, though: one that kept the heading is an ordinary jump, and backing that up would take the file follow just named off the top row. What makes that reachable is not only the last file: a block ends at its last hunk, so any file followed only by hunkless ones, a binary note or a conflict, runs out of rows the same way. A clamp on the last file was the first fix here and covered one case of the class.

**What this is not is the *newest* hunk, and that is the case to reopen on.** What a reader asks is *what did it just do*, and the diff alone cannot say: the newest hunk is the one that differs from the previous tick's diff, which is a comparison with staleness rules of its own. Busiest is never worse than the heading it replaces and is right in the shape reported, so it is what ships. The case that would reopen it is the accumulating one, a rewrite from ten minutes ago outvoting the tweak that just landed, and the answer there is the comparison rather than a third heuristic.

**A landing belongs to the file it was armed for, and to no other.** It names a row *inside a file* while the viewport holds an index, and the changed set is renumbered by every advance: a file committed, an edit reverted, a branch switched. Ticks coalesce and only the paint is shared, so an advance can land between the follow that armed a landing and the frame that would resolve it, including on a tick that names no path at all, which a `.git/index` write is. So the request is applied only while the position still names the path the tick named, and a refusal **settles** it rather than holding it: the test is re-read every frame, and a debt kept through a refusal fires the moment an index names that path again, on a frame nothing armed. Pressing `f` to disengage settles it too, for the reason a manual scroll does. What does *not* settle it is a frame that could not serve it, a pane dragged below the floor where no diff is drawn at all: that frame resolved nothing, and the tick that armed the request has been spent.

**It costs nothing, and the signature is what keeps that true.** `App::follow` still takes `&Frame`, so following still cannot diff, cannot read and cannot `stat`, which also means it cannot find the row: the loop advances, follows and only then draws, so at the moment of the jump the frame holds the previous tick's diff for the one file that just changed. The jump therefore names the file and the walk that draws it resolves the row, from a diff it has already fetched, once. `tests/follow.rs::following_a_file_costs_no_diff_and_no_read` is unchanged and `landing_on_a_change_costs_no_extra_diff` is the gate over the resolution.

**How the chrome fits**, which is I6. One rule, and the layout follows from it: **a thing made of items breaks, a thing made of characters marks its edge, and content is neither.**

The **hint bar is a list**, so when the footer cannot hold both halves on one line it takes a **second line** rather than shortening anything. The state moves to the upper of the two and the hints keep the bottom row, so narrowing a pane never moves the hints out from under a reader's eye. It grows only while at least two body rows survive — a monitor with no diff left in it has stopped being one — and only when there is a state worth moving. A notice, which replaces the hints, inherits that whole line when there is one, but **never causes it**: a notice is transient, and a footer that grew for one would jog the reader's diff down a row and back every time a file vanished between being named and being read. The height is a function of width, follow state and changed-file count, all of which change only when the diff does.

Below the width where even a full line holds the bar, it drops **whole hints** and never part of one: `q quit` first, then `? keys`, leaving `f follow` last.

**The bar is three items, and that is [#80](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/80) ruled 2026-08-17 from use: *"keep only q f and ? on the bottom bar"*.** `q quit · f follow · ? keys`, twenty-three columns at its widest where the four-item bar was thirty-eight and the original was forty. What makes it defensible rather than merely shorter is B12: the keymap is on a sheet now, so a bar that named four gestures of sixteen was giving a reader an arbitrary quarter of a table they can open in full. `jk scroll` went the way of `JK files`, and both are on the sheet. **Neither is a loss of the only copy**, which was the argument for keeping them here; and a scroll key is the gesture a pager reader tries first without being told, where nothing on the pane hints that a keymap exists.

**So the bar is what it is for**: the way out, the state a reader can lose invisibly, and the door to everything else. The order settles one new question, which is where `? keys` sits: above `q quit`, because a reader who cannot find the keymap cannot find anything else, and below `f follow`, because a sheet nobody opened costs nothing while a follow mode nobody can see costs the screen.

**The arrows are deliberately unadvertised, and #80 asked for that in writing either way.** They are named on the sheet and nowhere on the bar. §11.1's own reason applies unchanged and is now written down rather than implied: `f follow` earns the last rung by being the one nobody would guess, and an arrow key is the one everybody guesses.

**There is no bonus rung any more, and that is what the shorter bar bought.** The footer's height is decided by the widest bar a reader is owed, and every rung here is owed, so `HINT_BASELINE` is rung zero and no hint can buy a body row by existing. `JK files` is why the constant exists: it made the widest bar exactly forty columns, so the footer began taking a second line at the width I6 is named for, and every reader lost a body row to advice including the ones who never pressed `J`. **[#147](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/147) did not get answered, it lost its subject**: whether a bonus rung may cost a status readout is unanswerable while there is no bonus rung, and the first hint anybody adds above the baseline brings the question back with it. What did change is the arithmetic it measured: at 26 columns of bar rather than 40, each readout arrives about eleven columns of pane earlier.

**And the state is two columns from the hints rather than one**, corrected in the same pass. The gap was one column and invisible for as long as the owed bar was twenty-nine wide, because `29 + 13 + 1` overflows a forty-column pane and the footer took its second line before hints and state could ever meet. A twenty-six column bar and a thirteen column state fit forty columns exactly, and the pane drew `? keys follow ▶` with a single space between them: the hint separator is ` · `, every other group on the row is two columns from the next, and the state read as a fourth hint. So the footer takes its second line at forty columns while following, which is what it did before, and the reason is now the same one the diagnostics group already had. `q` and `jk` are pager reflexes and four keys reach quit, while `f` is the one nobody would guess and the only one that restores a state a reader can lose without noticing. The state has its own ladder, `follow ▶  N/M` then `follow ▶` alone, because the header already carries the file count. **State outlives advice at every width**, which is what keeps the mode visible when the pane is at its worst.

**And advice outlives instrumentation**, which is the rung the two status readouts join. `0.8ms frame` and the memory cell sit left of the state as one **diagnostics** group, dropping the memory cell first and the frame cell second, so both are gone before a single hint is. That ordering is not the ladder rule above repeated: the hints are how a reader *operates* the tool and the state is what the *tree* is doing, while these two describe **`vigia` itself**, which is the least of the three things a narrow pane owes anyone. Memory before frame time, because the frame cell reports a budget a reader can act on and the memory cell reports a claim that barely moves.

**Neither readout can change how many rows the footer takes**, and that is a rule rather than a consequence. `Footer::plan` decides its height from the state ladder alone, exactly as it did before these existed, and the diagnostics take only what is left after the hints have their widest fitting rung. Two things would otherwise jog a reader's diff down a row and back for no reason they could see: the frame cell **does not exist on the first paint**, because no frame has completed to have a p99 of, and the memory cell does not exist on a platform with no cheap read. That is the same rule a notice already has one paragraph up, and it is here for the same reason — a transient or absent thing must not move content.

**The footer's right-hand side carries three colours, and they are the picture's.** `assets/preview.svg` draws `0.8ms` and `24MiB` in its cyan, the word `frame` beside them in the same grey as the hints, and the follow marker in green. The shipped footer drew all of it in one grey, so the two numbers a reader *checks* and the marker saying the mode is on looked like the words a reader *reads*. §5.1's rule that a published artifact answering a question is the answer applies to colour exactly as it applied to layout. The position `N/M` keeps the grey it has: the picture gives no colour for it, and it is a place rather than a measurement.

Both styles are **reused rather than named anew**, which keeps this a reading of the picture rather than a palette decision: the cyan is the same one every other structural mark uses and the green is the diff's own added colour, each matching the picture to the byte on the dark palette. That is the reuse `not watching` already makes of the alert.

Drawn as a **second pass over the cells**, not as separate placements, and that is load bearing rather than an implementation note. Each readout is part of a token a ladder picks whole: `0.8ms frame   19MiB` is one rung and `follow ▶  N/M` is another. Placing each colour separately would take the choice of what to draw away from the ladders and would oblige the footer's width arithmetic to know about colour. Tinting afterwards cannot move a column.

**The pass must terminate and must not reach past the diagnostics**, and both are rulings rather than implementation notes because both were got wrong. A walk that asks whether a cell *opens* a measurement and whether it *carries* one has to consume the opening cell whatever it says: `>` answers yes and no, so on `>1s` the two questions spun against each other and the pane froze mid-frame. And the marker is found within the state's own columns, never the whole row, because a notice is an error string carrying a path and `▶` is a legal filename character: scanning the row let a file name fabricate the one glyph that says the view is live.

**Both cells are a constant width whatever they say.** `0.8ms frame`, ` 12ms frame` and `999ms frame` are all eleven columns, and the number is right-aligned inside them, so a value changing between frames never shuffles what is beside it. Past the field, magnitude gives way to a sigil rather than to precision: `>1s` for a frame and `>1GiB` for memory, because the exact value at those magnitudes tells a reader nothing the sigil does not, and a number that grew a column would move the footer under their eye.

The **header never grows**, and after the ruling above that has to be said of both sides rather than of one token. A worktree name is not a list and has nowhere to break, so it marks its edge like every other single token; the clause it now leads *is* a thing made of items, and it breaks the way the rule says by dropping a whole rung rather than by taking a line. A second line could not guarantee a fit either way, and it would spend a body row on a maybe.

**The header is laid out by subject, the way the footer is.** Two facts about the **tree** on the left, the worktree name and then how much of that tree has changed, and the one fact about **`vigia` itself** on the right, which is whether the watch is still live. That is the three-subject rule the footer runs on further up — advice, then what the tree is doing, then what `vigia` is doing — applied to the row at the other end of the screen. **The grouping is borrowed and the priority is not**, and the difference is worth stating because the two rows rank the same subject oppositely: on the footer, what `vigia` is doing is the least a narrow pane owes anyone and the diagnostics drop first, while on the header the self-fact is the last thing standing. Neither is inconsistent, because the priority was never the subject in the first place. It is **recoverability**: the footer's readouts describe a budget and a number that barely moves, and both are recomputed on the next frame, where a header that has stopped saying it is live leaves a reader with no way to find out. It is a ruling rather than an arrangement, and [#67](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/67) is what it costs to get wrong: the header used to seat the count beside the mode word, where `watching · 3 files` read as *"watching 3 files"*, a participle with an object naming a curated watch set that does not exist and that B6's no-flags ruling puts out of scope. Neither fact was wrong. Their **adjacency** was, and a separator that promises one subject is what let English supply one. Reported by a reader who asked, in order, what `watching` is, how to toggle watching something, and whether it is automatic. All three questions follow from the row, and the screen confirmed the wrong answer rather than correcting it, because `3 files` sat directly above exactly three summary rows.

The count reads `changed` rather than `files` for a reason that only appears once it has moved: `vigia · 3 files` would be a **worse** claim than the one it replaced, since the repository has more than three files in it. Next to the name of a tree, a bare noun describes that tree; `changed` is what makes the number a fact *about* it. It is also one rule where there were two, because `changed` is a participle with no plural to inflect.

**The ladder keeps its order and changes only which side each rung is dropped from.** Widest first: `{worktree} · {N} changed` with the mode word, then the worktree name with the mode word, then the worktree name alone. That is the ladder; it is not the whole list of screens, because the two sides degrade independently. A narrowing pane walks through **six** states, which the paragraph below gives with their widths: both facts and the word, the name and the word, a marked fragment of the name and the word, the word alone, the name alone, and a marked fragment alone. The count still yields before the mode word, for the reason it always did. Inside the left-hand clause the rule the rest of this section runs on decides the rest: the count is an **item** and drops whole, the name is a **token** and marks its edge, and the count is the one that goes, because the name is the header fact a reader cannot reconstruct by looking at the body and because B3's empty state leans on it to say which repository this is. Both facts on the left are drawn in one weight, which is the visual half of the same ruling: two weights inside one clause would tell a reader in colour that these are separate claims, which is the seam being removed.

**The left-hand side leads with the worktree, not the program**, and this is the first of the two places the layout departs from `assets/preview.svg` on purpose, the other being §5.1's element split. The picture puts `vigia` there. A title bar spends six of forty columns telling the reader which program they started, which is the one thing they already know, and what they do not know from looking is *which tree* — the question that decides whether two panes side by side mean anything. Written here 2026-07-31 rather than left in a code comment, because §5.1's own rule is that a published artifact answering a question is the answer, so a deliberate departure from one is exactly the thing that has to be argued in the open or it reads as drift.

And it is the worktree's own name **even when the path it was given does not contain one**. `vigia .` hands `gix` a workdir of `.`, which has no final component, so the header drew `.` — the single thing it exists to say, and the one thing it could not. The path is resolved for **display** before its last component is taken. Display only: the value the watch compares event paths against is left alone, because resolving it there would introduce `\\?\C:\` on Windows and `/private/var` on macOS, and [#30](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/30) is the record of what a root that matches no event path costs.

**The mode word, and the set it implies.** It sits on the right, alone. The mockup drew it there with the count beside it; `assets/preview.svg` was corrected with the ruling above rather than departed from, so the picture and the shell agree on this row and the deliberate departures stay at two.

| Mode | What it means |
|---|---|
| `watching` | the watch is live, so the screen follows the tree |
| `not watching` | the watch never armed, or it ended, so this is a still picture |

**Two, and I1 is the reason rather than minimalism.** §5.1 read `watching` as implying at least a settling state and an idle one. It implies neither, because both are *durations* and this shell wakes only when something changes. `Watcher::next_tick` blocks until a burst has settled, so the shell is never awake **during** settling and could not draw it without a redraw nothing schedules; and "idle" needs a wake that says nothing happened, which is the timer I1 forbids. Either word could come into existence and then never leave, which is precisely the frozen clock the recency ladder above rejected for the pulse. A header that lies about the present until the next file is written is worse than one with fewer words in it.

**The word starts at `watching`, before the watch is armed.** Arming happens after first paint on purpose, so the watch does not observe the shell's own setup reads. A third word for those microseconds would flicker on every single launch to describe a state that always resolves the same way within one wake, and when arming genuinely fails the failure arrives as its own wake and corrects the word.

**`not watching`, not `stalled` and not `still`.** It is the mockup's word negated, so a reader who has learned one has learned both. `stalled` reads as temporary when this is not, and `still` means both "motionless" and "continuing".

**The mode is state, so the reason for it is not.** A watch that ends puts `not watching` on the header **and** its error on the footer as a notice. The header carries what is durable — this diff has stopped being live — and the notice carries which failure did it, which is not. Before this the durable half rode the notice alone, and survived only because the tick that clears a notice can never arrive again once the watch is gone.

**And a dead watch is drawn loud while a live one stays quiet.** `watching` takes the header's dim grey, the one the footer's own secondary tokens are drawn in; `not watching` takes the footer's alert. The changed-file count left that class with [#67](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/67) and now takes the worktree name's weight, which is the ruling one paragraph up rather than an exception to this one. A state nobody can catch at a glance has not been reported, and §5 makes colour half the differentiator, so a header whose failure looks exactly like its working state fails twice. It is the **footer's own** alert rather than a colour of its own, because the notice carrying which failure already uses it and the two halves of one event should not arrive in two different reds. That is a reuse of an existing style rather than a palette decision, which stays [#11](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/11)'s.

**A file whose language this process has not met yet arrives plain and colours a moment later.** Ruled and shipped 2026-08-21 ([#129](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/129)). The reader sees the text immediately, at the pane's ordinary few milliseconds, and the colour follows when the grammar has compiled off the frame: a cold screenful measures 124ms under Rust and 695ms under Markdown, and 506ms in the report this was filed on. **What it replaces is not colour but a freeze.** The loop is single-threaded, so before this the pane stopped answering for exactly that window and the footer then read `506ms frame`; a reader watching an agent write Swift beside them described it as *"momentarily, it goes back to 1-5ms as usual after 1-2 seconds"*.

This is I7's opening rule generalised rather than a new one. I7 already draws the first frame of a process without colour on the grounds that a compile belongs behind a screen that has content on it, and the only thing #129 changed is that the same reasoning now applies to the frame that meets a *grammar* rather than only to the frame that starts a process.

**Only the hunks under that grammar are plain**, never the pane, and it happens once per grammar per session. **And the case a reader is actually watching should not flicker at all**, which is why the three languages the repository leads with are compiled at launch from the index: a pane is very often opened on a clean tree *before* the agent writes, so a warm over the changed set has nothing to warm, and what a repository is mostly made of is the best available guess at what will be written next. Three, and not more, because the tail costs +58.00 MiB (§6).

**The mode word outranks everything else wherever it fits at all**, which is why it holds the side that is placed first: the count summarises a body that is on screen and can be recovered by counting it, and whether the pane is still live is recoverable from nowhere. That is the footer's own rule further up, and it matters most at exactly the widths where the body has nothing in it to count.

**It is not, however, the last thing standing on the row, and the difference is measurable rather than pedantic.** The two sides have independent budgets: the word is all-or-nothing at its own width, while the name marks its edge at any width above zero. So on a live watch the name has the row alone from 5 to 7 columns, the word takes it alone from 8, a marked fragment of the name rejoins it at 10, and both are drawn whole from 14; with `not watching`, four columns wider, those are 5 to 11, 12, 14 and 18. Widening a pane from 7 to 8 columns therefore *removes* the worktree name. That is unchanged behaviour and it is what placing the right first has always meant; it is recorded because the tidier claim — one total order over three facts — is false, and this file's own rule is that an invariant with no failing test is a wish. There is no test for the tidier claim because there is nothing true to gate.

**The numbers in the paragraph above are gated by `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_header_degrades_at_the_widths_the_spec_records`**, and that is a rule rather than a courtesy. They were wrong here first: this said both facts appear from 13, where 13 draws `vig› watching` and only 14 draws the name whole. A measurement quoted in prose and nowhere else drifts from the thing it measured, silently, because no document fails.

**The count is nothing at all when it is zero**, the same way a position is nothing when there is no diff to be positioned within. `0 changed` spends columns restating what the empty state says below in words.

**And there is no fourth fact.** The header carries the worktree, the changed-file count and the mode word, and it does not carry a repository-wide `+`/`-` total. That is a ruling rather than an omission, and it is [#49](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/49): a total needs every changed file's diff, which puts first paint back in proportion to the size of the diff and is exactly what I4 forbids, and computing it behind the frame to dodge that needs a wake no filesystem event caused, which is I1's. §10 carries the argument and §5.1 records what it settles about the mockup. `crates/vigia/tests/render.rs` gates it over the drawn row rather than over what a frame cost, because a total computed on a handle of its own would never reach the stats `tests/reads.rs` reads.

**And the mode word is never cut.** It is drawn whole or dropped, which is stricter than the marking rule the rest of the header follows: `wat›` is a state a reader cannot read, and unlike a path it has no half that identifies it. **What delivers that is `put_right`, which draws its token whole or not at all**, rather than a ladder of the word's own. It had one until [#67](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/67), because this side carried the count too and so had a real choice to make; moving the count left left a ladder of one rung wrapped around a mechanism that was already doing the work, and two mechanisms guaranteeing one rule is a rule nobody can locate.

**The empty state**, which is B3. This is the screen the tool sits on most of the time, and the first thing anyone sees when they open it beside an agent that has not written yet, where a blank pane and a hang look identical.

Four facts, two of which the header already carries, so the body spends one line rather than four:

| Fact | Where it is drawn |
|---|---|
| which repository | the header's left-hand side |
| that it is watching | the header's mode word |
| which branch | the body line |
| that there is nothing to show | the body line |

The line reads `no unstaged changes · main`, and `no unstaged changes` alone when HEAD names no branch.

**It no longer reads `working tree clean`, and that phrase was wrong rather than merely plain.** It is git's, and git means index-against-HEAD as well as tree-against-index. This diff is the working tree against the index, so a worktree with every change staged draws zero files and was being told it was clean while `git status` said the opposite. `no unstaged changes` is exactly what zero files means here, untracked files included, since an untracked file is unstaged too.

**The branch is orientation, not the comparison.** Nothing about it changes what is diffed, and it is named anyway because two agents on two worktrees of one repository are otherwise identical on screen, which is the multi-worktree case §4 defers rather than rejects. A detached HEAD names no branch and the line drops it rather than inventing one: `HEAD@abc123` would put a commit id in a monitor that shows no commits.

**It costs one `.git/HEAD` read, taken only on frames that draw no diff.** I4 holds because the thing read is the thing drawn, and the frame that draws it is the cheapest one there is: nothing to diff, and nothing else to read.

And a token that had to lose characters says so, in the direction it lost them. `…` on the **left** means the beginning is gone, and only a file path uses it, because the end of a path is what names the file. `›` on the **right** means it continues past the edge: the worktree name, a notice, a hunk header, a note, the empty-state line. A hunk header silently cut to `@@ -258,7 +25` reads as a different line number, which is the failure this closes.

**A blank row closes every file's block but the last.** Ruled 2026-08-15 ([#165](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/165)), and it is the one boundary on this screen the picture could not settle: `assets/preview.svg` draws a single file's diff, so §5.1's law that a published artifact answering an open question *is* the answer does not reach a question the picture never poses. The stream has one boundary per changed file and drew none of them, while the boundary between the two **regions** one level up gets a rule of its own. A heading is a `Painter::file_row` carrying the kind letter, the path, the pulse, the heat strip, the sparkline and the counters, so without the blank a dense row lands directly under a dense row and the only thing marking the seam is content.

**Trailing rather than leading, and that is what keeps the map's promises.** Every jump here resolves to `Position { file, row: 0 }`: `n`, `p`, the digits, `g`, `G`, and a click on a listed file. So row zero of a file has to be its heading. (A follow jump resolves there too and is then moved off it by the landing rule in §11.1, which is a row *into* the block and leaves this claim about row zero exactly as it was.) A leading blank would put one above every heading a reader jumped to, or force the jump to resolve to heading-minus-one, which is the off-by-one this row model has already been bitten by. `crates/vigia/tests/scroll.rs::every_jump_lands_on_a_heading_and_never_on_a_gap` asserts the drawn row rather than the position, because the two designs produce identical `Position`s and only the row tells them apart.

**And not after the last file, which is the exception rather than the tidy rule.** The first draft of this ruling was uniform, on the argument that a per-file conditional costs more than one blank at the bottom of the stream. It does not, and the reason is not arithmetic: the paragraph above about resting on the last **screenful** rules the bottom of the diff to be content, `crates/vigia/tests/scroll.rs::the_bottom_of_the_diff_is_content_rather_than_blank` is the gate over it, and that gate carries its own warning about having once been weakened into asserting the defect it was named against. A blank there would separate the diff from nothing, since the footer is chrome with a row of its own, and it would spend the row a reader who scrolled to the end asked for.

**A blank row and not a rule**, because §11.2 B11 has just ruled the rule between the regions stays bare, so a second rule inside the stream would mean something different on the same screen, and §5.3's furniture law would run it full-bleed. Space is the quietest thing that is still a boundary.

**It is counted, which is the half that had no gate.** `view::rows_of` and `view::span_of` are the counting twins the row-exact scrollbar is scaled against, and the blank belongs to neither: whether a file is followed by one is a fact about its **position**, where both are handed a file. `view::gap_rows` is that fact written once, and a caller holding a position asks for a **block** rather than adding the term: `view::block_of` where the file has been diffed, `view::block_rows` where the span cache already knows, `view::diff_rows` for the total. **That shape is a correction, and the correction is the part worth keeping.** The first version made `gap_rows` public and let all six expressions that sum file heights add the term themselves, under a doc enumerating them; the doc named four of the six on the day it landed, and the two it missed broke silently — a cost diagnostic went out by exactly `files - 1`, and a scrollbar-drag gate stayed green only because two omissions cancelled. `Body`'s rule one region up already had the answer: *"All three numbers come from one function because they have to agree."* Nothing checked that agreement before this ruling, since every other gate on this surface reads a *position* and a drifting total moves no position at all — it moves the thumb, silently, on a bar this project already had to make row-exact once. `crates/vigia/tests/scroll.rs::the_counting_twins_agree_with_the_rows_drawn` is that gate.

**A clipped diff line is marked too, and is not a truncated label.** Content cannot wrap, because a wrapped line moves every line below it and the shape of the screen stops meaning anything. Nor can it elide, because unlike a label no part of it is the identifying part. So it is clipped and marked `›`, and I6's "truncated-to-useless labels" is read as being about labels rather than about content. That is a ruling, not an omission: the alternative was a horizontal pan, which is a key and a mode this spec does not name.

**The branch is drawn on every frame, and the empty state stopped drawing it.** Ruled 2026-08-17 ([#158](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/158)). B3 gave the branch to the empty state because nothing else on the pane named it, and it answers *which line of work*, which is the one thing here a reader cannot reconstruct from the body: the list says what changed and the diff says how, and neither says against what. The masthead's arrival made that line draw it **twice** on the one screen where both are visible, so it moved to the header, which is the better owner for being there always where the empty state is there once. Its rung sits **between the count and the name**: the count drops first because the list repeats it, the name drops last because B3 leans on it to say which repository this is, and the branch is in between, nowhere else on screen but not what identifies the pane. At a width too narrow for the rung nothing names the branch, which is already what happens to the count and the mode word.
