A detached HEAD draws no branch anywhere rather than inventing one, because `HEAD@abc123` would put a commit id in a monitor that shows no commits.

**And `branch_for`'s guard is gone, which is the rule surviving its instance.** It read *never touch a file the frame does not draw*, and its instance was that only the empty state names a branch, so a populated frame must not go near `.git/HEAD`. Drawing the branch always makes that premise false: the read is now a read the frame **is** going to draw, which is exactly what the rule permits. The general form stands and `crates/vigia/tests/reads.rs` asserts the other direction now, that every frame reads it and reads it once.

**A clear column stands between the sigil and the line, and it does not ladder.** Ruled 2026-08-15 ([#164](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/164)), towards the picture, and it is the **fifth** departure from `assets/preview.svg` this document has had to record rather than a preference argued from taste. The mockup states its own grid in a comment, one cell at `13.5px` being about `8.1px`, and in the diff body it places the line number end-anchored at `x=60`, the sigil at `x=72` and every content origin at `x=88`. The sigil is one cell, `72` to `80.1`, so `7.9px` of clear column has stood after it since before any of this was built, and the origin is uniform down the block: the unsigilled context rows take their content from `x=88` too, and every indent is `88` plus a whole number of cells, which the file's own comment calls out at `120.4` for four spaces. The shell drew none, so a removal's red `-` ran into the first token a reader scans for. §5.3 calls §5.1's departure list the complete set of licensed disagreements and this was not on it, which makes it an omission rather than a decision. (The list's own arithmetic is separately wrong and [#156](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/156) owns that; this paragraph adds a member to it without settling the count.)

**Why it does not degrade, where every other spacing decision here does.** #119's margin is a ladder and the row's glance elements are a ladder, so a rung was the obvious shape. The sigil column **is the diff signal** at any depth or on any palette that cannot wash the row, which the tinted-row cell of §5.1 states and `crates/vigia/tests/colour.rs` gates, so a column that keeps that signal legible is part of the signal rather than decoration beside it and I6's "every cell is contested" does not reach it. A gap that vanished at 43 columns would take the signal's legibility away exactly where the pane is most crowded.

**And it is uniform rather than conditional on the line, which is the part worth stating because the cheaper rule is so nearly right.** The gap earns its column only where a line has no leading whitespace of its own; an indented line already stands off its sigil by its own indentation. Drawing it only for unindented lines would make a content row's origin a function of its *contents*, which is the intermittent layout this section has already refused twice, for the counts cell and for the scrollbar's reserved column. So every content row pays it, and the ones that did not need it are the price of the ones that did.

**What it costs, measured rather than waved at.** One column of every content row, and it lands hardest at the forty I6 is named for: on the reference fixture the eighty-column pane clips nothing before or after, and the forty-column pane goes from **one** clipped line to **three**. `crates/vigia/tests/paint.rs`'s row-cost gates are bounds on characters examined rather than exact counts, so they gain slack rather than moving. The hunk header, the file heading and both regions' chrome are untouched: the column comes out of content and nothing else. **The gutter is untouched in what it draws and moved in when it is affordable**, which is the correction this ruling nearly shipped without: the column comes out of content, so the rule deciding *how much content is enough* is the one thing that had to be recharged, and it was not. `gutter_width` measured a pane against a prefix that no longer existed, so at the narrowest width keeping the line numbers it enforced `MIN_TEXT_WIDTH`'s floor at 23 rather than 24, on a pane with a scrollbar and therefore in the ordinary case for a diff taller than its region. One width per digit count, all below 43. **Nothing reddened, because that floor had no test**: it was a threshold two expressions held by hand, which is the wish `CLAUDE.md` names, and the two gutter gates that exist sample away from the boundary and assert the gutter does not *vanish* rather than that what survives clears the floor. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::a_drawn_gutter_leaves_the_text_its_floor` is what fails now, and the drop boundary moves out one column per digit count as the floor's own ruling is finally paid. **Rejected: moving the gutter's existing trailing space to the far side of the sigil**, which is free and wrong twice, because the picture puts a gap on *both* sides of the sigil and because `260-` reads as arithmetic rather than as a line number beside a removal.

**How a diff line is coloured**, which is what I2b pays for. Content is syntax highlighted, and the mockup had already ruled how: added, removed and context lines all carry the same classes, the sigil is green or red, and the line number stays faint. **Ruled 2026-07-30: follow the picture literally**, per §5.1's rule that a published artifact answering an open question is the answer. Only content is highlighted. A file heading, a hunk header and a note are chrome, and chrome that changed colour by what it happened to name would stop being readable as chrome.

Three things follow, and each is a constraint rather than a preference.

**The engine emits meanings, not colours.** `vigia-core` maps a syntax scope onto one of nine classes and stops. Which colour a class gets is the shell's, and therefore [#11](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/11)'s. A core that emitted truecolour would have settled the palette question in the one place §6 says has no terminal in it, and it would have pre-empted the 256-colour degradation path before anyone wrote it.

**The diff signal degrades at sixteen colours, and since 2026-08-17 it degrades to the sigil column *and the left bar* rather than to the sigil alone.** What separates an added line from a context line in the mockup is the row tint and the left bar of §5.1. Sixteen foreground-only colours cannot draw the **tint**, and the sentence that follows is why; they can draw the **bar** ([#218](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/218)), because it is a background on a blank column of margin with no text for a solid block to destroy, so `ansi` carries one in named green and red at every depth. The loss recorded below is therefore real and smaller than it was: what is lost at sixteen colours is the band, not the edge. So until #11 lands a background, `+` and `−` carry it alone. Recorded out loud because §5 makes shape and colour the whole differentiator, and this is the one place where following the picture spends some of it. The alternative considered and rejected was to keep unclassified text green on an added line and red on a removed one: it preserves the wash, it contradicts the picture, and it would have made the shell's colours mean two different things at once.

**A file type nothing recognises is not an error.** The syntax is resolved by §6's five steps — the ambiguity table, whole file name with a leading-dot retry, extension, the nearest-grammar approximations, then the file's first line when one is known — and anything unresolved draws exactly as it did before there was highlighting at all. A monitor that refused a file because it could not colour it would have inverted its own job.

**How recency is drawn**, which is I10 and which §5.1 asked for as two separate things. It is **one ladder with three rungs**, read from one store through one lookup, and each rung is an intensity on the file's own heading:

| Rung | What it means | How it draws |
|---|---|---|
| **pulse** | named by the **most recent tick** | brightest, plus a `●` in the pulse colour |
| **live** | has a sample inside the 120-second window | plain |
| **cold** | nothing in the window, so nothing is tracked for it | faintest |

Three rulings are inside that table and none is cosmetic.

**The top rung is not a duration, and the reason changed on 2026-08-22 while the conclusion did not.** §5.1 reads the mockup's persisting mark as a decay, and a decay measured against a wall clock has to be *seen* decaying. This said that needs "a redraw nothing schedules" and that inventing a timer to get one "is exactly what I1 forbids", and [#243](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/243) reversed both halves: the window ages on a clock, and I1's licence reaches it. So the argument from I1 is gone and the conclusion stands on coherence instead. The rung means *there is ink in the newest sample*, so it expires with that sample, on the same grid as the band and the sparklines drawn beside it. A duration of its own would be a second clock disagreeing with them about how long ago *now* was, which is what #234 forbids.

Two sentences here were the freeze itself, argued for. This read *"a tree that has gone quiet keeps the mark on the file that really is the newest change, indefinitely and correctly"*, and indefinitely is not correctly: the mark sat at full brightness beside a band that had almost drained, saying a two-minute-old write was the present. The store still advances only on a wake, and the wake is now either a tick or the window's own bounded clock.

**Cold is not a fourth rule.** A path with nothing left in the window is dropped from the store entirely, per I10, so "cold" and "untracked" are the same state. That is also what the **first** frame of a session looks like: a worktree that was already dirty draws every row cold, because a monitor has no way to know what happened before it was looking and inventing a recency for it would light up rows nothing has touched.

**Three is a property of the store, not of the palette, and this file said otherwise for a phase.** §5.1 and `theme.rs` both promised that the *real* gradient arrives with [#11](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/11)'s truecolour, on the reasoning that sixteen foreground-only colours have only bold, plain and dim to spend. The arithmetic is right and the conclusion does not follow. What #11 widens is how many distinct colours a rung may be drawn *in*; what decides how many rungs there **are** is `Recency`, and it has three variants because the store can answer exactly three questions about a path: is it in the newest tick, is it anywhere in the window, or is it not tracked at all. A fourth rung would have to mean *how far through the window*, which nothing currently computes and which the row above makes expensive to add honestly: the window is real time, the sampling is event-driven, and a fraction of it read on a quiet tree is a number that ages without being redrawn. So #11 replaced bold, plain and dim with three chosen luminances, and stopped there. Widening the ladder is an I10 change with a budget attached, not a theme.

The general shape, and it is the second time this file has recorded it: **a limit blamed on the rendering layer was a limit of the data behind it.** §5.2 caught the mirror image, an expensive-looking property that turned out to be a by-product already computed. Before assigning a limitation to the thing that draws, check what the thing that supplies can actually distinguish.

**The sparkline never draws a partial bucket or a squeezed strip**, and until 2026-08-20 that was because it was read as a thing made of items and **broke**: it dropped whole buckets, oldest first. **It is a projection now** ([#234](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/234)), so a narrower rung sums adjacent source buckets and draws the whole window at a lower resolution, which is the clause below stated for a second element rather than a new rule.

**The reason the old ruling was reversed rather than outvoted.** The clause below was written on 2026-08-16, and the worktree churn band landed the same day; both postdate the sparkline's ruling, which was made when nothing else on screen drew the same window. A sparkline's items are slices of one **window**, in order, and the set of them is the claim, so a strip showing the newest half of a window beside a band drawing all of it is the same misdescription the clause below refuses one axis over. Measured before it was believed, over forty seeded series in three work patterns: dropping the oldest half costs **shape as well as span**, taking a twelve-bucket strip from 5.75 distinct drawn heights to 5.25 on a steady worktree, and what carries the shape is the number of drawn columns rather than the period each covers. So the count became a rung and every rung covers the window. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_sparkline_reprojects_rather_than_dropping_buckets` is the gate, over a window written at both ends and quiet between them, which is the one shape where truncation and re-projection differ.

**And the bound the retired ceiling left is the window rather than a period.** #161 ruled twelve a ceiling because a drawn bucket had to stay coarser than a churn-band column; [#232](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/232) retired the band's fixed period and the bound with it. What replaced it is that both elements read one store over one window and a reader has both on screen, so what has to agree is the **window they cover**: the band covers all of it at every width, and so does the sparkline at every rung. `crates/vigia/tests/masthead.rs::the_band_is_never_coarser_than_a_drawn_sparkline_bucket` is what compares them, where the retired gate compared two constants.

**One consequence is a denominator rather than a width, and it is the half that is easy to get wrong.** A drawn bucket summing `n` source ones has to be measured against what `n` source buckets are worth, and that is **not** the finest figure multiplied: `scale_of` averages the non-empty values and grouping merges empties into their neighbours, so multiplying is exact on a busy worktree and wrong by up to `n` on a quiet one. Measured on a sparse fixture the estimate moved a cell by as much as **four rungs of the eight-level ramp**, so `vigia_core::History` computes one figure per grouping in the walk it already makes, and `vigia_core::SPARK_GROUPS` is the ladder it computes them for.

**The heat strip is a fourth kind of thing, and the rule needed a third clause to say so.** *A thing made of items breaks, a thing made of characters marks its edge, and content is neither* covers a list, a token and a line. A heat strip is made of items and is **not a list**: its items are slices of one file, in order, and the set of them *is* the claim. Dropping the last six would draw the first half of a file as though it were the whole of it, and a reader would conclude the tail is untouched. Less detail is honest; a prefix presented as a whole is not.

So **a projection re-projects rather than dropping items**: a narrower rung sums adjacent slices and classifies the sums, which at halves is exact. Twenty-four slices, then twelve, then six, then none. **The source resolution and the drawn count stopped being one number on 2026-08-18** ([#161](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/161)): `heat_at` groups `HEAT_BUCKETS / width`, so the source is the ceiling on this ladder, and doubling it is what lets a pane wide enough to deserve it draw a finer strip. **Twelve is a rung now rather than a constant**, which keeps §5.1's ruling instead of overturning it: what `assets/preview.svg` answers is how many slices a pane draws **at the picture's own width**, its comment records that width as 109 columns, and `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::the_pictured_width_still_draws_twelve_slices` is what turned that from a sentence into a gate. **What bounds the ladder from here is the projection and not the layout**: below a source of 48 a file shorter than the strip draws a solid change as dashes, because consecutive lines land on non-adjacent slices, so the 48-slice rung waits on [#230](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/230) rather than on a width. `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs` gates it with a file changed at both ends and quiet in the middle, which is the one shape where truncation and re-projection differ, and it reads **cell colours** rather than symbols because every slice draws the same block.

**And the two strips scale against different things, deliberately.** A sparkline's height is measured against `scale_of`'s figure over every bucket **on screen**, because the question a reader asks down a file list is which file is busiest, and a row scaled against itself draws every file at full height the moment it is the busiest thing it has ever been. A heat slice's intensity is measured against the busiest slice **of its own file**, because the question is where in *this* file the work is, and the neighbouring rows have nothing to do with it. One is a comparison down the list, the other across a row. Its heights are scaled against that one figure **on screen** rather than against each row's own maximum, because scaling per row draws every file at full height the moment it is the busiest thing it has ever been, which answers a question nobody asked while destroying the one comparison a file list is for.

**A sparkline bucket with no writes draws a track, and a file with no churn is the all-empty case.** This paragraph used to end *"a file with no churn draws no strip at all rather than an empty one, **so it spends none of its row**"*, and the clause after the comma is what made it wrong: the reserved-slot ruling earlier in this section takes the slot from the pane whether or not a row can fill it, so the row is spent either way and "nothing" bought exactly what "a track" costs. Replaced 2026-08-03 ([#78](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/78)). §5.1 carries the ruling and why the track is `_` rather than the ramp's floor.

**The heat strip's empty case is not the same case, and its opposite ruling stands.** `heat_at` draws nothing for a file with no line diff at all, because a strip of pure track there would say *"this file is in the diff and I cannot tell you where"*. That is an **epistemic gap**: the row asserts the file changed, and the strip cannot place it. A sparkline's empty state is a **fact** the store actually holds, that nothing has been written since the watch opened, so the two elements draw opposite conclusions from the same-looking input and both are honest. The distinction is what keeps #78 from being an argument for changing the strip too.

**And no glance element may take a heading below twelve columns of path.** The counters, the pulse, the strip and the sparkline are all placed from the right, against a floor the path keeps; which of them survives a given width is the layout table's business rather than a per-row allocation, and the order is the one recorded above. A row reduced to `M …` would have stopped naming its own file, which is the truncated-to-useless shape I6 forbids, arrived at by decoration rather than by narrowing.

**CLI.** One optional positional path, defaulting to the working directory. **One flag, `--version` (and `-V`), which prints `vigia <version>` and exits successfully**, and every other argument beginning with `-` is refused with one line naming that fact rather than being taken as a path, so `vigia --colour=never` is told what the surface is instead of being told `--colour=never` is not a repository. **A second argument is refused too**, which this line said implicitly and nothing enforced until [#12](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/12): the classifier saw only the first argument, so `vigia . --colour=never` watched `.` and dropped the flag in silence, which is a worse answer than the refusal a flag gets alone because the tool starts and draws and so appears to have accepted it. Arity is part of the surface, so the classifier takes the whole argument list rather than one of it. That is not `--help` implemented; B6 leaves the question of whether it should be, and rules everything else about this line, including why the version query is not the thing it forbids. The version is `CARGO_PKG_VERSION`, so the string cannot drift from the manifest the release was cut from, and the classification lives in the library rather than in `main.rs` because §7 makes the test suite the proof and a test cannot import a `main.rs`.

**Configuration is a theme file and one environment variable**, which is B6 ruled 2026-07-31 and amended the same day. The CLI gains nothing either way.

**A palette is a preference, so it lives in a file.** `~/.config/vigia/theme` is read when nothing overrides it, resolved from `HOME` or `USERPROFILE`, which is **one** rule rather than one per platform: no XDG matrix, no `%APPDATA%` special case, no discovery crate. `VIGIA_THEME` still names a built-in or points at a file, and still wins, because a variable is how you say "not this time" without editing anything.

**A colour depth is not a preference, it is a property of the terminal you are in right now.** One machine, one user, one afternoon: this pane is truecolour, that one is an `ssh` into something ancient, the third is CI. A file gives all three the same answer and is wrong for two of them. So `VIGIA_COLOR` stays a variable, which is the same reasoning that makes `NO_COLOR` one.

**A font is the same kind of fact as a colour depth, so `VIGIA_GLYPHS` is a second variable and not a second setting.** Added 2026-08-17 ([#159](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/159)). It takes `block`, `braille`, `octant` or `auto`, and it overrides a detection that reads the terminal's identity exactly as the one above does. **B6's count is unchanged by it, for B6's own reason**: that ruling counts one environment variable of *configuration*, which is `VIGIA_THEME`, and a variable saying what the terminal in front of you can draw configures nothing. The test is the one the paragraph above states, and a font passes it the same way a depth does: a file would give three panes on one machine the same answer and be wrong for two of them. **Refused rather than ignored** when the value is not one of the four, on `VIGIA_COLOR`'s rule that a reader who set a variable and got no effect cannot guess that it was silently discarded.

The amendment costs less than B6 assumed because **the format and the parser already existed**: `VIGIA_THEME` has always been able to point at a theme file. What was added is a place to look, not a surface. And it removes a step rather than adding one, since a preference set once now survives a new shell.

**And the monitor writes nothing** — not into the worktree, not into a state directory, not into `.git`.

B7 ruled 2026-08-05, and it is stated here because it was load-bearing in two places before it was a rule in any: §6 makes the settle margin a table lookup rather than a measurement on the grounds that measuring would need a probe write, and §10's settle-margin bullet rests the whole 2-second constant on *"a monitor never writes, so it only ever sees the gaps its user's tools happened to leave"*. Both arguments were true because `vigia` happens to open no file for writing, which is nobody's decision. A cache, a lock file or a crash dump added in good faith would have made two recorded rulings quietly wrong, and nothing would have said so.

**It is a rule about the filesystem, and deliberately not about what the process emits.** Bytes going to the terminal this program draws into are a different surface with different costs, and reading this rule as covering them would foreclose questions it has no business deciding: [#120](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/120) asks about OSC 8 links on paths and a yank key over OSC 52, both pure emission, and it is right to treat those as outside this line rather than settled by it. Where the two meet is that neither may become a *reason* to write: a clipboard fallback needing a temp file, or a link needing a resolved path cached to disk, is this rule's business again. **Both were ruled 2026-08-15 and both were declined, as §11.2 B8 and B9** — which is where a reader following this pointer should go, and neither decline rests on this rule, exactly as this paragraph required.

**The mechanism a reader revisiting B7 needs, because it is the part that is not obvious.** A log inside the worktree is not untidy, it is a **self-sustaining wake loop**: the write is a filesystem event, the event is a tick, the tick is a frame, and the frame writes again, at frame rate, for as long as the process lives. `vigia-core`'s event filter decides how visible that is, and of the four places a write can land it hides three. A path *outside* the worktree fails `strip_prefix` and is dropped. A path inside it and **gitignored** is dropped by the exclude rules. A path inside `.git` is dropped unless its file name is `index`, which is the one file there that changes a diff. A path inside the worktree and **not** ignored is accepted. So the guard a writing monitor would need is not a convention about where the file goes; it is the worktree root resolved before the file is opened, and a refusal — which is a behaviour, not a detail, and is one of the five B7 counts.

**Where an instrument goes instead, since declining one is not the same as having none.** The harness, which is where every other measurement of this product already lives: the soak is `vigia::run` with the terminal removed (§7), and `VIGIA_BUDGET_SLACK` is the standing precedent for a variable the tests read and the binary does not, so a measurement switch can cost the product surface nothing. What the *reader* gets is the footer's own readouts, which is the other half of the same split and is §5.1's business rather than this paragraph's.

Gated by `crates/vigia/tests/writes.rs`, over every entry under a fixture root with `.git` included and the root itself stamped, across a first paint, six reader actions, a second tick and the grammar warmer **joined** rather than detached, because a write that lands after the comparison is a gate that passed by racing. One thing a reading could have got wrong was checked rather than assumed: `gix`'s status walk is never asked to write the index — `write_index` is called nowhere in `crates/` — and the gate confirms it empirically, so a monitor that reads a working tree many times a second leaves it byte for byte as it found it. Both directions are tested, per §7: a sibling gate writes a file and asserts the comparison **reports** it, because a comparator that cannot say "something moved" has not been tested.

### How colour degrades

`SPEC.md` §5 makes shape and colour the whole differentiator, so a palette that assumed 24-bit colour would be a product that silently stops working on the terminals that do not have it. §10 named that and left it open. This is the answer, and it is **two axes rather than a fallback chain**.

**The palette decides what may be drawn. The depth decides how finely it can be expressed.** Both have to allow an element before it appears, and they genuinely disagree: `ansi` refuses a row wash at *every* depth, because a wash has to assume a background and that palette's entire contract is that it assumes none, while `dark` draws one wherever the depth can express it.

Three palettes ship. **`ansi` is the default**, unchanged from what shipped before there was a choice: every colour in it is one of the sixteen *names*, so it resolves to whatever the reader's own terminal scheme says and `vigia` matches the pane beside it instead of fighting it. It is the only palette that is correct on a background nothing has detected, and detecting one needs a tty round-trip this shell does not make. `dark` is `assets/preview.svg` read out of the file rather than approximated. `light` is the same design re-picked at light-background luminance, with every ramp reversed, because on white it is the dark end that has contrast to spend.

The depth ladder, and what each rung loses:

| Rung | Foreground | Background |
|---|---|---|
| truecolour | 24-bit, as authored | as authored |
| 256 | quantised to the xterm palette | **dropped** |
| 16 | nearest of the sixteen names | dropped |
| none | dropped | dropped |

**A background needs 24-bit and a foreground does not**, and that asymmetry is the one thing here worth arguing with. §5.1 already records that a quantised background is a solid block rather than a tint: the darkest available green behind a line of code is a slab, and a slab destroys the syntax colours sitting on it. Worse than no tint. So the row wash needs the top rung while the text goes all the way down to sixteen, and below it the diff signal narrows back to the sigil column exactly as §11.1 recorded before #11.

**That boundary sat at 256 for a phase and it took a screen to move.** The cube has six levels per axis and its darkest two are 0 and 95, so a *subtle* colour has nowhere to land: `#1b3d29` quantises to `#005f00` and `#45222a` to `#5f0000`, saturated primaries at roughly two and a half times the authored luminance. Over a hunk that reads as a strong tint and the report would have been "the colour looks wrong". Over a **newly added file, where every row is an addition**, it is a screenful of flat green, and §5's whole claim is that colour carries signal. Reported 2026-08-03 with two photographs of it. The argument was already written here as the reason Windows detects 24-bit rather than 256; what was missing was applying it to the rung itself.

**The grey ramp is not the escape**, and it is worth naming because it is the obvious next idea. It is far nearer in distance than the cube, and it is where the quantiser would send a desaturated wash if its chroma gate did not stop it. But `#1b3d29` and `#45222a` average to the *same* grey, so an added row and a removed row would be one colour, which is the one thing §5 says may never happen at any rung that has colour at all. There is no honest wash below 24-bit. A reader whose terminal draws more than detection can prove says so with `VIGIA_COLOR`, and inside `tmux` that also needs the multiplexer to pass 24-bit through rather than approximate it.

Detection is a precedence chain, first answer wins: `VIGIA_COLOR`, then `NO_COLOR`, then `TERM=dumb`, then `COLORTERM` claiming 24-bit, then **`TERM_PROGRAM`**, then **Windows with `WT_SESSION`**, then `TERM` promising 24-bit, then `TERM` containing `256color`, then **Windows**, then sixteen.

**A terminal that names itself sits above `TERM`, and that ordering was learned rather than designed.** Git Bash and MSYS export `TERM=xterm-256color`, so a chain that read `TERM` first sent the most common shell for this repo to 256, where a subtle colour has nowhere to land: the xterm cube's darkest axis levels are 0 and 95 with nothing between, so a row wash of `#1b3d29` quantises to `#005f00`, a saturated primary rather than a tint. A terminal that names itself is better evidence than a variable describing a terminfo entry.

**That ruling was written for `WT_SESSION` and it generalises, which is what `TERM_PROGRAM` is doing here.** The chain used to have exactly two positive signals on a Unix, and both of them fail in the arrangement §1 describes as the whole point of the tool. `COLORTERM` is the only convention for claiming 24-bit and **nothing propagates it**: `ssh` forwards `TERM` alone, and a multiplexer replaces `TERM` with an entry of its own. `tmux` at its default `default-terminal screen` is the ordinary case, and `screen` contains neither `256color` nor anything else the chain read, so a reader running an agent in one pane and `vigia` in the pane beside it landed on sixteen. Sixteen drops backgrounds by the rule above it, so `dark` coloured every foreground and washed no row, and from the outside that is a palette being ignored rather than a rung being wrong. Reported from a real macOS pane 2026-08-03, where the giveaway was the `@@` header: at sixteen `#58a6ff` resolves to `LightCyan`, and it was cyan.

`TERM_PROGRAM` survives all of it, because it names the process that owns the screen rather than the entry the innermost layer chose to advertise. **`Apple_Terminal` is the one entry in that table that is not truecolour**, and it is why the table answers with a rung rather than a yes: Terminal.app has never drawn 24-bit, it rounds the sequences to its own palette, and claiming truecolour there is the over-claim this ladder's floor exists to avoid. An under-claim only looks flatter than it had to.

**`TERM` reads two more promises than it did, both terminfo's own vocabulary.** The `-direct` suffix is what the database calls direct colour, and a handful of terminals ship an entry naming themselves (`xterm-kitty`, `xterm-ghostty`, `alacritty`, `wezterm`, `foot`, `contour`, `rio`), none of which spells `256color` and none of which has ever had a 16-colour era. A terminal that merely *usually* has truecolour is not in that list: it belongs behind `COLORTERM` or `VIGIA_COLOR`, because the list is evidence about terminals someone checked rather than a claim about the ones nobody has.

**Windows detects truecolour, and 256 was not a safe default but a different wrong one.** What it was protecting against is consoles older than Windows 10 1703, which has not been a supported target for years. A reader on something genuinely older says so with `VIGIA_COLOR`, which is what the override is for.

**`TERM` only ever promotes.** On a Unix it is the last signal rather than the whole one; on Windows it describes a world where sixteen was the floor, so reading it as a demotion would take colours away from consoles that have them. An entry that promises neither rung yields nothing and leaves the floor where it is. The floor is sixteen because it is the one answer that is never actively wrong.

**Modifiers survive every rung, including none.** `NO_COLOR` asks for no *colour*, and bold is not colour. On a monochrome terminal it is the only distinction left.

**Quantising happens once, at startup.** The palette is walked a single time and the result stored, so the frame path draws with values that already mean what this terminal can show and I9 never sees any of it.

**What a wider palette does not buy is a wider ramp.** Mapping a 24-bit colour onto sixteen entries cannot keep nine syntax classes distinct: `#ffa657` and `#e3b341` are both yellow, and that is arithmetic rather than a choice. `ansi` is the answer for a sixteen-colour terminal and hand-picks all nine. What may **never** collapse at any rung that has colour at all is the diff signal, because §5 spends it on colour, and that has its own gate.

**A theme that does not parse is reported before the screen is taken**, which is the rule B5 already states for a path that is not a repository and holds here for the same reason: an error painted inside a TUI that then hands the terminal back is an error nobody sees. An unknown key is **refused rather than ignored**, with the line it was found on, because a silently dropped key is a theme that does nothing and "it was discarded" is the one explanation a reader cannot arrive at by looking at their screen.

**A path that is not a repository exits before the screen is taken.** That is the first half of B5, and it shipped with the shell rather than being ruled first: `Worktree::discover` and the opening walk both run ahead of `Session::enter`, so the failure reaches a terminal the reader can still read. An error painted inside a TUI that then hands the terminal back is an error nobody sees. Recorded 2026-07-31, when [#40](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/40) found §11.2 still calling it proposed, which is this section's warning box in miniature.

**The first signal from outside leaves the way the quit key does: the terminal goes back, nothing is printed, and the exit status is 0.** Ruled 2026-08-08 with [#24](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/24) rather than left to fall out of the code, because it is the part of that work a reader can observe from outside the process. Nothing failed, so there is nothing to report, and a message printed after the terminal has already come back would arrive on a shell prompt the sender was not looking at. **The first ask deliberately does not re-raise** so that a parent's `wait` reports *killed by SIGTERM*: it reports *exited 0*, because from this program's point of view it was asked to stop and it stopped. If a script ever needs to tell "quit" from "killed" on the first ask, this is the line that has to change.

**The second signal takes the default disposition, so it kills the process and restores nothing.** Also ruled 2026-08-08, and it is the half that keeps the first half safe. Arming a handler takes away the guarantee the sender had before it: an unarmed `vigia` died on one `kill`, where an armed one turns that `kill` into a wake for a loop, and a loop wedged for any reason would swallow every further one. That process is unkillable short of `SIGKILL`, and reaching for `SIGKILL` produces exactly the wrecked terminal this invariant exists to prevent, so the graceful path has to have a floor under it. A reader whose first ask appears to do nothing gets, on the second, precisely what they would have got if none of this existed. The consequence is stated rather than buried: **on the second ask the terminal is not restored**, `wait` reports killed by that signal, and the I8 row names it as a by-choice exclusion.

**No dependency was needed for that, which corrects the reason this section carried for a day.** The first version of this ruling said re-raising would need `libc` on every Unix target to restore a default disposition and raise again. It does not: `signal-hook`'s `low_level::emulate_default_handler` is exactly that call, it is in the crate §6 already names, and the unique-crate count on all three tier-1 targets is unchanged by using it. The exit-0 choice for the first ask is therefore a choice about what leaving politely means, not a concession to a dependency, and saying so is the difference between a ruling and an excuse.

### 11.2 Undecided

Each carries a recommendation marked **(proposed)**, except one ruled in the same change that gave it a number, which has no earlier recommendation to carry: B7 is the first and says so where it sits. None is settled until ruled on, and none may contradict §3 — if one does, §3 wins and the recommendation is wrong. A ruled item moves to §11.1 and leaves its number behind here, because the numbers are cited elsewhere and renumbering would silently repoint those citations. **That is the rule for an item ruled *in*, and B8 through B11 are the amendment it needed**: a question ruled **no** has no behaviour to describe in §11.1, so its body stays here where the reasoning is, and §11.1 carries a pointer instead. Four consecutive items took that form, which was a convention rather than four omissions, and it is written down because the sentence above otherwise reads as unfollowed. **B10 is now the case both halves have to cover at once**, having been ruled *out* and then *in*: §11.1 carries the behaviour as the first rule says, and the body stays here as the amendment allows, because what a reversal owes a reader is the reasoning it overturned rather than only the answer it reached.

> [!NOTE]
> **This heading used to read "these gate Phase 2"**
>
> It stopped being true the moment Phase 2 closed with B3 through B6 still open,
> and it stayed on the page for a whole phase after that, because a heading
> naming a phase goes stale on a date nobody is watching. Which phase a decision
> blocks belongs in `ROADMAP.md`, the file whose job is phases; what belongs here
> is only whether a thing is decided. Corrected 2026-07-31 by
> [#40](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/40), which found it while
> ruling B3.

**B1 — What happens to follow mode when the reader scrolls. Ruled 2026-07-30: the proposal stands. See §11.1.** `less +F` semantics, on at startup, disengaged by any manual scroll, re-engaged by `f`. Rationale, kept because it is the part a later reader will want to argue with: disengage-on-scroll is the only rule that never fights a reader mid-read, and a dedicated toggle beats overloading `G`/`End`, because "jump to the last file" and "resume following" are different intents that would otherwise be the same key.

*An earlier draft of this bullet proposed `G`/`End` as the re-engage key. That contradicted the mockup, which is public and predates the question. When a published artifact already answers an open question, it is the answer — the question is only whether to keep it. It was kept.*

**B2 — Which file wins when several change at once. Ruled 2026-07-30: the proposal stands. See §11.1.** Follow the file whose write landed **last** in the settled batch, and let §5's visual pulse carry the others. Rationale: it reads "newest" literally, it is stable rather than heuristic, and the pulse already exists to say "these moved too" without moving the viewport for each.

What ruling it exposed, and what §11.1 now records: "last in the batch" is only affordable because the filesystem event names the path. Deriving it instead would mean `stat`-ing every changed file, which is [#19](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/19)'s breach, so the cheap answer and the correct one happened to coincide here rather than by design.

**B3 — The empty state. Ruled 2026-07-31: the proposal stands, with its wording corrected. See §11.1.** Zero changes is not an edge case; it is the state the tool sits in most of the time, and it is the **first** thing anyone sees when they open it beside an agent that has not written yet. A blank pane is indistinguishable from a hang.

Rationale, kept because it is the part a later reader will want to argue with: name it, with the repository, the branch, "no changes", and an explicit statement that it is watching. All four are drawn and two of them are the **header's**, which is why this and the mode word were ruled in one pass rather than separately: the mode word is what makes "and it is watching" sayable in zero extra rows, and the empty state is the screen that makes the mode word worth having.

What was corrected is the wording. The proposal said "no changes" and the shell said `working tree clean`, and both are looser than the diff underneath them: this one compares the working tree against the **index**, so a fully staged worktree has no changes here and plenty for git.

**B4 — Is the file list navigable, and is it a region? Ruled 2026-08-01: the list is a region, and it is not navigable. See §11.1.** ([#66](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/66).) Both halves, because the question as written only ever asked one of them.

*The smaller half stands unchanged.* **Not navigable**: no selection, no focus, no second mode, for the rationale below, which is kept because it is the part a later reader will want to argue with.

*The larger half is ruled the other way from the proposal.* **The file list is a region of the screen**, pinned above the diff with a rule between them, and it scrolls. What settled it is not taste: three of §5's four glance elements ride a file heading, so a single stream makes the differentiator visible only at scroll position zero, and the picture has drawn two regions since before the question existed. The list is ordered like the stream, costs one `Frame::diff` per **visible** row, tracks the diff on its own and takes `J`/`K` to move; §11.1 carries all of it, including why the diff's scrollbar is row-exact.

*(proposed, and this was the wording that hid the second half)* **Not navigable in v1** — one continuous scroll, list as map. Rationale: selection implies focus, focus implies a second mode, and modes are reviewer-class (§2). The pane is 40 columns beside an agent, not a full-screen client.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **The question above is the smaller half, and the proposal settles the larger one silently**
>
> Filed 2026-08-01 as [#66](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/66). *Navigable* is
> downstream of **whether the file list is a region of the screen at all**, and
> "one continuous scroll" answers that second question without asking it.
>
> `assets/preview.svg` draws two regions: a summary block of every changed file,
> pinned above a rule, with one file's diff beneath. The proof is in the picture
> rather than in an intention — `src/engine/watch.rs` is drawn **twice**, once in
> the block and once as the diff heading, which one stream never does. The shell
> draws one stream: [`Row`](crates/vigia/src/view.rs) is a flat enum and
> `Painter::body` is a single loop over `View::rows`, so the sparkline, the
> counters and the heat strip ride a heading **inside** the scroll.
>
> Nothing there is a defect and every element is specified in §5.1, built and
> gated. What has no ruling is the container they were drawn for, and the cost of
> that is not cosmetic: three of §5's four glanceability elements are visible only
> while their own heading is on screen, so **the glance surface disappears exactly
> while the pane is being read**. Also note "list as map" presupposes a list to be
> a map of; there is none, there are headings.
>
> Two constraints belong to §3 rather than to taste, and they are why this is not
> a rendering choice. Ranking a pinned list by diff size needs every changed
> file's diff, which is [#49](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/49)'s
> argument against a repository-wide total arriving somewhere else; ranking it
> from `History` costs nothing, since that store is already bounded and fed from
> the watch. And a heat strip needs the whole-file line count on `FileDiff`, so a
> pinned row for a file the frame did not diff cannot draw one without breaching
> I4 the same way. Whichever way it is ruled, **§5.1's own rule applies to the
> picture too**: a published artifact answering a question is the answer, so one
> answering it differently from the code is a wrong specification and not a stale
> asset.
>
> **Resolved 2026-08-01 towards the picture**, which is the direction that costs
> something: the region is built rather than the mockup corrected away. What the
> picture got wrong is smaller and is corrected in it — it drew a summary block
> of *every* changed file, and an automatic unbounded changed set cannot have
> one, so the region caps and scrolls and says so with a bar.
>
> **They agree on the layout, which is what B4 asked. They do not agree cell for
> cell, and the remaining gaps are recorded in §5.1 rather than left to be
> rediscovered**: the element split across the two regions, and the order the
> mockup places a row's right-hand side in. Neither is a promise about a region,
> which is what this question was about.

**B5 — Not a git repository, and submodules.** Neither appeared anywhere in this spec. **Half ruled 2026-07-31**, and the halves are separated here rather than one number being retired for the sake of tidiness, because only one of them is decided.

**Not a repository: ruled, and it had already shipped. See §11.1.** Exit non-zero with one line, **before** entering the alternate screen — an error painted inside a TUI that then restores the terminal is an error nobody reads. The proposal stands unchanged; what is worth recording is that `run` has ordered `Worktree::discover` ahead of `Session::enter` since the shell was built, so this sat here marked `(proposed)` for two phases while the code did it. Found by [#40](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/40) while ruling B3, which is exactly the drift §11's warning box describes and which `take-next`'s pre-flight cannot see, since it compares invariant tokens rather than behaviour.

**Submodules: still open.** *(proposed)* Out of v1, shown as an opaque directory and said so, because recursing into them costs the incremental guarantees in I2a.

**B6 — CLI surface and configuration. Ruled 2026-07-31 with theming, which is what it said it was waiting for, and amended the same day. See §11.1.** The CLI stays at one positional path and gains no flags **that configure it**. Configuration is **a theme file and one environment variable**.

**Amended 2026-08-17 by [#159](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/159): `VIGIA_GLYPHS` joins `VIGIA_COLOR` and neither is counted here.** The count is of *configuration*, and this ruling's own §11.1 text already draws the line it needs: a palette is a preference about you and belongs in a file, where a colour depth is a property of the terminal you are in right now. Which glyphs the font carries is that second kind, so a second detector gets a second override and the surface this ruling guards is untouched. Stated rather than left implicit because the headline sentence counts to one and a reader arriving at three variables would reasonably think it had drifted.

**Amended 2026-08-08 by [#12](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/12): `--version` and `-V` print the version and exit, and they are not the thing this ruling forbids.** The line said "no flags" for two phases and the word was doing more work than the argument under it, which is the drift `CLAUDE.md` rule 1 exists to catch: what the paragraphs below actually argue is that a *setting* costs the zero-input thesis, because a flag that changes what is drawn splits the tool into variants and makes "correct with no input" a claim about one of them. An **identity query** is a different kind of thing. It prints one line and exits before a terminal is taken, before the alternate screen, before anything is watched, so there is no frame it can change and no state it can leave; the set of drawn things is exactly the same on every invocation that draws at all. The test is not whether an argument begins with `-`, it is whether the process behaves differently *after* it has started drawing, and against that test a version query is not configuration in the way a hypothetical `--no-follow` would be.

**What forced it is that the release could not be smoke-tested.** `RELEASE-SMOKE.md` §2 has required `vigia --version` since 2026-08-05 and `main.rs` refused every argument beginning with `-`, so two artifacts in this repository contradicted each other and the one that would have lost is the one a user meets. That is the second half of the case and the weaker one, recorded because it is what surfaced the first: a binary that arrives through four channels (`cargo install`, a tap, two install scripts, a release page) and cannot say which build it is turns every report into an unanswerable question, and Phase 7's filter is precisely about what happens once the binary is in someone else's hands. `-V` comes with it because a user who tries one tries the other, and refusing exactly one of the two conventional spellings is a worse surface than refusing both.

**`--help` is untouched and stays open.** §11.1 has always said B6 leaves that question, and this amendment does not answer it: help text is a description of a surface, so it grows when the surface does and has to be kept true, where a version string is generated from the manifest and cannot drift. Nothing else is admitted either. An argument beginning with `-` that is not one of these two spellings is still refused with one line, which is the half of the old sentence that survives intact, and `crates/vigia/tests/cli.rs::an_option_that_is_not_a_version_query_is_still_refused` is what holds it there.

The proposal above said configuration lands *with* theming, and its own rationale is what decided the shape: *a config file with one thing in it invites a second thing*. That argument does not stop at files. A monitor is launched from the shell rc that opens the pane and then left alone for days, so the natural home for a setting made once is the line that already starts it, not a dotfile the process has to find, parse, and decide whether to watch. A file also costs a discovery rule across three platforms and a TOML parser that `SPEC.md` does not name, for a surface with two settings in it.

**Amended within the day, and the correction is worth more than the original.** The first ruling was *two environment variables and no file*, on the argument that a config file with one thing in it invites a second thing. Reading it back against a real reader: a variable has to be re-declared per shell, and the instruction for making that permanent is `$PROFILE` or `.bashrc` or `.zshrc`, so the *mechanism* is portable while the *instruction* is not. A file is set once.

What the original ruling got right is that not everything belongs in one. The split is by **what the setting is about**, not by convenience: a palette is a preference about you, and a colour depth is a fact about the terminal in front of you. The second genuinely differs between two panes on one machine, which is what a variable is for.

What is still left open, and is the intended pressure: there is nowhere to put a setting that is neither of those. §2 makes the reference `btop`, where you never configure anything, and every option added here is a behaviour that owes this section a line.

**B7 is the first candidate that pressure produced, and it was refused rather than accommodated.** That is what this line predicted would have to happen and it is not a resolution of it: the gap is exactly where it was, and the next setting that is neither a preference about you nor a fact about the terminal in front of you will find it again.

**B7 — Does the monitor write an observation log? Ruled 2026-08-05: no. See §11.1.** ([#117](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/117).) B6's pressure arriving with a concrete proposal behind it: `VIGIA_OBSERVE=<path>`, off by default, appending per-tick aggregates — frame p99, changed-file count, depth rung, follow transitions — to a file **outside** the worktree, so that the week of real use [#72](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/72) needs leaves numbers rather than memories.

**It is the first entry here with no `(proposed)` line, and that is deliberate rather than an omission.** B1 through B6 were written into this section open and ruled later, so each keeps the recommendation it was carrying. This one arrived as a tracked question and was ruled in the same change that gave it a number, so there is no earlier recommendation to preserve; what a later reader will want to argue with is the adopt case below, which is recorded in full for exactly that reason.

**The adopt case, recorded because it will be raised again and because it is not weak.** Off by default; the path resolved the way the theme file's already is; append-only, flushed on frames that already happened, so no wake is invented and I1 is untouched; an invariant-shaped budget for the cost when enabled and a structural gate proving that disabled costs nothing. What it would buy is real: the changed-file count over time is the input **every** deferred cost on the shelf is a function of, and four shelf entries are ranked today on judgements about it rather than on numbers. And this document's own method is that a claim without a measurement is a wish, which is an argument for instruments and not against them. The nearest precedent went the other way: refusing the footer's memory readout was recommended on a similar economy argument in [#41](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/41) and was **overruled**, correctly.

**Declined, on five things that were checked rather than argued.**

1. **It does not answer the question the week exists for.** Of #72's five items, *"any disagreement with `git diff`"* needs an oracle and *"what was reached for and not found"* needs a person. The second one **is** the question [#50](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/50) asks, and #50 is the row #72 exists to unblock. An aggregate of frame times and file counts reaches neither, so the log buys the cheap half of the week and leaves the expensive half exactly where it was.
2. **The cheap half is not `vigia`'s to measure.** How many files are dirty at once is a property of the **worktree**, so anything that can read the worktree measures it already: `git status --porcelain`, counted, on a timer, in a pane. I1 constrains this program; it does not constrain a shell loop sitting beside it. That the highest-value number in the proposal needs no product code at all is the finding that decided this.
3. **It is neither of B6's two kinds of setting.** A palette is a preference about you and a colour depth is a fact about the terminal in front of you; a log is a fact about a measurement somebody is running once. B6's closing line named that gap before there was a candidate for it, and answering the pressure by widening the surface is the one answer B6's own rationale rules out.
4. **The cost is not one variable.** A write target needs a state directory *created* where the theme file needs one *read*; a policy for a path that is unwritable or a disk that fills, since a monitor that dies of its own logging is worse than one with no logging; a growth policy over the days I3 is a claim about, and §10's hour ran **73,265 frames**, which is 20.35 a second, so a 64-byte line is **4.47 MiB an hour and 107 MiB over I3's own 24-hour window**, which needs rotation (a second mechanism) or a cap (data loss at the tail); a flush inside I9's budget; a budget this document would then have to name; and a refusal of any path under the worktree, for the wake-loop reason §11.1 now carries. Five behaviours owing §11.1 a line, for an instrument used once.
5. **An instrument for measuring this product already has a home, and it is not the product.** The soak is `vigia::run` with the terminal removed (§7), and `VIGIA_BUDGET_SLACK` is the standing precedent for a variable the tests read and the binary does not. So "there is no way to measure this without a product surface" is the one thing the proposal needed to be true and is false. §11.1 carries where that leaves the reader's own numbers.

**What declining costs, stated rather than smoothed over.** Three things, and the first is what to reopen this on if daily use asks. The changed-file histogram at *frame* resolution, which point 2's sampler replaces at one-second resolution — adequate only because the question is how many files are dirty at once, which is a state that persists, rather than how spiky the arrivals are. The *"was it ever over 16ms"* the footer cannot answer, because `Samples` is a 128-frame ring and a breach rolls off it. And follow-transition counts, which nothing else can see, and which nothing has asked for.

**A variant inside the existing surface, refused on a recorded principle.** A max-ever frame time drawn in the footer would cost no configuration at all, and it fails where [#49](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/49)'s deferred total and the pulse's elapsed-time decay failed: a number that only rises says *"something was slow once, hours ago"* forever, which is a stale claim that looks live. The pulse escaped by being redefined against **event identity**; a maximum has no such escape, because it is defined by history rather than by anything on screen.

**And one of the four fields proposed is a constant.** The depth rung resolves once at startup and never moves, so logging it per tick would record one value for a week. That nothing on screen reports it either is a real gap, and a separate one.

**B8 and B9 arrived as one question and are two.** ([#120](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/120), ruled 2026-08-15.) That issue proposed OSC 8 hyperlinks on drawn paths and a `y` key putting the current path on the clipboard over OSC 52, together, as "outbound affordances that write nothing", and offered one property as the gate for both: *what a terminal that supports neither sees is nothing different*. **That property is true of the first and false of the second**, which is the finding that split them. A terminal that ignores OSC 8 renders the same cells; a terminal that ignores OSC 52 leaves the reader holding whatever was in the clipboard before, and nothing anywhere can say so. Two affordances whose failure modes point in opposite directions do not share a ruling, and the numbers are separate so that reopening one cannot drag the other with it. Both are declined, and B7 above is what makes that a real decision rather than a restatement: **the writes-nothing rule is about the filesystem and does not reach either of these**, so neither is declined on it.

**B8 — OSC 8 hyperlinks on drawn paths. Ruled 2026-08-15: no, and the ground is what it costs the instruments rather than what it costs the reader.** It costs the reader nothing, which is the honest half of the proposal: the sequence is emittable under `ratatui` (`ratatui-core`'s own `merge_diff_link` and `merge_diff_split_link` are exactly this shape, the bytes going into a cell's *symbol* with `CellDiffOption::ForcedWidth` carrying the real display width and a long link split so the opener and closer sit in the first and last cell), it changes no drawn cell, and a reader who never discovers it loses nothing. **It is deliberately not declined on discoverability**, which is the tempting wrong reason and the one the issue itself reached for: an undrawn link is not the same shape as an undrawn *instruction*, so §11.1's ruling against drawing the digits' rank ([#149](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/149)) does not reach it and must not be cited as though it did.

What it costs is measured, and it lands on the gates. **A linked path measures 46 columns instead of 1**, because `unicode-width` gives every character in the sequence a width — the two `ESC`s included, at one column each — rather than recognising any of it as a control sequence, and `Span::raw` is how `occupied`, `rows_at` and `legibility.rs`'s whole 1..=120 sweep all measure a row. Worse, `TestBackend`'s `Display` **is** every snapshot in this repository, and a linked cell renders there as `]8;;file:///…/src/main.rs\M]8;;\`, so adopting would commit raw escape sequences and the path with them into `.snap` files.

**Both costs were priced by building it rather than by predicting it, and the number depends on the URI, which is itself a finding.** Wrapping the path in `Painter::file_row` with a **repository-relative** URI and running the workspace fails **29 tests across four binaries**: 19 in `render.rs`, 8 in `legibility.rs`, and one each in `follow.rs` and `rows.rs`, with the linked cell measuring 46. With the **absolute** URI a `file://` link actually needs, it is **35 across five binaries** and the cell measures 53, `palette.rs` joining because the absolute path reaches a fixture assertion. So the honest figure for an adoption is the larger one, and the smaller one is what a session reproducing this from a relative path will see. Neither set is about hyperlinks: they are the width ladder, the collapse order, the sparkline and heat gates and the snapshot suite, every one reading a cell that now claims a width it does not have. **The absolute form is also what puts a machine path into a committed `.snap` file**, which `CLAUDE.md`'s house rules forbid outright, so the shape that is cheapest to gate is the one that does not work.

**And the painter cannot emit it at all, which is the cost nobody had priced.** `ratatui`'s `set_stringn` filters graphemes with `!symbol.contains(char::is_control)`, so every escape written through `Painter::put` is **silently dropped** and what reaches the screen is the payload as visible text: the first attempt at the mutation above drew `]8;;file:///crates/…` into the row and reddened 23 tests across five binaries, two of them in `legibility.rs`, for a reason that had nothing to do with hyperlinks. A link therefore has to be written onto the cell with `set_symbol`, after `put` has run, which is a **second drawing route for path cells that bypasses the width ladder, the elision rule and the edge marking** every other drawn label goes through. What that route costs is the property `crates/vigia/src/render.rs`'s `Painter::list` states as *"one drawer, one degradation ladder, one set of gates, and no way for the two regions to disagree"*, and which §11.1 carries as its own rule that a list row draws exactly what a heading draws.

Against all of that, the benefit cannot be measured from here at all: a `file://` URI hands the path to the reader's **system handler**, so whether a Ctrl-click opens anything they want is a fact about their machine, and B6 forecloses the configuration key that would let them aim it. A measured cost to the instruments against an unmeasured benefit is the trade §7 exists to refuse, and refusing it here is that section applied to its own gates rather than to the product.

**Reopen it on `ratatui` carrying a hyperlink as cell *metadata* rather than as bytes inside the symbol**, which would delete both measurements above and with them this entire ruling; or on [#72](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/72) reporting readers reaching for files and finding no route. The terminal survey the issue asks for is a cost of **adopting** rather than of declining, which is why declining did not need it: a decline is safe under every possible survey result, and only an adoption depends on which one it is.

**B9 — a yank key over OSC 52. Ruled 2026-08-15: no, and the ground is that it would be the first key on this map to destroy something the reader owns.** Not the first *irreversible* one, and the distinction is worth keeping rather than smoothing: `q`, `Esc`, `Ctrl-C` and `Ctrl-D` quit, which no keypress undoes either. What separates them is whose state they spend. Quitting ends this program and touches nothing else; every remaining binding moves a viewport or toggles drawn state and is undone by pressing something else. The system clipboard is **shared mutable state other programs read**, so `y` would overwrite whatever the reader had there, on one unmodified keystroke, with no undo and no warning, and the thing destroyed is not `vigia`'s to spend.

**And `vigia` could not tell them it had worked.** OSC 52 has no reply, so the only mechanism that could confirm the write is OSC 52's own query form, which reads the reader's clipboard back — a privacy escalation far past the affordance. So the program can neither report success honestly nor fail visibly. A `copied` notice would be a claim it cannot support, and it would also **outstay its subject**: a notice is cleared by the next `Wake::Tick` and by nothing else, and a keypress produces no tick, so a notice raised by `y` would sit on the footer until the worktree next changed, which on an idle tree is never. That is a sharper objection than a clock, and it is the one that holds: I1 is not needed to refuse this. The dependency is the smallest of the reasons and is recorded because the issue claimed the opposite: `crossterm` does ship this, as `clipboard::CopyToClipboard`, but behind an `osc52` feature that is `dep:base64`, and `base64` is not in the graph today.

**The case for it is stronger than the issue made and is recorded here rather than dropped, because it is what this will come back on.** The proposal argued convenience. The real argument is that **`vigia` has already taken the ordinary route away and truncated the replacement**: `Session::enter` takes `EnableMouseCapture` unconditionally, so a native drag-select depends on whatever bypass modifier the reader's terminal happens to offer, and at the forty columns I6 is named for the path on screen is *elided* — `render__the_glance_elements_at_forty_columns.snap` draws `M …ngine/watch.rs` through the same `Painter::file_row` the list uses, so even a successful selection yields a string that is not the path. On the pane this tool is built for there is no route to the real path at all, and that is a gap this program created. It loses today because no reader has reported it ([#72](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/72) has one recorded session) and because a first irreversible key is a poor instrument for discovering whether a gap bites. **Reopen it on [#72](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/72) showing that it does** — and if it does, OSC 52 is not the only shape worth considering, so this ruling deliberately does not pre-commit the answer to the question it is inviting.

**Each has its own gate, and one gate could not have held both.** `crates/vigia/tests/legibility.rs::a_drawn_path_carries_no_escape_sequence_of_its_own` holds B8. It does not try to name a hyperlink, which is not nameable from drawn output, since a correctly rendered link looks exactly like a path; it forbids the **mechanism**, an escape smuggled into a cell, which is B8's only workable route. `crates/vigia/tests/input.rs::the_yank_key_is_refused_rather_than_unbound` holds B9, and it exists because the first one cannot: **an OSC 52 write draws nothing.** It touches no cell and leaves every buffer identical, so the entire rendering suite stays green while B9 is violated, and a ruling whose only tripwire is a drawing gate would report that nothing had happened. The keymap is where B9 is decidable, because a clipboard write needs a key to arrive on. That the two rulings needed two gates for the same reason they needed two numbers — their effects land in different places — is the clearest evidence available that splitting them was right.

**B10 — a hover highlight on what the pointer is over. Ruled 2026-08-15: no. Reversed 2026-08-16: yes. See §11.1.** ([#123](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/123); the build is [#186](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/186), filed apart so the ruling did not wait behind an implementation.) The body stays here rather than moving out with the behaviour, because this entry was ruled twice and what a reader needs is not the answer but **how a conclusion survived both of the reasons it was given**. Each reason was checked only when it was raised, and neither was checked again once it had done its work.

**The first reason was I1, and it was found void inside the ruling that used it.** The issue framed hover as a trade against the idle-cost invariant, and §5.3 stated the same thing as settled. §3 already has to say it: `crossterm`'s `EnableMouseCapture` writes `?1000h ?1002h ?1003h ?1015h ?1006h` as one bundle, and `?1003h` is *any-event tracking*, every pointer movement whether or not a button is held. Those events arrive as `Wake::Input` today, and the cost is larger than a wake: `continue` leaves that wake and not the batch, so `sample_memory`, `shell.draw` and `record_frame` all run once the batch loop closes, and a batch of nothing but motion already performs a memory read, a whole-frame paint and a recorded frame ([#154](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/154)). **Nor can any of it be given back**: dropping `1003` and keeping `1000`, `1002` and `1006` is not portably available, because on Windows `EnableMouseCapture::is_ansi_code_supported()` is `false` and the bundle goes through the console API writing zero bytes. So the cost was wholly sunk in both directions, and it still is **for the mark**: it adds no wake, no paint and no frame, only an `Option` of a target and a style on cells that were being redrawn anyway. **What the build added, and this ruling did not price, is the focus step's own traffic**: asking for `?1004h` makes `FocusGained` and `FocusLost` arrive where they did not, at a wake and a frame each, and only one of the two retires anything. §11.1 carries it. That is a small bill and it is the ruling's to own rather than the build's, because the clearing ladder is what asked for the step.

**The second reason was substituted after the first collapsed, and it is the one this reversal turns on: *nothing would ever tell it to turn off*.** It read *"no mouse protocol reports a pointer leaving, the takeover does not enable focus reporting, and on an idle tree nothing repaints"*. **The middle clause described a line this repository had not written, phrased as a property of the world.** `crossterm` has shipped `EnableFocusChange` (`?1004h`) and `Event::FocusLost` for years, this keymap already handles the event and has simply never asked for it, and asking is one step in `TAKEOVER`. And the outer clause was answered closer to home than anybody looked: `?1003h` reports motion at **cell** granularity, so leaving row 5 arrives as entering row 6. Inside the pane nothing was ever needed. The staleness the decline described could only ever have been about leaving the pane, and it was written as though it were about everything.

**The first clause is false too, and this pass let it stand for a draft, which is worth recording rather than quietly fixing.** *"No mouse protocol reports a pointer leaving"* is refuted by the same xterm document this ruling leans on for `?1004h`: DEC Locator's **DECEFR** defines a filter rectangle and generates an event whenever the locator is detected *outside* it, which is a mouse protocol reporting a pointer leaving a region and nothing else. It is out of reach here, and that is the true sentence: `crossterm` exposes no DEC Locator, it needs `DECELR` first, and adopting it would be a second mouse mechanism inside I8's takeover. **The point is not the protocol.** This ruling exists because an absolute about the outside world was asserted and never checked, and the audit of it re-asserted a different absolute about the outside world in the paragraph doing the diagnosing, for the same reason: it was the clause that had already done its work. A quantifier is where the next one will hide too.

**What the reversal pass added is the size of what is left, and it is a multiplexer default rather than a terminal gap.** No terminal that was checked is missing the mode. **tmux is: its `focus-events` defaults to off**, so on a reader running §1's two panes under tmux the rung that clears a mark on leaving the window is absent until they turn it on, and the residual case below is ordinary for that reader rather than exotic. That is a fact the decline would have been entitled to, and it is stated here because §11.1's ladder turns on it. `RULINGS.md`'s B10 section carries the survey behind both sentences and is the only copy: it is a claim about other people's software, it will date, and one place to correct it is the point of that file.

**So the clearing is a ladder rather than a wall, and the residual rung is accepted.** §11.1 states the three. What the residual costs is one mark left on the last target the pointer crossed, and what it buys is the constraint that pays for it: **a hover mark is the quietest thing that is still visible in its region.** A mark that quiet costs a glance nothing when it is stale, which is the property the decline was right to demand and wrong to think unreachable, and it self-corrects at the only moment it matters, since a pointer coming back is the thing the mark exists for. Refusing the whole affordance to avoid a mark nobody is looking at is the trade this reverses.

**The pressed mark and the hover mark are ruled here as one question, which the reopening asked for and was half right about.** It said the shell already draws *"exactly the thing B10 forbids"*, meaning [#166](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/166)'s lit step button, and **B10 as written did not forbid it**: the clearing reason does not reach a gesture with an observable end, and the confinement rule below does not reach a mark on the scrollbar, because the bar is chrome rather than a body region. That last is a citation and not an assumption: §11.1 rules it in the same paragraph that keeps the thumb the only lit thing on the column, *"a button is chrome, in the bar's own dim style rather than the thumb's"*. Saying so matters, because an inconsistency stated too widely gets fixed too widely. The line that does hold is the mechanical one the reopening named itself: **a press ends** with an `Up`, and `Held::ends` covers a lost one, so its mark needs no ladder; **a hover has no end at all**, only a change of position, so it needs one. That is a difference in what the program can observe, not in taste.

**What B10 did reach is a hover mark on a list row, and that rule is amended rather than dropped.** It said *"facts about `vigia` itself are confined to the chrome, and hover would be the first mark inside a body region that is about an input device"*, which was the narrow survivor after the tempting generalisation (that every drawn mark is a fact about the worktree) was falsified by §11.1's own words. The amendment: **a mark about an input device is licensed inside a body region on a surface the reader's pointer can act on**, and is bounded by the quietness rule above. That keeps what the original was protecting, which is that the body is where the worktree is read, while ending a confinement that would have refused acknowledgment on the one region where a reader has to aim at a one-row target. **It says *the pointer* rather than *a click* deliberately**, and `RULINGS.md`'s I1 entry is why: the last amendment made here under pressure was written as narrowly as the case that forced it, and blocked identical later cases. The case is four rows above this one in `ROADMAP.md`. [#177](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/177) is a **drag** in the diff body, so a licence keyed to a click would have made *is a press that begins a drag a click* a question somebody had to answer before they could know whether a mark was allowed there. Hover's own scope is narrower than the licence and is stated in §11.1 rather than derived from it, so widening the licence does not quietly widen the feature. [#149](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/149)'s taxonomy is still deliberately not borrowed: a hover mark is *location* rather than *instruction* under it, so that ruling does not reach hover in either direction.

**Both branches, and the losing one first because it is the one that will be raised again.** On an idle monitor the pointer is more often parked than aimed: a reader typing in the agent's pane leaves the mouse wherever it was, frequently over this one, and a lit row then means *the mouse happens to be here*. The residual rung is where that case is strongest and a default tmux is where it lands. It is unevidenced in both directions and stays labelled so — §2 says interaction is *"rare or never"* and says nothing about where a resting pointer sits, while every mouse defect §11.1 records from use describes somebody **aiming**, which is the only evidence anywhere on this question and points the other way. The case for is that acknowledgment before action is most of what "snappy" means with a pointer, and that it costs an `Option` and a style.

**The gate that held the decline is the gate that survives it, and that is not a coincidence.** `crates/vigia/tests/input.rs::pointer_motion_over_a_laid_out_screen_is_still_no_action` asserts that motion over a laid-out screen produces no *action*, and it stays green through [#186](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/186) because **hover is view state and never an action** (§11.1). So the assertion written to catch a hover being built now keeps hover out of the keymap, and its message was rewritten rather than its subject.
