Two others are unchanged and both matter: `nothing_a_reader_did_not_ask_for_becomes_an_action` is the weaker sibling and says why in its own comment, since `Regions::default()` is a screen every region-gated arm returns `None` against; and `crates/vigia/src/terminal.rs::every_command_is_the_escape_sequence_it_is_named_for` asserts the mouse bundle byte for byte, `?1003h` included, so the sunk cost above stops being true loudly rather than quietly. **The gate #123 originally asked for is still not written**, for the reason the decline gave: it wanted *"zero paints for motion over a non-target"* and the honest count is one paint per motion batch, so writing it would assert something untrue until [#154](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/154) lands.

**B11 — a speaking rule: the diff's heading embedded in the border line between the regions. Ruled 2026-08-15: no, and on what the heading *is* rather than on when it is there.** ([#124](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/124).) The proposal is the terminal's oldest space-saver, `─ src/engine/watch.rs ● ────`, costed as a relocation: the diff *"draws its heading as a full row"* below the rule, so folding it into the line *"gains one body row"*. **A heading here is not a line of text.** It is a `Painter::file_row`, drawing the kind letter, the path, the pulse, the heat strip, the sparkline and the counts through the one drawer §11.1 rules for both regions, on the one degradation ladder. Folding that into a border line has two branches and each spends something already ruled: **drop the glance elements**, and the pinned occurrence stops being what a list row is, which is the property B8 states as *"one drawer, one degradation ladder, one set of gates, and no way for the two regions to disagree"*; or **keep them**, and the rule is a full glance row with dashes at the ends, which is not a rule. Neither branch is a relocation, and neither depends on where the reader has scrolled to.

**The row it would reclaim is real, and more often than the first draft of this ruling said**, which is worth recording because the correction cuts against the decline rather than for it. That draft said the heading is under the rule *only at scroll zero*. `App::jump_to` lands every jump on the file's heading, its own words being *"the resolution every jump on this map shares: `g`, `G`, a click on a listed file, a digit, and `n`/`p`"*, and follow is on before anything is touched, which is I5. So the row is there at startup, after every jump, and on a followed tick, and it is absent only after a *relative* scroll: `j`/`k`, `d`/`u`, a page, a wheel, a drag. **[#257](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/257) narrows the followed-tick clause and nothing else**: follow lands inside a file whose busiest change is not drawn from its heading, so on a tall diff being actively written the row under the rule is a hunk header rather than a heading. That cuts the same way the correction below does, against how often the saving is available, which is against adopting rather than for it. §2 prices those as the rare interactions. **On a monitor being watched rather than read, the saving is available most of the time**, and that is the strongest form of the case for adopting. What is true of the frames in between is only that the row under the rule is then content, a hunk header or a note, so a rule that spoke would be pinning a heading no longer otherwise on screen: a sticky heading, which is new state rather than a moved row.

**And B4 already answered stickiness the other way, by construction rather than by preference.** *The heading scrolls away, so you lose track of which file you are in* is the reason recorded above for building the pinned list at all, since three of the four glance elements ride a `Row::File` heading and are visible in one stream only while that heading is. What makes that airtight rather than merely usual is `Body::split`, which sets `rule: list > 0`: **the rule and the list are coextensive**, so there is no state anywhere with a rule and no list to answer the question the rule would be answering. (Below `affords_caret`'s seventeen columns the list draws without a caret, which is far under I6's forty.) **What this ruling deliberately does not rest on is counting carriers.** An earlier draft argued that the caret and the footer's `N/M` already carry the current file so a rule would be a third, and that proves too much: §11.1 rules restatement *in* where it makes a map rather than a second opinion, a stream heading is already a second full-row carrier of what a list row carries, and the three carry three different facts — a path, a row, an ordinal. The footer's index is also not unconditional, since `state_rungs` drops `N/M` a rung before the follow marker on narrow panes, and §11.1 calls it *"the half a reader can reconstruct"*.

**What reopens it is not #125, and saying so is the correction that matters most for whoever reads this next.** An earlier draft named #125 on the ground that a speaking rule *"earns where many regions compete for identity"*. #125 proposes no such thing: its widest rung turns the list from a strip above the diff into a **left rail**, which is the same two regions rearranged, and it says outright *"Not the borders — #124 owns the chrome question … Ruled apart they still stand alone."* A left rail has no horizontal rule at all, so #125 does not reopen B11, it **dissolves** it: the subject stops existing and the question becomes a different one. What would genuinely reopen this is [#72](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/72) measuring the quantity the ruling turns on — how much of a real session is spent at a file boundary against scrolled inside one — because that is what decides whether the row is usually there, and the structural objection in the first paragraph is what a reopening has to answer first.

**Declining costs the picture nothing, which the issue made a deliverable of the other branch.** `assets/preview.svg` draws the rule at `y=178` and the heading at `y=202`, two separate rows, so the artifact and the shell already agree and no departure is created. The count of standing departures is left alone here rather than asserted, because §5.1 records a first, a second, a third and a fourth while two other sentences still say the total is two, and reconciling that is its own question ([#156](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/156)). `crates/vigia/tests/render.rs::the_rule_separates_the_regions_and_spans_the_pane` is what fails if the rule ever speaks: it asserts the rule row **equals** the rule glyph repeated across the pane at 40, 64 and 120 columns, and asserts the rows above and below do not contain that glyph, so a title in the line reddens it on the first character.

**B12 — where the keymap lives once it outgrows the hint bar: a gestures sheet drawn over the pane. Ruled 2026-08-17: yes, on a ground that is structural rather than spatial. Shipped the same day. See §11.1.** ([#167](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/167); the build is [#206](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/206).) `?` toggles a centred, bordered sheet of the keymap, composited over cells that are already drawn. *It buys space* is true and is the weaker half of the case. The stronger half is that **an overlay is the only form of a cheatsheet that cannot move content**, and §11.1 has already ruled that a transient thing may not: the footer refuses to grow for a notice so that a file vanishing between being named and being read cannot jog a reader's diff. Every answer that lives in the footer runs at that rule from the other side, because more items is more width, more width brings the second footer line at more pane widths, and the second line takes a row off the diff. That route is not merely tight, it is **full**: [#121](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/121) measured the eighty-column footer at 40 columns of hints, 2, 19 of readouts, 2 and 13 of state, which is exactly the 76 that eighty leaves after the inset. An overlay changes no rect and takes no row.

**The key is `?`, and the reference class is unanimous.** `btop`, which §2 makes the reference for what monitor-class feels like, opens its help on `?`; so does `bottom`, which runs the same `ratatui` plus `crossterm` pair §6 names; `rtop` uses `?` or F1, `htop` uses F1, `less` uses `h`. It is also unbound here, so nothing is overloaded. **`h` is refused**: it is a vi *motion* everywhere else and this pane has no horizontal scroll to give it, so the one reader who guesses would guess wrong.

**`Esc` does not close it, and that is a fact about this keymap rather than about the convention.** `bottom` closes its help with `Esc` and can afford to, because its own binding table reads `q`, `Q`, `Ctrl-c` for **Quit** and `Esc` for *"close dialog windows, search, widgets, or exit expanded mode"*, which is dismissal and never departure. This pane binds `Esc` to Quit. Adopting the convention here would teach a reader that `Esc` means *dismiss the thing in front of me*, one keystroke from the meaning it carries on every other frame, which is *end the program*. So dismissal is the toggle and the close control, and the convention is declined on the specific ground that `Esc` is already spent.

**The sheet is not a mode, which is what reconciles it with B4.** No key changes meaning while it is up: `q` still quits, `j` still scrolls the diff behind it, a digit still jumps. Only `?` and the close control dismiss it. B4 refused selection and focus because *"selection implies focus, focus implies a second mode, and modes are reviewer-class"*, and what makes that defect real is keys meaning different things depending on where focus sits. Here that is impossible by construction rather than by discipline, so this is a third thing the pane can be **drawing**, which it already is with the masthead, and not a third thing it can **be**.

**What it costs is stated rather than softened: it covers the content this tool exists to show.** That is the real objection to it and the answer is not that the cost is small, it is that the cost is **held by the reader**: a sheet is asked for and dismissed in the same second, where chrome that takes rows takes them from everybody on every frame. The rows the masthead spends are the comparison, and they are why `m` exists.

**Centred rather than full pane**, so the header, the footer and the edges of the diff stay visible and a reader can see the tool is still alive behind the sheet. A full-pane sheet hides the state a monitor exists for, which is the one thing that must not go dark while someone reads instructions about it.

**It is retained state rather than a one-frame draw, and this is the constraint most likely to be missed.** The pane wakes on filesystem events, so an agent's write redraws the frame underneath the sheet, and a sheet that lived for one frame would be dismissed at random by somebody else's build. The mechanism is one issue old and is followed rather than invented: `m` gave the masthead a private `App` field, a `Chrome` field the drawers read and an `Action` the keymap resolves to. **It snaps** in both directions, which is §5.3's *snap, never ease* and not a preference: an animation needs a clock, and a clock needs the timer I1 forbids.

**It degrades on two axes, and both floors live in the layout rather than in the painter.** Width picks the spelling for the whole table, wide or tight. **This sentence read *"drops the second column of pairs first"* when the ruling was written, and the build corrected it**: the sheet was one column at every width, because sixteen rows already fit a twenty-four row pane and a two-column shape would need a hundred and twelve columns to draw what fifty-six draws, in exchange for reading as two unrelated tables. **That correction was itself corrected on 2026-08-24 by [#220](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/220), and this ruling's original two columns are half right after all.** The reason above is true of a pane at least twenty-four rows tall and reaches no other, the hundred and twelve is arithmetic about a shape that charges both columns the widest fields of either, and *two unrelated tables* describes two columns of pairs rather than the two groups this sheet already labels. §11.1 carries the ruling, its measurements and what is still open. Height drops the mouse group before the keyboard group, then keyboard rows in their own drop order, leaving `f`, `m` and `?` last. **That order became a second constant on 2026-08-24** ([#285](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/285)), because the rung that heads the ladder now needed the table itself reordered for a reader and *from the top down* would then have kept `q` and dropped `f` and `m`. That order is §11.1's own reason applied one surface over: the unguessable outlives the reflexive, which is why `f follow` is the hint bar's last rung standing. The floors belong to the body's split for the reason [#158](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/158) paid for once already, where the band's width floor lived only in the drawer for one commit and a narrow pane reserved four rows for a region the painter then declined to draw. **Below its floor `?` still toggles and nothing is drawn**, which is exactly what `m` does on a pane that cannot carry the band: a key whose effect is sometimes invisible is better than one that is sometimes not a key.

**I1 is untouched, and the frame budget is quoted with its headroom rather than as a ceiling.** A reader pressing `?` is not idle, so I1's *0 wakeups while idle* does not reach this in either direction, and §5.3 prices ink at zero. The frame path sits at 2.4ms p50 and 3.1ms p99 against I9's 16ms, and the sheet **covers** diff rows carrying highlighted spans with about nineteen rows of plain text, so the expectation is that it costs less than what it hides. That was an expectation and #206 was named as owing the measurement; #206 closed without it. **Paid 2026-08-24 by [#220](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/220)**, which made the debt larger rather than smaller: the sheet's widest rung went from 56 columns to 104 and its drawer moved from bulk string writes to per-cell ones. `crates/vigia/tests/budgets.rs::a_frame_under_the_sheet_holds_the_frame_budget` times a frame with the sheet up on the pane the two-column rung arrives on, and it asserts the rung is the one it is named for so it cannot quietly become a second copy of the gate above it. **And `a_frame_under_the_roomy_sheet_holds_the_frame_budget` beside it since [#285](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/285)**, because the widest rung and the largest one stopped being the same rung: 68 by 29 is 1,972 cells against 104 by 14's 1,456, and the roomy rung is the one a full-screen terminal takes where the two-column rung is what a short one falls to.

**The shape, at eighty columns.** Fifty-six columns wide and nineteen rows, centred, with a twenty-two column keys field and a twenty-eight column verb field. One row per **gesture**, aliases spelled inside the keys cell, which is the shape the README's table already has and which answers half of [#80](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/80)'s open question about whether an item may express a relationship rather than a key: here it may, because here there is room.

```
┌─ gestures ──────────────────────────────────────── ✕ ┐
│ j  k  ↓  ↑              scroll a row                 │
│ Space  PgDn  PgUp       page                         │
│ d  u                    half a page                  │
│ g  Home  /  G  End      first / last changed file    │
│ n  /  p                 next / previous changed file │
│ 1  to  6                jump to that row of the list │
│ J  K  Shift+↑  Shift+↓  scroll the pinned file list  │
│ f                       follow the newest change     │
│ m                       show or hide the churn band  │
│ ?                       this sheet                   │
│ q  Esc  Ctrl+C  Ctrl+D  quit                         │
│ mouse ────────────────────────────────────────────── │
│ wheel                   scroll what you point at     │
│ drag a scrollbar        move that region             │
│ click a track           send that region there       │
│ click  ▲ ▼              one row, and repeats held    │
│ click a listed file     jump the diff to it          │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**And at forty columns**, thirty-eight wide and nineteen rows, with a thirteen column keys field and nineteen for the verb. One rung of the ladder is visible here rather than described: the alias cells have dropped from the right, so `q  Esc  Ctrl+C  Ctrl+D` is `q  Esc` while the arrows survive on `j  k`, because they are the alias a reader is likeliest to try. **The mouse group used to be gone here too, and it is not any more**: [#286](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/286) shortened two tight mouse verbs so the whole table fits the width I6 is named for, which took this box from thirty-five wide and thirteen rows to thirty-eight and nineteen and took the pane from eleven gestures to sixteen.

**Both boxes drew `q` on the first row until 2026-08-24 and now draw it at the foot of the keyboard block**, which is [#285](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/285)'s reorder reaching a picture that predates it: they are read off the shipped drawer, and the drawer's order is §11.1's. Nothing else in either moved.

**The widths are the shipped drawer's, not the ruling's estimate.** This block read thirty-eight wide with a fourteen column keys field when B12 was written, which was a mockup drawn to chosen fields; the fields are derived from the content of the rows a rung actually draws. **The mouse group wins both fields at this spelling and neither at the wide one**, on `click a track` at thirteen against eleven and, until #286, `scroll what you point at` at twenty-four against eighteen. That verb is now `what you point at` at seventeen and `one row, repeats held` is `a row, held repeats` at nineteen, which is what puts the whole table inside forty columns. Corrected from the drawn output by #206 and again by #286, which is the direction §5.1 rules for a picture and a screen that disagree.

```
┌─ gestures ────────────────────── ✕ ┐
│ j  k  ↓  ↑     scroll a row        │
│ Space  PgDn    page                │
│ d  u           half a page         │
│ g  /  G        first / last file   │
│ n  /  p        next / prev file    │
│ 1  to  6       jump to a list row  │
│ J  K           scroll the list     │
│ f              follow the newest   │
│ m              the churn band      │
│ ?              this sheet          │
│ q  Esc         quit                │
│ mouse ──────────────────────────── │
│ wheel          what you point at   │
│ drag a bar     move that region    │
│ click a track  send it there       │
│ click  ▲ ▼     a row, held repeats │
│ click a file   jump the diff to it │
└────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**And the same forty columns on a pane of twelve rows, which is where B13 is visible**: three pages, the counter in the title bar, and `?` closing after the last. The mouse group's heading is a line the split can land on like any other, so page two ends with it and page three is the group.

```
┌─ gestures  1-6 of 16 ─────────── ✕ ┐
│ j  k  ↓  ↑     scroll a row        │
│ Space  PgDn    page                │
│ d  u           half a page         │
│ g  /  G        first / last file   │
│ n  /  p        next / prev file    │
│ 1  to  6       jump to a list row  │
└────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─ gestures  7-11 of 16 ────────── ✕ ┐
│ J  K           scroll the list     │
│ f              follow the newest   │
│ m              the churn band      │
│ ?              this sheet          │
│ q  Esc         quit                │
│ mouse ──────────────────────────── │
└────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─ gestures  12-16 of 16 ───────── ✕ ┐
│ wheel          what you point at   │
│ drag a bar     move that region    │
│ click a track  send it there       │
│ click  ▲ ▼     a row, held repeats │
│ click a file   jump the diff to it │
└────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**The close control is the pane's first, and it inherits a vocabulary rather than inventing one**: [#166](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/166) settled that a button is a glyph in the chrome's own weight, with shape rather than colour telling it from what it sits on, and B10 settled that a click is always brighter than a hover. Which glyph is #206's to settle, on the same CP437 argument the step buttons had, since the `✕` drawn above is outside it exactly as `▲` and `▼` are.

**What this does to [#80](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/80) is the second-order effect that made it worth ruling first.** Once the sheet exists, the hint bar's job stops being to carry the keymap and becomes to **point at it**, so #80 is re-scoped from *thirteen gestures do not fit at any width* to *which three items, and where does `? keys` sit in the ladder*. Something of the shape `q quit · f follow · ? keys` is three items where there are four today, so the bar gets **shorter** while the pane advertises more. The rung table stays #80's to write and is not ruled here. **[#147](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/147) is not settled either**, and it is worth saying plainly: whether a bonus rung may cost a status readout is the same question after this ruling as before it, only cheaper to answer in the readouts' favour, because a bar that points rather than lists competes for fewer columns.

**The behaviour moved to §11.1 the same day, and the ruling stayed here**, which is the convention at the top of §11.2 doing what it says. What is left in this entry is the *why*: the structural argument, the `Esc` finding, and the branch not taken. What §11.1 carries is what the shell does, gated by `crates/vigia/tests/sheet.rs`.

**The ruling stood for four hours before it drew anything, and that gap should not have existed.** #167 shipped as a ruling with its build filed as #206 and nothing on screen, which is what the decision-issue rule asked for and is a bad trade at this size: a reader pressed `?`, got nothing, and had to go and find out that the tool had been *told* about a feature it did not have. `.claude/skills/take-next/SKILL.md` now says a ruling of **yes** carries its build in the same pass unless the build is genuinely too large for one, and that the report's first line is what a reader can do that they could not before. Recorded here rather than only in the skill because this is the entry that produced the lesson.

**Declined, and what that would have cost, because the case for the road not taken gets raised again.** The keymap stays in the footer, #80 has to fit thirteen gestures into a bar that cannot grow without buying a second footer line at more widths, and #147 gets worse rather than better, since every item added to the bar is a column taken from the diagnostics group that already yields first. The honest summary of that branch is that the pane goes on advertising four gestures of the sixteen the sheet above lists, while the other twelve live only in the README, which is the state that produced this issue.

**B13 — how a pane too small for the whole table reaches the rest of it: the sheet pages. Ruled 2026-08-24: `?` advances, and the last page closes it. Shipped the same day. See §11.1.** ([#286](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/286).) Before this the height axis **dropped**: the mouse group first, then keyboard rows until three were left, and nothing on screen said it had happened. Measured on `main` at 0.25.0, over every width from 20 to 140 against every height from 3 to 40, a 40 column pane reached **11 of 16** gestures at every height and a pane of 8 rows reached 4. A help sheet that silently omits help is a worse failure than a tight one, because the reader cannot tell it happened.

**`?` keeps exactly one meaning, and that is the whole of why paging wins.** B12's reconciliation with B4 is that no key changes meaning while the sheet is up, and every route to a **scrolling** sheet breaks it: `j`/`k` scroll the diff, `Space`, `d` and `u` page it, and taking one of them back is the mode B12 refused. The one route that takes no key is the wheel, and a mouse-only answer does not reach a keyboard reader, on exactly the narrow pane where a reader is likeliest to be inside `tmux` or over `ssh` with no mouse reporting at all ([#188](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/188) is that case already filed). A scrolling sheet would also need its own bar, which is furniture inside furniture. **Paging costs one key that was already the sheet's**: closed to page one to page two to closed, and on every pane whose sheet is one page, which is every pane the first three rungs serve, it is the toggle it has always been.

**The third candidate is kept rather than declined.** #286 offered *say so only*: a last row naming how many gestures the pane has no room for. On its own it does not meet the gate, because the gestures stay out of reach. As the page counter in the title bar it is the honest half of the answer and it costs no content row, which is the property B12 chose an overlay for: a say-so taking a row would spend it on the gestures it was apologising for.

**What is promised, and where.** At forty columns and up, every gesture the sheet binds is reachable at every height the sheet draws at all. Forty is I6's own width and that is why it is the line: below it the mouse group has no spelling that fits, and the counter's ordinals stop below sixteen so the reader can see it. Getting to forty cost two tight verbs, `scroll what you point at` becoming `what you point at` and `one row, repeats held` becoming `a row, held repeats`, which took the whole table in one column from 43 columns to 38. That narrowed the tight two-column rung from 76 to 71 and moved its arrival from 78 to 73, which is additive in the direction that matters and is a correction to this ruling's own plan, which predicted those numbers unmoved.

**What it costs, stated rather than softened.** A pane under thirty columns now draws no sheet at all, where it drew four gestures of sixteen. The counter rides the title bar, every rung charges its widest spelling so the counter can never run into the close control, and thirty is what that floor comes to. A sheet that cannot say how much of the table it is hiding is the state this ruling exists to end, so declining to draw one is the same answer `m` gives on a pane that cannot carry the band. Nothing at or above I6's forty is affected.

**Two things B13's own audit found, both of them this ruling's defects rather than B12's.** The sheet's close control sent the same action `?` sends, so on a six-page pane it *advanced*: the only escape a pointer has walked the reader forward a page at a time and needed six clicks to let them out, while §11.1 and the code both said it dismissed. It is `Action::CloseSheet` now, which is the shape the two meanings were always going to need once `?` stopped meaning close. And the last page's box moved: its content is a remainder, the frame was sized from it, and the sheet is centred, so the final page shrank and slid down half the difference and took the close control out from under a resting pointer. Every page of a pane is one box now, with the tail blank inside the frame, which is what a paged surface looks like. Neither was reachable before this ruling and neither was visible to the suite that shipped it: the close gate asserted an action's identity on a one-page pane and never applied it, and the box gate read the two edges that did not move.

**B14 — whether the left rail arrives on its own or is asked for: it is asked for, on `r`. Ruled 2026-08-24, and the default is the strip above the diff at every width. Shipped the same day. See §11.1.** ([#295](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/295).) [#252](https://github.com/breferrari/vigia/issues/252) shipped the rail arriving at 134 columns on its own, and what that meant for a reader is one number: at 133 the diff plans against 129 columns and at 134 against 60. Widening a pane past a threshold nobody chose more than halved the thing this tool exists to show. **The derivation of 134 is not what is reversed and is not touched**: both regions read one glance ladder, so splitting a pane costs each half what the whole had, and 134 is the only width at which the stacked list would otherwise have spent its new columns on a wider heat strip. What it decides now is where the gesture can be honoured.

**It is not a mode**, which is what keeps it out of B4's refusal the way B12's sheet is kept out of it: no key changes meaning while the rail is up, and the list is still not navigable, it is merely somewhere else. B6 keeps it a keystroke rather than a flag. Below 134 it changes nothing drawn and eats no gesture, and the request survives a pane too narrow to honour it, so narrowing and widening again returns the rail rather than the question.

**The branch not taken: leave it automatic and add an opt-out.** Rejected because it inverts the cost. The reader who has not thought about it is the one whose diff is narrowed from 129 columns to 60, and the discovery path is the same gestures sheet either way. If the sheet is how a reader learns the gesture whichever direction it points, the default should be the one that changes nothing.

**What it cost, and it is the sheet rather than the pane.** A key is a row on the gestures sheet, so `KEYBOARD` is twelve and the counter counts to seventeen, and every row count §11.1 states moved with it: the one-column rung is eighteen lines, the two-column rung is `104 x 15` and `71 x 15`, the roomy rung is `68 x 30`. **No width moved**, because `r`'s cells sit inside the existing field maxima, so 38, 56, 68, 71, 104 and the thirty-column floor are exactly what B13 left them. `r` is given up at rank eight of `DROP_ORDER`, one before `f`, so the keep-set is still `f`, `m` and `?`: that order only binds at 30 to 34 columns and a rail needs 134, which makes `r` the one gesture in the set that cannot fire on the pane dropping it.
